# INQUIRY CALCULUS — REGENERATIVE QUESTION-PROGRAM NETWORK

**Status:** Derived executable network over the current canonical Inquiry Calculus  
**Created:** 2026-08-25  
**Authority rule:** This document does not add semantic primitives. Every `QP` below is a compiled program/interface built from typed relations, partial binding/open ports, questions, answer-dependent inquiry programs, reciprocal return, support/warrant, protected consequence, folds/reopening, binding extension, and reification.

## 0. Governing compression

A retained inequality is not stored only as:

\[
A\neq B.
\]

It is represented as a regenerable question interface:

\[
\boxed{
\Delta
=
\langle
\mathcal H,\,
W_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{SepQ}_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{RoleQ}_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{ApplyQ}_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{BreakQ}_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{ResidualQ}_\Delta,\,
\mathsf{Unlock}_\Delta
\rangle
}
\]

where the static inequality is merely one compact rendering of a question-generated protected partition.

The current \(M\)-catalogue is treated as provenance and regression ancestry. The active semantic representation is the smaller network below.

---

# 1. The eight compiled question-program generators

## QP1 — ROLE COMPLETION / HOLE FILLING

### Open relation

For a typed relational web \(W\) with open ports \(I\) and bound ports \(\beta\):

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{fill}}(W,\beta,I)
=
?\,\theta_I[
W(\beta\oplus\theta_I)
].
}
\]

### Purpose

Discover domain-local forms that lawfully occupy relational roles.

### Outputs

- `COMPLETE(theta)` — all protected open roles filled.
- `PARTIAL(F)` — residual completion field remains.
- `AMBIGUOUS(F)` — multiple protected-distinct completions remain.
- `EMPTY(cert)` — emptiness positively established under declared coverage.
- `UNKNOWN(reason)` — current resources cannot determine the completion.
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP(delta)` — required role/relation cannot be expressed in the current binding.

### Residual routing

- `PARTIAL` → QP1 on remaining ports or QP2 if remaining completions are protected-distinct.
- `AMBIGUOUS` → QP2.
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP` → QP7.
- `COMPLETE` → QP5 before standing-dependent method execution.

### Regenerates

Constraint completion, witness construction, residual classification, global completion fields, method-local role typing, fixed-point candidates when the binding supplies the closure/stability law.

---

## QP2 — PROTECTED SEPARATOR

### Open relation

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{sep}}(x,y;\mathcal H)
=
?K[
K\in\mathcal H
\land
\Con_K(x)\neq\Con_K(y)
].
}
\]

More generally, for an unresolved completion field \(F\):

\[
?K[
\exists x,y\in F.\,
x\not\equiv_{\mathcal H}y
\land
\mathsf{Ans}_K(x)\neq\mathsf{Ans}_K(y)
].
\]

### Purpose

Turn every useful static inequality into the inquiry that makes the inequality consequential.

### Outputs

- `SEPARATED(K, cells)`.
- `NO_SEPARATOR_CERTIFIED(scope,coverage)`.
- `UNKNOWN`.
- `MATERIALIZATION_GAP`.
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP`.

### Residual routing

- separator found → QP4 to stress necessity/scope; QP5 if the separator depends on evidence/warrant.
- representation gap → QP7.
- no separator under exact declared horizon → QP8 candidate fold.
- unknown → remain open.

### Regenerates

Most ledger inequalities; oracle-free discrepancy; behavioral-vs-semantic distinction; endpoint-vs-derivation; determination-vs-presentation; resource-location-vs-authority; revision-role separations.

---

## QP3 — RECIPROCAL RETURN / RECOVERY

### Open program

Given supported determination \(x\) and an admitted oriented relation use \(N_u\):

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Ask}
(
?y[N_u(x,y)],
\lambda y.\;
\mathsf{Ask}
(
?x'[N_u^{-1}(y,x')],
\lambda x'.\;
\mathsf{Return}_I(x,y,x')
)
).
}
\]

### Purpose

Determine what survives an explicit positive departure and return through the relation that licensed it.

### Outputs

- `RECOVERED([x]_\mathcal H)`.
- `PLURAL_RETURN(F)`.
- `DEPARTURE_UNSUPPORTED`.
- `RETURN_UNKNOWN`.
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP`.

### Residual routing

- plural return → QP2.
- failed recovery → QP2 or QP7.
- successful recovery → QP4/QP8 to test whether the path can be compressed.
- transformative actual probe → QP4 on future inquiry consequences.

### Regenerates

Answer-dependent strategies, reciprocal inquiry, transformative probes, recovery ambiguity, boundary traversal, provenance-sensitive transfer histories.

---

## QP4 — VARIATION / STRESS / COUNTERFACTUAL FAMILY

### Open relation

For current filling \(\theta\), admissible variation relation \(V\), and protected consequence \(P\):

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{vary}}(\theta,V,P)
=
?\theta'[
V(\theta,\theta')
].
}
\]

Continuation asks:

\[
\boxed{
\Con_P(\theta')\stackrel{?}{=}\Con_P(\theta).
}
\]

For strategies/environment contracts:

\[
?\sigma[
\forall e\in E.\;G(\sigma,e)
].
\]

### Purpose

Test necessity, robustness, comparator semantics, quantifier order, context stability, future inquiry value, support withdrawal, and least-restrictive environments.

### Outputs

- `INVARIANT`.
- `CROSSING(separator)`.
- `FRONTIER`.
- `COUNTEREXAMPLE`.
- `UNKNOWN`.

### Residual routing

- crossing/counterexample → QP2.
- stable family → QP8 candidate compression/generalization.
- unexplained failure → QP7.
- strategy/environment completion required → QP1.

### Regenerates

Deviation stability, robust strategy guarantees, context extension, environment-conditioned guarantees, support-failure profiles, future-aware question choice, comparator-relative guarantees, prospective evaluation integrity.

---

## QP5 — GROUND / SUPPORT / WARRANT

### Open relation

For candidate relation \(\lambda\):

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{ground}}(\lambda)
=
?E[
E\in\Supp(\lambda)
\land
\Closed(E,\lambda)
\land
\mathsf{Authorized}(E,\lambda)
].
}
\]

### Purpose

Separate applicability/generation from evidential support and standing.

### Outputs

- `GROUNDED(E,authority)`.
- `OPEN_DEPENDENCY(R)`.
- `AUTHORITY_MISMATCH`.
- `ROOTLESS_CYCLE`.
- `UNKNOWN`.

### Residual routing

- open dependency → QP1/QP2 on missing relation.
- authority mismatch → QP6 if a bridge may transport authority; otherwise QP7.
- rootless cycle → reject standing; seek independent ingress.
- grounded → method applicability may complete.

### Regenerates

Witness authority, certified sets, support independence, prospective/retrospective independent discharge, grounding routes, no-self-warrant, certified mutual closure, revision warrant.

---

## QP6 — BRIDGE / TRANSPORT / COMMUTING PRESERVATION

### Open relation

Given source question/relation \(q\), source filling \(x\), and target binding \(\mathbb B'\):

\[
\boxed{
?b[
\mathsf{Bridge}(b,\mathbb B,\mathbb B')
\land
\mathsf{TypedTransport}(b,q,x)
\land
\Preserve_{\mathcal H}(b)
].
}
\]

Protected commuting form:

\[
\boxed{
b_C\circ\Con_q
\equiv_{\mathcal H}
\Con_{b_Q(q)}\circ b_X.
}
\]

### Purpose

Transport relations/questions without transporting source objects.

### Outputs

- `EXACT_BRIDGE`.
- `ONE_SIDED_BRIDGE`.
- `APPROXIMATE_BRIDGE`.
- `PARTIAL_BRIDGE`.
- `NO_BRIDGE`.
- `UNKNOWN`.

### Residual routing

- target role filling → QP1.
- protected mismatch → QP2.
- target representation absent → QP7.
- exact bridge → QP8 may fold domain-specific realizations under one question program.

### Regenerates

Scale transport, transmission factorization, interpretation-commuting bridges, stratified representation, future reinterpretation, temporal presentation transport, authority succession, higher-order transfer.

---

## QP7 — RESIDUAL REPAIR / EXTENSION / SUCCESSOR

### Open relation

Given residual \(\Delta\):

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{repair}}(\Delta)
=
?r[
\mathsf{Addresses}(r,\Delta)
\land
\Preserve_{\mathcal H}(r)
].
}
\]

The repair role is open. A filling may be:

- a new relation;
- separator;
- representation coordinate;
- probe;
- decoder;
- grain;
- method;
- compiler path;
- binding;
- protected successor presentation.

### Earliest-failure localization

Before extending, ask which relation failed:

\[
\mathsf{Representable},
\mathsf{Fillable},
\mathsf{Generable},
\mathsf{Actualizable},
\mathsf{Checkable},
\mathsf{Warrantable}.
\]

### Outputs

- `REPAIRED(candidate)`.
- `PARTIAL_REPAIR`.
- `WRONG_LAYER`.
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP`.
- `BINDING_GAP`.
- `BLOCKED`.
- `UNKNOWN`.

### Residual routing

- candidate → QP4 breaker stress + QP5 warrant + QP6 source/foreign round trip.
- representation/binding gap → recursively QP7 at higher-order presentation/binding port.
- blocked → explicit blocker.

### Regenerates

Representation invention, discrimination-basis extension, unknown-signature inquiry, concept refinement, retrospective gain, governed rule revision/self-revision, probe invention.

---

## QP8 — FOLD / REGENERATE / REOPEN

### Open relation

For candidate compression \(c:X\to Y\):

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{fold}}(c)
=
?\,\Lambda_c[
\mathsf{ConsequenceSufficient}(c,\mathcal H)
\land
\mathsf{ContinuationDescends}(c,\mathcal A)
\land
\mathsf{Recovery}(c)
\land
\mathsf{UnlockComplete}(c)
].
}
\]

### Purpose

Determine when distinctions, presentations, methods, paths, bases, or recurrent programs may be identified or compiled without losing protected behavior.

### Outputs

- `EXACT_FOLD`.
- `APPROXIMATE_FOLD(delta)`.
- `NOT_FOLDABLE(separator)`.
- `RECOVERY_DEFECT`.
- `UNLOCK_DEFECT`.
- `UNKNOWN`.

### Residual routing

- separator → QP2.
- recovery/unlock defect → QP7.
- exact fold → retain expansion route and ancestry.
- approximate fold → keep direction/error contract explicit.

### Regenerates

Behavior-preserving rewrite, representative selection distinctions, certified irrelevance, derivation-sensitive context, conservative derived forms, future reinterpretability, minimal determining families, presentation quotientability, recovery fields, regenerative contextual equivalence.

---

# 2. Derived method-applicability program

Method applicability is not a ninth primitive question program. It is a standard composition over the eight interfaces.

Let a method contract be:

\[
\mathfrak M
=
(
W_M,\,
\mathcal H_M,\,
\mathsf{Authority}_M,\,
\mathsf{Guarantee}_M,\,
\mathsf{Coverage}_M,\,
\mathsf{Failure}_M,\,
\mathsf{Unlock}_M
).
\]

Compile:

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{ApplyProg}(\mathfrak M,\Sigma)
}
\]

as:

1. `Ask(QP1(W_M), ...)`
2. if protected-distinct completions remain: `Ask(QP2(...), ...)`
3. if required relations are filled: `Ask(QP5(...), ...)`
4. if standing/applicability is complete: execute/invoke the admitted method discharge route;
5. feed success to QP4 and/or QP8;
6. feed failure/residual to QP7;
7. use QP6 only when the method contract is transported across bindings.

Applicability statuses:

\[
\{
\mathsf{Complete},
\mathsf{Partial},
\mathsf{Ambiguous},
\mathsf{Inapplicable},
\mathsf{Unknown},
\mathsf{RepresentationGap}
\}.
\]

`Unknown` never becomes `Inapplicable`.

---

# 3. Residual-induced method graph

The method graph is not a fixed scheduler. Edges exist only when one residual can discharge another method's required web.

Core question-program edges:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
QP1_{\mathrm{ambiguous}} &\to QP2,\\
QP1_{\mathrm{gap}} &\to QP7,\\
QP2_{\mathrm{separated}} &\to QP4,QP5,\\
QP2_{\mathrm{no\ separator\ exact}} &\to QP8,\\
QP3_{\mathrm{plural\ return}} &\to QP2,\\
QP3_{\mathrm{recovery\ defect}} &\to QP7,\\
QP4_{\mathrm{counterexample}} &\to QP2,\\
QP4_{\mathrm{stable}} &\to QP8,\\
QP5_{\mathrm{open\ dependency}} &\to QP1,QP2,\\
QP6_{\mathrm{target\ roles}} &\to QP1,\\
QP6_{\mathrm{mismatch}} &\to QP2,\\
QP6_{\mathrm{language\ gap}} &\to QP7,\\
QP7_{\mathrm{candidate}} &\to QP4,QP5,QP6,\\
QP8_{\mathrm{separator}} &\to QP2,\\
QP8_{\mathrm{unlock}} &\to QP7.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Recurrent chains are candidates for operator compilation only after QP8 establishes regenerative equivalence and preserves all failure exits.

# 5. Major folds licensed by this factorization

## Fold A — all static inequalities into QP2 records

The following are no longer retained primarily as terminal propositions:

- endpoint ≠ derivation;
- behavior ≠ semantic identification;
- quotient ≠ representative ≠ canonicalization;
- local compatibility ≠ global completion;
- semantic extension ≠ probe extension;
- current behavior ≠ support robustness;
- survivability ≠ repairability;
- support topology ≠ recovery topology ≠ authority topology;
- boundary ≠ exclusive partition;
- operational rule ≠ revision rule ≠ revision warrant;
- self-application ≠ self-warrant;
- reflective reach ≠ reflective authority.

Each becomes a `DistinctionRecord` whose primary executable field is a QP2 separator family.

## Fold B — extension taxonomy into QP7 answers

`CarrierExtension`, `RelationExtension`, `QuestionExtension`, `ProbeExtension`, `ApplicabilityExtension`, `CompilerExtension`, and `BindingExtension` are not separate dispatch methods. They are typed answers to the question "which relation failed and what repair fills the residual?"

## Fold C — context family into QP4/QP8

`ContextShield`, `ContextExtensionStable`, `EnvironmentConditionedGuarantee`, `LeastRestrictiveProtectedContext`, and support-failure profiles are different bindings/refinements of stress over an admitted context family, with QP8 used when context may be occluded/folded.

`CertifiedMutualClosure` remains a method contract requiring a binding-specific closure theorem plus QP5 grounding; it is not flattened into generic context variation.

## Fold D — reflective/self-revision family into QP5+QP7

`ReflectiveRepresentation`, `UnknownSignatureInquiry`, `GovernedRuleRevision`, `SelfApplicableRevision`, and `GovernedSelfRevision` reduce to higher-order role filling/repair plus independent grounding. The self-referential carrier does not create a new question species.

## Fold E — bridge family into QP6

Scale transport, cross-domain factorization, coordinate/representation transport, stratified transport, temporal presentation transport, and higher-order transfer are distinct method/domain realizations of the same bridge question only at their declared protected horizon. Their binding-specific theorems remain in method contracts.

Historical method names are provenance, not dispatch keys.

# 8. Executable source-form skeleton

Conceptual source inquiry form:

```text
ApplyProg(M, Sigma) :=
  Ask(
    QP1.Fill(M.required_web, Sigma),
    lambda fill_result:

      case fill_result of

        COMPLETE(theta):
          Ask(
            QP5.Ground(M.required_claims(theta)),
            lambda support_result:

              if support_result is GROUNDED:
                ExecuteOrDerive(M, theta)
                  .then(
                    lambda return:
                      Ask(
                        QP4.StressSuccess(M, theta, return),
                        lambda stress:
                          ContinueFrom(stress)
                      )
                  )
                  .residual(
                    lambda delta:
                      Ask(
                        QP7.Repair(delta),
                        lambda repair:
                          ContinueFrom(repair)
                      )
                  )

              else:
                ContinueFrom(QP5.residual(support_result))
          )

        PARTIAL(field):
          Ask(QP1.Fill(field), continuation)

        AMBIGUOUS(field):
          Ask(QP2.Separate(field), continuation)

        INAPPLICABLE(witness):
          Return_I(witness)

        UNKNOWN(reason):
          Return_I(Unknown(reason))

        REPRESENTATION_GAP(delta):
          Ask(QP7.Repair(delta), continuation)
  )
```

This is not a hidden controller. Every continuation is generated from the supported answer/residual.

---

# 9. First method-to-method adjacency map

These edges are relational, not name-driven:

1. **Any completion ambiguity** → QP2 separator methods.
2. **Any certified representation collapse** → QP7 representation/probe/binding repair methods.
3. **Any support open dependency** → methods whose outputs can fill the missing support relation through QP1.
4. **Any bridge mismatch** → separator/localization methods before stronger transport is attempted.
5. **Any fold breaker** → QP2, then QP7 reopen/refine.
6. **Any successful method with apparently excessive preconditions** → QP4 necessity attack.
7. **Any stable recurrent traversal** → QP8 promotion/fold into an operator while preserving its expansion route.

---

# 10. Global breaker against the whole hypothesis

The proposition under attack is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Every useful retained distinction/method can be regenerated from this
question-program network plus binding-supplied laws.}
}
\]

A genuine breaker must exhibit a protected method behavior for which:

1. its roles cannot be represented as a typed open relation (QP1 fails semantically, not operationally);
2. no protected separator question can regenerate a consequential distinction it relies on (QP2 fails despite adequate representation);
3. its answer-dependent progression cannot be expressed by ordinary inquiry continuation/reciprocal return;
4. failure cannot be represented as a residual relation/question (QP7 fails);
5. transport/folding failure cannot be localized as a bridge or protected-equivalence defect;
6. the failure is not merely missing representation, binding, probe, coverage, authority, or resources.

No existing M001–M066 item currently provides such a breaker.

---

# 11. Current active frontier

The catalogue-to-network transformation is provisionally successful at the first global pass.

The strongest remaining question is not whether more \(M\)-patterns can be assigned to the eight programs. They can.

It is whether any two of the eight question programs themselves can be folded without loss.

Highest-leverage candidate:

\[
\boxed{
QP1\ \text{ROLE COMPLETION}
\quad\text{vs}\quad
QP2\ \text{SEPARATOR}
}
\]

because a separator is itself a completion of an open discriminator relation.

Possible stronger compression:

\[
\boxed{
QP2
=
QP1
\text{ applied to a discriminator-shaped relational hole}
}
\]

But the fold may fail if separator search has protected coverage/negative-result semantics not recoverable from generic completion alone.

### NEXT QUESTION

Does QP2 reduce exactly to QP1 plus a discriminator contract, including all distinctions among:

- separator found;
- exact no-separator certification;
- failure to find;
- materialization gap;
- representation gap;
- coverage and resource bounds?

If yes, the network compresses from eight to seven programs.

If no, identify the exact protected consequence that requires QP2 to remain a separate compiled program.

---

# 12. Reopening conditions

Reopen this network if:

- any M-pattern cannot be regenerated from its declared QP factorization;
- a source-domain method requires an omitted question distinction;
- a foreign binding satisfies the compressed QP but breaks an original protected guarantee;
- residual-to-method adjacency requires hidden domain labels;
- a recurrent compiled traversal loses a failure exit;
- any QP fold loses source, role, method, failure, transport, or reopening regeneration.



---

# 13. Ablation pass 1 — QP2 separator is a refinement of QP1

## Result

`QP2 — ProtectedSeparator` does **not** need to remain an independent generator.

A separator is a completion of the discriminator-shaped relation:

\[
\boxed{
W_{\mathrm{sep}}(K;x,y,\mathcal H)
=
K\in\mathcal H
\land
\Con_K(x)\neq\Con_K(y).
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
QP2
=
QP1[W_{\mathrm{sep}}]
+
\mathsf{SeparatorDischargeContract}.
}
\]

The discharge contract must preserve these distinct outcomes:

- a separator filling is found;
- the separator fiber is certified empty under declared coverage;
- current execution fails to find a filling;
- a lawful separator schema exists but is not materialized;
- the current represented language cannot express a required separator;
- resource/coverage bounds leave the result unknown.

The fold is exact because generic open-relation completion already distinguishes denotational emptiness from executable failure; separator-specific meaning is carried by the relation schema, horizon, coverage, and warrant, not by a second question former.

## Updated structural layering

The network should no longer be read as eight peer semantic generators.

It has:

### Base question program

\[
\boxed{
Q0=\mathsf{OpenRoleCompletion}
}
\]

### Contract refinements / reusable compiled programs

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}}&=Q0+\text{protected discriminator contract},\\
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}&=Q0+\text{variation/quantifier contract},\\
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}&=Q0+\text{support/authority contract},\\
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}&=Q0+\text{transport/commutation contract},\\
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}&=Q0+\text{residual/successor contract},\\
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}&=Q0+\text{protected equivalence/recovery contract}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

### Composed recurrent operators

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Recip}}
=
Q0(N_u)
\bind
\left(
y\mapsto Q0(N_u^{-1}[y])
\right)
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ApplyProg}(M)
=
Q0(W_M)
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}}^{*}
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\bind
\mathsf{NativeDischarge}_M.
}
\]

Thus "same question shape" is separated from "same contract/guarantee."

---

# 14. Ablation pass 2 — applicability cannot fold into grounding

A tempting compression is:

\[
\mathsf{Applicable}(M,\theta)
\stackrel{?}{=}
\mathsf{Grounded}(\theta).
\]

Reject.

`Applicable` says the method contract may be used for the current filling.

`Grounded` says the relations/claims on which standing depends have an admitted support/warrant route.

The two may use the same base `Q0` syntax but have different protected consequences and failure classes.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{SAME OPEN-COMPLETION SHAPE}
\not\Rightarrow
\text{SAME QUESTION-PROGRAM CONTRACT}.
}
\]

`ApplyProg` must continue to call grounding explicitly rather than treating method-local role completion as evidence.

---

# 15. Current next fold candidate

The next highest-leverage proposed fold is:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}}
}
\]

because a successful stress test ultimately exposes or fails to expose a protected separator.

Breaker condition:

If variation-family semantics, quantifier order, information structure, or environment coverage can change a method guarantee even when the same endpoint separator family is available, `Stress` must remain a separate contract refinement.

Canonical candidate breakers already present in ancestry include:

\[
\exists\sigma\forall e
\neq
\forall e\exists\sigma_e,
\]

and:

\[
\text{realized path}
\neq
\text{method strategy}.
\]

### NEXT QUESTION

Can every protected use of `Stress` be regenerated as repeated separator queries, or does structured variation preserve consequential quantifier/strategy information that separator outcomes alone erase?


---

# 16. Ablation pass 3 — Stress does not fold into Separator

Proposed fold:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}}^{*}.
\]

Reject as an exact program fold.

A separator family can record which realized cases are protected-distinct, but structured variation additionally preserves dependency/quantifier information.

Canonical breaker:

\[
\boxed{
\exists\sigma\,\forall e\;G(\sigma,e)
\neq
\forall e\,\exists\sigma_e\;G(\sigma_e,e).
}
\]

The two regimes can expose the same environment-by-environment success/failure distinctions while differing on whether one uniform strategy exists.

A second breaker is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{realized transcript}
\neq
\text{answer-dependent strategy}.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
=
Q0[W_{\mathrm{variation}}]
+
\mathsf{Dependency/QuantifierContract}
}
\]

must remain a distinct contract refinement even though every individual subquestion is an ordinary open-role completion and stress failures usually generate separator questions.

The edge is directional:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}^{\mathrm{counterexample}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}},
\]

not an identification.

## Current next fold candidate

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}
\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
}
\]

because rebinding can be viewed as variation of representation/context.

Breaker condition:

A bridge requires typed correspondence between source and target roles plus a protected commuting consequence law. If two bindings can have matching stress outcomes without any lawful typed role translation, `Bridge` must remain separate.

### NEXT QUESTION

Can all protected bridge semantics be regenerated as stress under binding variation, or does typed cross-binding role correspondence/commutation carry consequential structure that stress alone loses?


---

# 17. Ablation pass 4 — Bridge does not fold into Stress

Proposed fold:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}
\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}[\text{binding variation}].
\]

Reject.

Stress can establish that protected outcomes survive a family of variations. It does not by itself establish:

- typed source-role to target-role correspondence;
- lawful question translation;
- source/target consequence comparison;
- direction of one-sided preservation;
- transportability of old terms, histories, or operator contracts.

Two bindings may agree on every tested outcome while lacking any lawful typed transport map for the open roles.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{MATCHING CONSEQUENCES UNDER TEST}
\neq
\text{TYPED TRANSPORT / INTERPRETATION BRIDGE}.
}
\]

The relationship is:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}
=
Q0[W_{\mathrm{bridge}}]
+
\mathsf{TypedCorrespondence}
+
\mathsf{CommutingConsequenceContract}.
\]

Bridge failure often emits a separator residual:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}^{\mathrm{mismatch}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}},
\]

and an expressibility failure emits:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}^{\mathrm{gap}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}.
\]

But `Bridge` is not identical to either.

# 18. Ablation pass 5 — Repair does not fold into Bridge

Proposed identification:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}
\stackrel{?}{=}
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}.
\]

Reject in both directions.

A repair may be entirely internal to one binding:

- materialize an already representable separator;
- refine a representation;
- add a probe;
- change grain;
- repair a method contract.

No cross-binding role translation is required.

Conversely, a bridge may be sought prospectively to transport an already adequate method/relation into another domain with no incumbent defect.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Repair}
\neq
\mathsf{Transport}.
}
\]

A bridge/binding extension is one possible answer class to a repair question when the localized failure is expressibility or cross-binding mismatch.

## Updated stable program-contract layer

The current smallest useful network representation is:

### One base semantic question constructor/program

\[
\boxed{
Q0=\mathsf{OpenRoleCompletion}
}
\]

### Distinct contract refinements

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}},
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}},
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}},
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}},
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}},
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}
}
\]

where `Q_Sep` is known to be a simple refinement of `Q0`, while the others remain protectedly distinct contracts even though their individual questions use the same open-relation form.

### Recurrent compositions

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Recip}}
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ApplyProg}.
}
\]

The network is therefore not a new semantic kernel. It is a library of typed relation schemas plus answer-dependent continuation contracts over the existing kernel.

## Current next fold candidate

Test whether `Q_Fold` is an independent contract refinement or can be compiled completely as:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
+
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
+
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}
\]

over a candidate quotient/compression relation, while preserving exact-vs-approximate direction, continuation descent, recovery, evidence, and unlock behavior.

### NEXT QUESTION

Does this decomposition regenerate every protected `Q_Fold` success/failure/reopening exit, or does fold admission have a consequential contract not recoverable from Stress + Ground + Repair?


---

# 19. Ablation pass 6 — Fold is not merely Stress + Ground + Repair

Proposed fold:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}
\stackrel{?}{=}
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}+Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}+Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}.
\]

Reject as stated.

An exact fold requires positive witnesses not supplied by stress alone:

1. protected consequence factorization/sufficiency;
2. for each protected continuation, an actual descended continuation;
3. regenerative recovery/reacquisition;
4. residual/provenance/factorization route;
5. unlock/reopening conditions;
6. evidence/authority for the licence.

`Stress` can search for breakers to 1–2 but does not itself construct the descended continuation witnesses.

`Repair` is not equivalent to recovery: a recovery route regenerates an old protected form; a repair changes or extends the current representation after a defect.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Recovery}
\neq
\mathsf{Repair}.
}
\]

The correct compilation is:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}
=
Q0[W_{\mathrm{compression\ licence}}]
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}^{\mathrm{breaker\ search}}
}
\]

with `Q_Repair` invoked only on a failed licence/reopening residual.

`Q_Fold` remains a reusable compiled contract/operator because its success value is a compression licence with exact/approximate direction, recovery, residual, and unlock semantics.

It remains derived, not primitive.

---

# 20. First recurrent traversal compiled — DiscriminatingCompletion

A large cluster of M-patterns is not merely mapped to common question nodes; it is generated by one recurrent answer-dependent traversal.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}(W,\mathcal H)
}
\]

conceptually:

```text
Ask(Q0.Fill(W), lambda result:

  COMPLETE(theta):
    Return_I(theta)

  PARTIAL(field):
    ResolveOpenWeb(field, H)

  AMBIGUOUS(field):
    Ask(Q_Sep(field), lambda sep:

      SEPARATED(K):
        ResolveOpenWeb(Refine(W,K), H)

      EXACT_NO_SEPARATOR:
        Return_I(ProtectedQuotient(field,H))

      UNKNOWN:
        Return_I(Unknown)

      REPRESENTATION_GAP(delta):
        Ask(Q_Repair(delta), lambda repair:
          Ask(Q_Ground(repair), lambda standing:
            if standing:
              ResolveOpenWeb(ApplyRepair(W,repair), H)
            else:
              Return_I(grounding_residual)
          )
        )
    )

  REPRESENTATION_GAP(delta):
    Ask(Q_Repair(delta), ...)

  EMPTY(cert):
    Return_I(Empty(cert))

  UNKNOWN(reason):
    Return_I(Unknown(reason))
)
```

This recurrent operator regenerates substantial parts of:

\[
\boxed{
M002,M013,M025,M031,M032,M033,M034,M036,M037,M038,M043
}
\]

with their binding-specific contracts/provenance retained as refinements.

Key property:

\[
\boxed{
\text{AMBIGUITY}
\to
\text{SEPARATOR}
\to
\text{REPRESENTATION PRESSURE}
\to
\text{GROUNDED REPAIR}
\to
\text{RETRY COMPLETION}.
}
\]

This is the first major method-graph compression from the catalogue.

Its expansion route is retained and every failure exit remains explicit.

## Current next question

Can `ApplyProg(M)` be factored through `ResolveOpenWeb(W_M,H_M)` so that method applicability, ambiguity resolution, representation repair, and grounding become one reusable pre-discharge operator without collapsing `Applicable` into `Supported`?

# 21. Meta-compile pass 2 — Applicability factors through ResolveOpenWeb

The pre-discharge method path is now factored exactly as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PrepareMethod}(M,\Sigma)
=
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}(W_M,\mathcal H_M)
\bind
\mathsf{CheckApplicability}_M
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}(\mathsf{RequiredClaims}_M)
}
\]

with the following protected exits:

- `READY(theta,support)` — method-local relational web is complete, applicability is established, and required standing claims are grounded;
- `PARTIAL(field)` — unresolved method-local roles remain;
- `AMBIGUOUS(field)` — protected-distinct role fillings remain;
- `INAPPLICABLE(witness)` — a positive incompatibility with the method contract is established;
- `UNKNOWN(reason)` — applicability cannot presently be discriminated;
- `REPRESENTATION_GAP(delta)` — a required relation cannot be expressed/observed;
- `WARRANT_GAP(delta)` — relational applicability is established but required evidential support is not.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Applicability}
\neq
\mathsf{Grounding}
}
\]

is preserved while the role-resolution machinery is shared.

---

# 22. Meta-compile pass 3 — Generic occurrence scrutiny

A generic post-invocation program must not take `Success | Failure` as primitive input.

The input is the actual or otherwise admitted method occurrence/return artifact \(u\), linked to method contract \(M\).

First ask the ordinary classification relation:

\[
\boxed{
?c[
\mathsf{ClassifiesUnderContract}_M(u,c)
].
}
\]

The result frame is derived from the method/decoder/checker contract and may contain:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{CandidateDischarge}(\phi),\\
&\mathsf{CheckedResidual}(\Delta),\\
&\mathsf{PartialReturn}(F),\\
&\mathsf{Blocked}(r),\\
&\mathsf{ResourceBounded}(r),\\
&\mathsf{Unknown}(r),\\
&\mathsf{Malformed}(r).
\end{aligned}
\]

Define the reusable compiled operator:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence}(M,\theta,u)
}
\]

conceptually:

```text
Ask(Q0(ClassifyUnderContract_M(u)), lambda class:

  CandidateDischarge(phi):
    Ask(Q_Ground(phi), lambda standing:
      if standing:
        propagate method guarantee
        Ask(success_challenge(M,theta,phi), continuation)
      else:
        Return_I(WarrantGap(phi))
    )

  CheckedResidual(delta):
    Ask(failure_localization(M,theta,delta), lambda localized:
      ContinueFrom(localized)
    )

  PartialReturn(field):
    ResolveOpenWeb(field, H_M)

  Blocked(reason):
    Return_I(Blocked(capability_residual(reason)))

  ResourceBounded(reason):
    Return_I(ResourceBounded(resource_residual(reason)))

  Unknown(reason):
    Return_I(Unknown(reason))

  Malformed(reason):
    Ask(Q_Repair(decoder_or_contract_residual(reason)), continuation)
)
```

### Key no-collapse laws

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MethodOccurrence}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticDischarge}
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OperationalNonreturn}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticNonDischarge}.
}
\]

Backend absence/crash/timeout/malformed return cannot be turned into semantic emptiness or impossibility.

### Success and failure share the operator, not the semantics

Both successful and failed method occurrences pass through `ScrutinizeOccurrence`, but their challenge directions differ.

For a method contract whose essential implication is:

\[
P\Rightarrow Q,
\]

checked failure attacks sufficiency:

\[
\boxed{
?x[P(x)\land\neg Q(x)]
}
\]

while standing success attacks necessity:

\[
\boxed{
?x[\neg P(x)\land Q(x)].
}
\]

So:

\[
\boxed{
\text{one return-scrutiny operator}
\neq
\text{one undifferentiated success/failure residual}.
}
\]

The generic operator preserves the orientation.

---

# 23. Question-program fold — success/failure challenges are two orientations of one implication boundary

The two breaker questions above are generated from one open relational skeleton:

\[
\boxed{
W_{\Rightarrow}(x;P,Q,\omega)
}
\]

where \(\omega\) selects which implication boundary is being attacked.

Define:

\[
\mathsf{Break}_{\mathrm{suff}}(P,Q)
=
?x[P(x)\land\neg Q(x)]
\]

and:

\[
\mathsf{Break}_{\mathrm{nec}}(P,Q)
=
?x[\neg P(x)\land Q(x)].
\]

These are typed renamings/orientations of the same two-pole occupancy relation over \(P,Q\), but they are not interchangeable because their conclusions revise different parts of the method contract.

Therefore the exact compression is:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{ContractAttack}}(P,Q,\omega)
=
Q0[W_{\Rightarrow}(\cdot;P,Q,\omega)].
}
\]

No separate `SuccessQuestion` and `FailureQuestion` species are required.

The orientation is protected data.

### Residual meanings

- sufficiency breaker found → method law/scope/applicability/factorization must be localized/repaired;
- necessity breaker found → a precondition may be unnecessary or the applicability field may be generalizable;
- exact no-breaker under declared coverage → retain the corresponding implication direction only at that declared coverage;
- unknown → do not strengthen the contract.

---

# 24. Residual-to-method adjacency is itself a question

A method graph edge is not established by type matching or by a hard-coded scheduler.

Let \(\Delta_i\) be the residual question web produced by method \(M_i\), and \(W_j\) the required relational web of \(M_j\).

A lawful adjacency exists only if there is a supported bridge/refinement/completion:

\[
\boxed{
?\beta,\theta[
\mathsf{BridgesOrRefines}(\beta,\Delta_i,W_j)
\land
\theta\models\beta(\Delta_i)\subseteq W_j
\land
\mathsf{PreservesRequiredHorizon}(\beta)
].
}
\]

Call this derived relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResidualApplicabilityLink}(M_i,M_j;\Delta_i).
}
\]

It compiles from:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}}
\bind
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}(W_j,\mathcal H_j)
\bind
\mathsf{CheckApplicability}_j.
\]

### Breaker

\[
\boxed{
\text{matching output/input type}
\neq
\text{lawful method adjacency}.
}
\]

Two methods can expose syntactically compatible carriers while differing on:
- scope;
- authority;
- coverage;
- guarantee kind;
- approximation direction;
- comparator semantics;
- information timing;
- binding;
- protected horizon.

These must be discharged by the link question before the edge is active.

### Consequence

The method graph is now **query-generated**.

Edges are not permanent scheduler instructions. They are supported relational links conditional on the current residual and binding.

---

# 25. Full generic method-cycle operator

The current pre- and post-discharge compressions compose into:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RunMethodAgainstHole}(M,H,\Sigma)
}
\]

conceptually:

```text
PrepareMethod(M,Sigma)

  READY(theta,support):
    NativeDischarge_M(theta) as occurrence u
    ScrutinizeOccurrence(M,theta,u) as s

    case s of

      standing_success(phi):
        Ask(Q_ContractAttack(Pre_M,Guarantee_M,NECESSITY), ...)
        Ask(Q_Fold(candidate_method_or_contract_compressions), ...)
        Return_I(success_state)

      semantic_residual(delta):
        Ask(
          ?next[
            ResidualApplicabilityLink(M,next,delta)
          ],
          continuation
        )

      partial(field):
        ResolveOpenWeb(field,H_M)

      operational_status(status):
        Return_I(status)

  nonready(residual):
    Return_I(residual)
```

This operator removes method-name-driven control flow while preserving method-specific contracts and native algorithms.

The method name remains provenance/indexing.

The executable meaning is:

\[
\boxed{
W_M
+
\mathsf{Applicability}_M
+
\mathsf{Authority}_M
+
\mathsf{NativeDischarge}_M
+
\mathsf{Guarantee}_M
+
\mathsf{FailureSchemas}_M
+
\mathsf{Unlock}_M.
}
\]

---

# 26. Active next question

The pre/post method cycle is now compact enough that the strongest remaining redundancy is between:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence}.
}
\]

Both:
1. take a typed relational state;
2. classify a return/completion field;
3. expose ambiguity;
4. generate separators;
5. open representation repair;
6. preserve Unknown/Blocked distinctions;
7. recurse through answer-dependent continuation.

### Proposed fold

Can both be generated from one smaller operator:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveReturnedRelation}(q,u,\mathcal H)
}
\]

where ordinary role completion is the degenerate case in which the “return” is the current supported completion field, and method occurrence scrutiny is the case in which the return must first pass through an explicit resolution path/decoder/checker?

### Breaker condition

If actual occurrence/provenance and decoder-path authority introduce protected structure that cannot be expressed as an ordinary input relation to `ResolveOpenWeb`, the two recurrent operators must remain separate.

### NEXT QUESTION

Can `ResolveOpenWeb` and `ScrutinizeOccurrence` be folded into one return-conditioned relation-resolution operator without collapsing represented possibility, raw actuality, decoding, checking, and warrant?

# 27. Ablation pass 7 — ResolveOpenWeb and occurrence scrutiny share a lower resolver

Proposed fold:

\[
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}
\stackrel{?}{=}
\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence}.
\]

Reject as whole-program identity, but license a lower common operator.

## Common core

Let \(\iota\) be a typed ingress form and:

\[
\rho:
\mathsf{Carrier}(\iota)\leadsto A(q)
\]

an explicit typed resolution path.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveIngress}(q,\iota,\rho,\mathcal H)
}
\]

by asking which answer/completion field is supported through \(\rho\):

\[
\boxed{
?a[
\operatorname{Run}(\rho,\iota)=a
\land
\Comp_q(a)
].
}
\]

The ingress carries its own source/authority/provenance contract.

### Internal represented completion

`ResolveOpenWeb` uses:

\[
\iota=\mathsf{RepresentedField}(W),
\qquad
\rho=\mathsf{Id}/\mathsf{projection}.
\]

### Actual method/probe occurrence

Occurrence scrutiny uses:

\[
\iota=\mathsf{RawEventReturn}(e),
\qquad
\rho=\mathsf{decoder/checker\text{-}facing\ resolution\ path}.
\]

The event ingress additionally requires:
- actual occurrence reference;
- raw return preservation;
- state-before/state-after references where protected;
- backend/binding/compiler provenance;
- no semantic interpretation before the raw occurrence is retained.

## Exact factorization

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}
=
\mathsf{ResolveIngress}[\mathsf{RepresentedIngress}]
+
\mathsf{ambiguity/separator/repair\ recurrence}
}
\]

while:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence}
=
\mathsf{ActualizeOrOperationalStatus}
\bind
\mathsf{PreserveActualIngress}
\bind
\mathsf{ResolveIngress}[\mathsf{ActualIngress}]
\bind
\mathsf{Ground/Challenge}.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ActualityBoundary}
\neq
\mathsf{ResolutionPath}.
}
\]

If no actual return occurs, there is no raw ingress to decode. `Blocked` or `ResourceBounded` is not a degenerate semantic answer.

## Compression result

The two higher-level operators remain distinct compositions, but share a reusable lower relation-resolution operator.

This decreases implementation duplication without erasing the actuality distinction.

---

# 28. Ablation pass 8 — Grounding does not fold into ResolveIngress

Proposed fold:

\[
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}
\mathsf{ResolveIngress}.
\]

Reject.

`ResolveIngress` establishes that a typed answer/completion is supported by an admitted resolution path from an ingress.

It does **not** establish that the semantic proposition carried by that completion enters standing.

Breaker:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SupportedCompletion}(a)
\land
\neg\Stand(a)
}
\]

is lawful whenever:
- a checker/warrant route remains open;
- applicability/scope is unresolved;
- the answer is a model-generated candidate;
- the result depends on an unsupported assumption;
- the support graph is rootless/circular.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Resolution}
\neq
\mathsf{Warrant}.
}
\]

The two compose:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Ingress}
\to
\mathsf{ResolveIngress}
\to
\mathsf{Candidate/Completion}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\to
\mathsf{Standing\ or\ WarrantResidual}.
}
\]

No further fold is licensed.

---

The first catalogue-to-network recursion now has the following shape.

No additional semantic question species have been introduced.

## Shared execution resolver

\mathsf{ResolveIngress}

is a compiled reusable operator over a typed ingress and explicit resolution path.

\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}},
\quad

Q_{\mathrm{Bridge}},
\quad
Q_{\mathrm{Repair}},
\quad
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}
}
\]

with separator inquiry compiled as a discriminator-shaped specialization of \(Q0\).

## Recurrent/composed operators

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&Q_{\mathrm{Recip}},\\
&\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb},\\
&\mathsf{PrepareMethod},\\
&\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{RunMethodAgainstHole}.

}
\]

These are program macros/compiled operators, not semantic primitives.

# 30. Current next question — can the full method cycle be reduced to one generic inquiry-cycle schema?

The remaining apparent duplication is no longer between question kinds.

It is between recurrent operators:

\[
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb},
\mathsf{PrepareMethod},
\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence},
\mathsf{RunMethodAgainstHole}.
\]

Each appears to instantiate:

\[
\boxed{
\text{OPEN}
\to
\text{RESOLVE}
\to
\text{GROUND}
\to
\text{ACT/DISCHARGE if required}
\to
\text{RETURN}
\to
\text{CHALLENGE}
\to
\text{RESIDUAL}
\to
\text{RECENTER}.
}
\]

### Proposed meta-compile

Define one higher-order compiled traversal:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{InquiryCycle}(C,\Sigma)
}
\]

where contract \(C\) supplies:
- current open relation;
- ingress/discharge mode;
- resolution path;
- standing requirements;
- success challenge generator;
- residual generator;
- reopening/fold obligations.

### Breaker condition

If this “cycle contract” merely hides all consequential differences inside an opaque controller object, it is not a real compression.

For the fold to be exact, each field of \(C\) must itself be:
1. a reifiable typed relation/program;
2. recoverable from existing method/question contracts;
3. independently challengeable;
4. sufficient to regenerate every current failure exit.

### NEXT QUESTION

Can the recurrent operators be regenerated as transparent partial bindings of one higher-order `InquiryCycle` program without merely moving the entire old method graph into an opaque contract parameter?

# 31. Meta-compile pass 7 — InquiryCycle collapses to the existing source inquiry language

Proposed new higher-order operator:

\[
\mathsf{InquiryCycle}(C,\Sigma)
\]

where \(C\) supplies open relation, discharge mode, resolution path, standing requirements, challenge generator, residual generator, and reopening obligations.

Ablation result:

If every field of \(C\) remains transparent and answer-dependent, the operator expands to the existing source inquiry form:

\[
\boxed{
K::=
\mathsf{Return}_I(a)
\mid
\mathsf{Ask}(q,\kappa).
}
\]

The apparent cycle:

\[
\text{OPEN}
\to
\text{RESOLVE}
\to
\text{GROUND}
\to
\text{ACT}
\to
\text{RETURN}
\to
\text{CHALLENGE}
\to
\text{RESIDUAL}
\to
\text{RECENTER}
\]

is not a new controller semantic. It is an answer-dependent succession of ordinary `Ask` nodes whose continuation constructs the next open relation from the supported result.

If \(C\) is instead treated as an opaque object containing arbitrary hidden dispatch logic, the proposed compression is invalid: it merely displaces the method graph behind one parameter.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TransparentInquiryCycle}
\equiv
\text{compiled } \mathsf{IProg}
}
\]

and no new `InquiryCycle` operator is retained.

## Consequence

`ResolveOpenWeb`, `PrepareMethod`, `ScrutinizeOccurrence`, and `RunMethodAgainstHole` remain named **compiled macros** only where they deliver strict implementation/reuse gain. Their semantics are their transparent expansion into `Ask/Return`, explicit relation schemas, discharge modes, resolution paths, and residual continuations.

---

# 32. Final layer separation after catalogue compression

The current result is most accurately represented as three layers.

## A. Semantic/program basis already present in Inquiry Calculus

\[
\boxed{
\text{typed relations}
+
\text{partial binding/open ports}
+
\mathsf{Return}_I
+
\mathsf{Ask}(q,\kappa)
}
\]

with actual effect lowering handled by the existing runtime semantics.

## B. Reusable relation-contract schemas

Not new question species:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{SeparatorSchema},\\
&\mathsf{Variation/StressSchema},\\
&\mathsf{GroundingSchema},\\
&\mathsf{BridgeSchema},\\
&\mathsf{RepairSchema},\\
&\mathsf{CompressionLicenceSchema}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Each schema is an ordinary typed open relation with a distinctive protected contract.

## C. Compiled recurrent macros

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&Q_{\mathrm{Recip}},\\
&\mathsf{ResolveIngress},\\
&\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb},\\
&\mathsf{PrepareMethod},\\
&\mathsf{ScrutinizeOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{RunMethodAgainstHole}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Each macro must retain:
- its `IProg` expansion;
- exact failure exits;
- applicability/authority distinctions;
- provenance;
- fold/reopening contract.

Thus the catalogue has not been replaced by a second hidden ontology of "question types." It has been replaced by a **relation-schema graph interpreted by one question-program language**.

---

# 33. Question-program graph normal form

A useful implementation normal form is therefore bipartite.

## Relation-schema side

Nodes are reifiable relational holes/contracts:

\[
W_1,W_2,\ldots
\]

including method requirement webs, separator webs, support webs, bridge webs, repair webs, and fold-licence webs.

## Program side

Nodes are transparent `IProg` macros:

\[
P_1,P_2,\ldots
\]

that open/resolve those webs.

## Edges

Edges are only established typed relations such as:

- `requires`;
- `residual-opens`;
- `fills`;
- `refines`;
- `bridges`;
- `grounds`;
- `breaks`;
- `folds`;
- `reopens`.

No edge is a hidden scheduler command.

The current residual determines which edge is executable.

This gives the desired architecture:

\[
\boxed{
\text{RELATIONAL HOLE}
\to
\text{ASK}
\to
\text{SUPPORTED RETURN}
\to
\text{ANSWER-CONDITIONED RELATIONAL HOLE}.
}
\]

---

# 34. Current global breaker after the first catalogue-to-network conversion

The strongest remaining challenge to the whole hypothesis is now precise.

Find a retained or new native method whose protected reasoning behavior cannot be represented as:

\[
\boxed{
\text{typed open relational web}
+
\text{answer-dependent continuation}
+
\text{explicit discharge/actuality/warrant contract}.
}
\]

A genuine breaker must not merely require:
- a new domain-specific relation;
- a new native backend;
- a different binding;
- dependent typing;
- concurrency;
- higher-order roles;
- a new probe;
- a new checker;
- a new representation;
- another residual schema.

Those can already enter as typed relational fillings/contracts.

It must require a consequential control/composition behavior that cannot be recovered from transparent `Ask(q,\kappa)` composition without hiding a primitive scheduler/meta-controller.

### NEXT QUESTION

Search the current M001–M066 regression index for the strongest candidate whose **composition**, rather than individual questions, might defeat this normal form. Begin with:
1. answer-dependent strategies;
2. true concurrency / independent joint evolution;
3. certified circular composition;
4. transformative probes changing the future inquiry field.

For each candidate, attempt to construct two systems with the same individual question/answer relations but protected-different global composition behavior. If transparent `IProg` composition cannot preserve the distinction, localize the missing relation before proposing new syntax.

# 35. Composition breaker — orchestration bind is not universal native composition

The strongest attack on the normal form uses true concurrency.

Event-structure semantics preserve causal dependency and conflict as relations among event occurrences. Two systems can therefore have similar or identical individual event labels and local response possibilities while differing in causal independence/conflict structure.

Separately, established effect-semantics work distinguishes ordinary monadic/sequential composition from parallel composition: sequential bind does not by itself axiomatize or preserve native parallelism.

This breaks the overstrong reading:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Ask/Return\ answer\text{-}dependent\ bind}
=
\mathsf{all\ native\ method\ composition}.
}
\]

The corrected law is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{InquiryOrchestrationComposition}
\neq
\mathsf{NativeSemanticComposition}.
}
\]

## Derived composition contract

No new question former is required.

Expose the native composition law itself as an open typed relation:

\[
\boxed{
?\mu[
\mathsf{CompositionLaw}_{\mathbb B}(\mu,P_1,P_2,P)
\land
\mathsf{Preserves}_{\mathcal H}(\mu)
].
}
\]

Call the reusable schema:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Compose}}
=
Q0[W_{\mathrm{composition}}]
+
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
+
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}^{\mathrm{composition\ breakers}}.
}
\]

Depending on the binding, \(\mu\) may instantiate:
- ordinary sequential composition;
- a concurrent/parallel product;
- independence-sensitive composition;
- resource-sensitive composition;
- feedback/circular composition with an admitted closure theorem;
- another native operator.

The program language orchestrates the question asking for and invoking \(\mu\); it does not derive \(\mu\)'s native law from `bind`.

## Four candidate composition breakers revisited

### Answer-dependent strategy

No new composition law is forced. Different strategies with the same local question/answer relation remain distinguished by their continuation:

\[
\kappa_1\neq_{\mathcal H}\kappa_2.
\]

Transparent `Ask(q,\kappa)` already carries this difference.

### Transformative probe

No new source combinator is forced. The protected distinction lies in the binding/runtime effect relation:

\[
(z,r,z')
\]

and event provenance. Same raw return with different successor-world effect is preserved by the probe/occurrence contract.

### Certified mutual closure

A cyclic set of local contracts does not derive its own global composition law. The missing relation is the independently grounded closure certificate:

\[
?\mu/c[
\mathsf{MutualClosureLaw}(\mu,c,\{M_i\})
].
\]

This is a binding-supplied composition contract plus grounding.

### True concurrency

This is the strongest breaker.

If causal independence/conflict is protected, serializing all effects and retaining only the sequential trace can lose the native relation.

The required repair is **not** a universal `Parallel` primitive. It is an explicit binding-supplied composition/independence relation, potentially lowered to:
- a native joint/concurrent method;
- a joint probe;
- a structured event-return object preserving partial order/causality;
- another proven compositional backend.

If no such relation/backend is available, the correct status is a representation/binding/composition gap, not a fabricated sequentialization.

## Updated network law

The first catalogue-to-network result is therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\text{one source inquiry language}
+
\text{explicit native composition contracts}
}
\]

rather than:

\[
\text{one source inquiry language derives every form of composition}.
\]

`Q_Compose` is a relation-contract schema, not a semantic primitive or scheduler.

---

# 38. Composition round trip — nontrivial parent schema survives

The too-weak parent:

\[
?\mu[P=\mu(P_1,P_2)]
\]

is rejected. It can hide the whole source method inside an arbitrary \(\mu\) and therefore provides no regenerative leverage.

A stronger common relation survives across sequential, concurrent, feedback, and resource-sensitive composition.

Let:
- \(P_1,P_2\) be component forms/programs/methods;
- \(G_1,G_2\) be their established local guarantees;
- \(I\) be the binding-supplied interface/wiring/dependency relation;
- \(\mu\) be the binding-supplied composition law/operator;
- \(P\) the composed form;
- \(G\) the claimed protected global guarantee;
- \(L\) the admitted local-to-global preservation/closure law.

Define the open schema:

\[
\boxed{
W_{\mathrm{comp}}(
P_1,G_1,
P_2,G_2,
?I,?\mu,?L,
?P,?G)
}
\]

with obligations:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{Compose}_{I,\mu}(P_1,P_2,P),\\
&\mathsf{Standing}(G_1),\quad \mathsf{Standing}(G_2),\\
&L(G_1,G_2,I,G),\\
&\mathsf{ProtectedGlobalConsequence}(P,G).
\end{aligned}
}
\]

The portable relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CompositionalGuaranteeFactorization}.
}
\]

It does **not** state which native composition law is used.

## Source regenerations

### Sequential composition

The binding supplies directed output/input or pre/post wiring and a sequential composition theorem. The global guarantee factors through the two local guarantees in that order.

### True concurrency

The binding supplies causal/independence/conflict/synchronization structure and a native concurrent composition law. The global guarantee must preserve whichever partial-order/independence consequences are protected.

### Certified feedback/circular composition

The binding supplies cyclic interconnection plus an independent closure theorem/certificate. Local guarantees alone are insufficient; \(L\) carries the extra closure condition.

### Resource-sensitive composition

The binding supplies a resource/permission/accounting interface and a composition theorem whose applicability depends on that resource relation.

## Strongest shared invariant

\[
\boxed{
\text{GLOBAL GUARANTEE IS NOT INFERRED FROM COMPONENT EXISTENCE ALONE;}
}
\]

it must factor through:

\[
\boxed{
\text{LOCAL GUARANTEES}
+
\text{EXPLICIT COMPOSITION INTERFACE}
+
\text{ADMITTED LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL LAW}.
}
\]

## Breakers

- same components + different interface topology can yield different global consequence;
- same interface carrier + different causal/resource/feedback law can yield different guarantee;
- local success does not imply global success without the composition theorem;
- a composition operator with no protected factorization theorem is not enough to transport method guarantees.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameComponents}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameComposition}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameGlobalGuarantee}.
}
\]

## Compression result

`CompositionLawSchema` survives as a genuine reusable **parent relation schema** with protected refinements.

The branches are not flattened:

\[
\boxed{
W_{\mathrm{comp}}
\to
\begin{cases}
W_{\mathrm{seq}},\\
W_{\mathrm{concurrent}},\\
W_{\mathrm{feedback}},\\
W_{\mathrm{resource}},\\
\ldots
\end{cases}
}
\]

The parent is useful exactly because it identifies the common method hole:

\[
?I,?\mu,?L[
\text{what interface and local-to-global law would make these
component guarantees compose into the required global guarantee?}
].
\]

A target domain must fill those roles with its own native structure.

## Residual-to-method routing

If composition fails:

- interface/wiring open → `ResolveOpenWeb`;
- composition theorem/closure law unsupported → `Q_Ground`;
- local guarantees insufficient → `Q_ContractAttack` / `Q_Repair`;
- protected causal/resource distinction inexpressible → `Q_Repair` / rebind;
- foreign composition transport requested → `Q_Bridge`;
- repeated composition pattern stable → `Q_Fold` candidate operator promotion.

# 40. Regenerative fiber refinement — deterministic source factorization

The mathematical source can be stripped to sets and functions.

Let:

\[
c:X\to B,
\qquad
d:X\to Z.
\]

Define:

\[
x\sim_c y
\iff
c(x)=c(y),
\]

and representation class/fiber:

\[
F_b^c
=
c^{-1}(b).
\]

The refined representation is:

\[
\boxed{
c_d
=
\langle c,d\rangle
:
X\to B\times Z
}
\]

with forgetting projection:

\[
p_B:B\times Z\to B,
\qquad
p_B\circ c_d=c.
\]

Its induced equivalence is:

\[
\boxed{
\ker(c_d)
=
\ker(c)\cap\ker(d).
}
\]

The decisive productivity test is factorization.

A discriminator adds no resolution to the current representation exactly when:

\[
\boxed{
d=\bar d\circ c
}
\]

for some \(\bar d\) on the image of \(c\), equivalently when \(d\) is constant on every \(c\)-fiber:

\[
c(x)=c(y)
\Longrightarrow
d(x)=d(y).
\]

A productive discriminator exists precisely when:

\[
\boxed{
\exists x,y.\;
c(x)=c(y)
\land
d(x)\neq d(y).
}
\]

No topology, geometry, metric, vector-space structure, bundle structure, or dimension is required.

A section/representative selector is optional stronger structure and is not full regenerative recovery.

---

# 41. Nondeterministic calculus lift

For a calculus question \(q\), use the supported answer profile:

\[
\sigma_q(x)
=
\{a:\;C_q(x,a)\}.
\]

Because hidden differences should reopen only when protectedly consequential, define a protected question signature/profile:

\[
\boxed{
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(x)
}
\]

as the represented profile of \(q\)'s supported answers/consequences after quotienting away answer differences that the declared protected horizon does not inspect.

Then define:

\[
\boxed{
x\sim_{c,q,\mathcal H}y
\iff
c(x)=c(y)
\land
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(x)=\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(y).
}
\]

The question is productive inside the current fold exactly when:

\[
\boxed{
\exists x,y.\;
c(x)=c(y)
\land
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(x)\neq
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(y).
}
\]

In the functional specialization \(\chi_{q,\mathcal H}=\pi_q\), this reduces to ordinary kernel intersection and product refinement.

The old representation is retained by an explicit forgetting/projection relation.

This projection recovers the old **representation coordinate**, not the full expanded ancestry.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProjectionToOldRepresentation}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativeRecoveryOfExpandedHistory}.
}
\]

The latter still requires the existing recovery/reacquisition/provenance contract.

---

# 42. Implicated-class localization

Define the set of representation values split by question \(q\):

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Imp}_{q,\mathcal H}(c)
=
\left\{
b:
\exists x,y.\;
c(x)=c(y)=b
\land
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(x)\neq
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(y)
\right\}.
}
\]

Only classes in \(\operatorname{Imp}_{q,\mathcal H}(c)\) require semantic refinement for this question.

However, mathematical class localization does not imply implementational recovery localization.

Selective reopening additionally requires that the compression licence's provenance/recovery graph can reconstruct the implicated distinction without reopening unrelated ancestry.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticLocalization}
\neq
\mathsf{RecoveryLocalization}.
}
\]

If the first holds and the second does not, return:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProvenanceLocalizationGap}
}
\]

rather than claiming globally minimal reopening.

A safe implementation may reopen a larger recoverable ancestor region, but may not call that result the smallest implicated reopening.

---

# 43. Compiled macro — RefineFoldByQuestion

`RegenerativeFiberRefinement` does not add a new relation schema.

It compiles as the recurrent macro:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RefineFoldByQuestion}(c,q,\Lambda_c)
}
\]

with transparent expansion:

```text
Ask(
  ?b[
    b in Imp_{q,H}(c)
  ],
  lambda implicated:

    if implicated is EXACT_EMPTY:
      Return_I(FoldStillSufficientFor(q))

    if implicated is UNKNOWN:
      Return_I(Unknown)

    for each b in implicated:

      Ask(
        ?r[
          RecoveryRoute(Lambda_c,b,r)
        ],
        lambda recovery:

          if recovery is unavailable:
            Return_I(RecoveryGap(b))

          if recovery cannot localize:
            Return_I(ProvenanceLocalizationGap(b))

          Reopen(c, separator_trigger(q,b))

          Ask(
            ?c'[
              Refines(c',c,q,b)
              and OldCoordinateFactorsThrough(c',c)
            ],
            lambda candidate:

              Ask(
                Ground(candidate),
                lambda standing:

                  if not standing:
                    Return_I(WarrantGap(candidate))

                  Ask(
                    StressPriorProtectedCapabilities(candidate),
                    lambda regression:

                      if regression breaks:
                        Return_I(PreservationFailure(regression))

                      Ask(
                        FoldLicence(candidate),
                        lambda new_licence:
                          Return_I(new_licence)
                      )
                  )
              )
          )
      )
)
```

This macro is composition of existing network structure:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Sep}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}/\Reopen
\to
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}/Q_{\mathrm{Repair}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Stress}}
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Fold}}.
}
\]

It should be retained as a reusable macro only if this recurrent expansion provides implementation/reuse gain.

---

# 44. Local regenerative fixed fold

For admitted protected question family \(\mathcal Q_{\mathcal H}\), define exact local stability:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal Q_{\mathcal H}}(c)
\iff
\forall q\in\mathcal Q_{\mathcal H},\;
c(x)=c(y)
\Rightarrow
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(x)=\chi_{q,\mathcal H}(y).
}
\]

Equivalently, in deterministic factorization form:

\[
\forall q\in\mathcal Q_{\mathcal H},
\quad
\chi_{q,\mathcal H}
=
h_q\circ c.
\]

A regenerative fixed fold additionally requires:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RegenerativeFixedFold}(c)
=
\mathsf{Stable}(c)
\land
\mathsf{ValidCompressionLicence}(c)
\land
\mathsf{Recovery/ReacquisitionValid}(c)
\land
\mathsf{UnlockComplete}(c).
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Fixed/Stable}
\neq
\mathsf{Recoverable}
\neq
\mathsf{Reopenable}.
}
\]

If \(\mathcal Q_{\mathcal H}\) is only an executable/sample frontier without a completeness theorem, the result is working/local stability, not absolute closure.

---

# 45. Foreign-binding correspondence

## LLM/context memory

- expanded field: detailed reasoning ancestry;
- representation: regenerative checkpoint/ledger;
- representation class: distinct histories currently summarized by the same active checkpoint coordinate;
- discriminator: a new inquiry question;
- recovery: ledger/provenance/dependency route;
- refinement: reactivate only answer-changing distinctions and write them into the successor checkpoint;
- projection: successor checkpoint retains enough to regenerate the predecessor checkpoint;
- fold: re-compress after the new distinction becomes protected.

Strong breaker:

A summary may preserve the old active coordinate while lacking the ancestry required to answer a later question. Therefore projection-to-old-checkpoint is not history recovery.

## Program abstraction / CEGAR

- expanded forms: concrete states/behaviors;
- compressed representation: abstract model/state;
- discriminator: validated/spurious counterexample relation;
- refinement: add distinctions needed to eliminate the spurious abstract behavior;
- iteration: re-check/refine.

This is a **partial/approximate** binding rather than an exact quotient in general: abstract interpretation and CEGAR often use conservative over-approximation, so exact fiber equivalence must not be imported.

## Automata contextual equivalence

- expanded forms: strings/states;
- representation classes: continuation-equivalent states;
- discriminator: suffix/context distinguishing future acceptance;
- exact quotient: Myhill–Nerode-style equivalence under the declared continuation family.

This is an exact contextual-equivalence binding when the full continuation family is admitted.

## Sufficient statistics

- expanded forms: full samples;
- representation: statistic \(T(X)\);
- protected consequence: inference about the declared parameter/family;
- sufficiency: target-relevant information factors through the statistic.

This binds the horizon-relative sufficiency relation but generally lacks regenerative ancestry/reopening. It is therefore a useful breaker:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SufficientForCurrentTarget}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativelyRecoverable}.
}
\]

---

# 46. Library correspondence and compression

The fiber-refinement program regenerates/refines the following existing library structure:

- `M017 RepresentativeSelection`: a section/representative is not the quotient or recovery.
- `M024 DerivationSensitiveContext`: projected old coordinate does not erase protected ancestry/path.
- `M025 ResidualDrivenRepresentationExtension`: a productive within-class separator creates a representation refinement residual.
- `M034 DiscriminationBasisExtension`: the new question/profile is an added discriminator.
- `M039 FutureReinterpretability`: later questions can reopen a formerly sufficient representation.
- `M045 InclusionMinimalDeterminingFamily`: several discriminator sets may induce the same protected quotient.
- `M047 PresentationQuotientability`: active representation minimality and protected substitutability remain different.
- `M051 ProtectedRegenerativeContextualEquivalence`: a fold is retained only under its licence and reopens on invalidation.

No M-patterns are merged solely by this correspondence.

The new macro is most naturally a compiled path through existing schemas rather than a new catalogue entry.

---

The strongest unresolved issue is now whether the protected question signature
\(\chi_{q,\mathcal H}\) itself can always be represented compactly enough to support exact
within-class factorization tests when \(q\) is nondeterministic, history-sensitive, or
answer-dependent.

A full answer-profile equality test may be:
- noncomputable;
- resource-bounded;
- representation-dependent;
- stronger than the protected consequence requires.

Can the calculus define a minimal **protected discriminator signature** for a nondeterministic or answer-dependent question that is sufficient for fold refinement without requiring equality of the complete answer profile?

If yes, compile it as a factorization interface over protected continuations.

If no, retain full profile comparison or an explicit working/approximate refinement licence.

# 48. Fixed-point pass — Bridge and Composition share Protected Path Factorization

## Proposed maximal fold

Replace `BridgeSchema` and `CompositionLawSchema` by one parent relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedPathFactorization}
}
\]

over an explicitly typed diagram.

Let \(D\) expose two lawful parallel composite paths:

\[
p,q:A\rightsquigarrow B
\]

or paths made comparable by an explicit comparison bridge. Let \(\preceq_{\mathcal H}\) be the declared protected comparison relation (exact equivalence, one-sided implication, bounded approximation, or another binding-supplied comparison).

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?D,?p,?q[
\mathsf{WellTypedDiagram}(D)
\land
\mathsf{Path}_D(p:A\rightsquigarrow B)
\land
\mathsf{Path}_D(q:A\rightsquigarrow B)
\land
p\preceq_{\mathcal H}q
].
}
\]

The diagram shape and edge roles must remain explicit. An arbitrary opaque `diagram` parameter is rejected as vacuous.

## Bridge refinement

For a cross-binding bridge the parallel paths are:

\[
b_C\circ \Con_q
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\Con_{b_Q(q)}\circ b_X.
\]

The bridge contract adds:
- source/target binding assignment;
- typed role translation;
- direction of transport;
- transportability of terms/histories/operator contracts.

## Composition refinement

For local-to-global composition the parallel paths are:

\[
\text{components/local guarantees}
\longrightarrow
\text{interface/composition}
\longrightarrow
\text{global consequence},
\]

and:

\[
\text{components/local guarantees}
\longrightarrow
L
\longrightarrow
\text{global consequence}.
\]

The composition contract adds:
- component incidence/interface;
- native composition law;
- local-to-global theorem/closure relation.

## Breaker

The fold fails if only endpoint equality is retained.

Two diagrams can have identical source/target forms while differing on:
- cross-binding interpretation;
- causal/resource interface;
- path provenance;
- guarantee direction;
- authority.

The repair is to retain typed path/edge roles and the protected comparison relation, not to restore separate semantic question species.

## Fold result

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BridgeSchema},
\mathsf{CompositionLawSchema}
\subset
\mathsf{ProtectedPathFactorization}
}
\]

as protected refinements.

Category-theoretic commuting diagrams provide a source realization: preservation is expressed by equality of composite paths, while functors preserve identities/composition and hence commuting diagrams. Monoidal composition provides a distinct source in which serial/parallel structure is binding-native rather than a cross-binding interpretation.

No category-theoretic ontology is imported into the calculus.

---

# 49. Fold/Compression is Factorization plus Recovery/Unlock

For exact compression \(c:X\to Y\), protected continuation descent means that every protected consequence/continuation factors through \(c\):

\[
\boxed{
\forall K\in\mathcal H,\;
\Con_K
=
h_K\circ c
}
\]

in the functional specialization, or the corresponding relational descent condition.

Thus the preservation component of `CompressionLicenceSchema` is an instance of `ProtectedPathFactorization`.

However factorization alone is insufficient for regenerative compression.

Construct:

- \(c\) preserves every current protected consequence;
- no ancestry/recovery route is retained.

Then \(c\) is currently sufficient but cannot regenerate a later protected distinction when the licence reopens.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedFactorization}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativeCompressionLicence}.
}
\]

Exact fold compiles as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FoldContract}
=
\mathsf{ProtectedPathFactorization}
+
\mathsf{Recovery/Reacquisition}
+
\mathsf{Residual/Provenance}
+
\mathsf{Unlock/Reopening}.
}
\]

The standalone `FoldSchema` is demoted to a transparent contract refinement rather than an independent schema generator.

# 50. Repair is open residual completion plus preservation/warrant

The named repair schema:

\[
?r[
\mathsf{Addresses}(r,\Delta)
\land
\Preserve_{\mathcal H}(r)
]
\]

is ordinary higher-order role completion.

Its exact executable expansion is:

\[
\boxed{
Q0[\mathsf{Addresses}(\cdot,\Delta)]
\bind
\mathsf{ProtectedPathFactorization}^{\mathrm{preservation}}
\bind
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}.
}
\]

Possible answers may be:
- separator;
- representation;
- probe;
- method;
- compiler path;
- bridge;
- binding;
- successor presentation.

## Breaker against identification with factorization

A representation/binding gap can require generation of a candidate repair before any preservation square exists.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepairSearch}
\neq
\mathsf{PreservationFactorization}.
}
\]

But this does not require a separate schema species. The distinction lives in program order and the residual relation.

## Compression result

`RepairSchema` is retained only as a compiled idiom/macro.

Its semantics regenerate from:
- open relation completion;
- residual relation `Addresses`;
- factorization/preservation;
- grounding;
- answer-dependent continuation.

# 51. Stress is quantified open inquiry, not an independent semantic schema

Variation/stress uses a binding-supplied variation relation \(V\) and explicit quantifier/dependency structure.

Examples:

\[
?y[V(x,y)\land C(y)\neq C(x)]
\]

and:

\[
?\sigma[
\forall e\in E.\;G(\sigma,e)
].
\]

The previously established breaker:

\[
\exists\sigma\forall e
\neq
\forall e\exists\sigma_e
\]

shows that the quantifier/dependency structure is protected.

But that structure is already part of the typed formula/question itself.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{StressSchema}
=
Q0[\mathsf{VariationFormula}]
}
\]

with quantifier/dependency order retained inside the relation.

`Stress` remains a useful compiled rendering, not an independently necessary schema family.

---

# 52. Grounding is an ordinary question over a non-eliminable standing relation

Grounding asks:

\[
?E[
E\in\Supp(\lambda)
\land
\Closed(E,\lambda)
\land
\mathsf{Authorized}(E,\lambda)
].
\]

Its question syntax is ordinary open role completion:

\[
\boxed{
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
=
Q0[W_{\mathrm{support}}].
}
\]

Therefore the **named grounding schema** is eliminable as a question species.

But the underlying standing/warrant distinction is not.

Breaker:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolvedCandidate}
\land
\neg\Stand(\mathsf{Candidate})
}
\]

is already protected throughout the calculus.

So:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GroundQuestionSyntax}
\subseteq Q0
\quad\text{but}\quad
\mathsf{Standing/Warrant}
\not\subseteq
\mathsf{GenericResolution}.
}
\]

The non-eliminable structure is constitutional standing/support policy, not a separate question-program schema.

\mathcal M=(

with a reified relational web:

\boxed{
W_M

whose typed relations include, as applicable:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{Implements}(M,R_m),\\
&\mathsf{Requires}(M,\rho),\\
&\mathsf{ApplicableUnder}(M,a),\\
&\mathsf{Guarantees}(M,g),\\
&\mathsf{CoveredOn}(M,h),\\
&\mathsf{AuthorizedBy}(M,w),\\
&\mathsf{DischargedBy}(M,b),\\
&\mathsf{CheckedBy}(M,c),\\
&\mathsf{Costs}(M,k),\\
&\mathsf{FailsAs}(M,f),\\
&\mathsf{ProvenanceOf}(M,p),\\
&\mathsf{ReopensOn}(M,u).

\]

The fixed product/record layout becomes one implementation encoding of this web.

## Source regeneration

Querying the distinguished relations reconstructs every field of the canonical method contract at the declared horizon.

`PrepareMethod`, `ScrutinizeOccurrence`, and `RunMethodAgainstHole` therefore operate over \(W_M\) rather than requiring the tuple as semantic ontology.

Erase the role identities and retain only graph incidence.

Construct two same-shaped webs in which two same-carrier edges exchange roles:

\mathsf{Authority}
\leftrightarrow
\mathsf{Cost}.
\]

The unlabeled graph remains isomorphic, but:
- one arrangement may license execution/standing;
- the other merely changes a resource preference.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GraphShape}
\neq
\mathsf{MethodContractSemantics}.
}
\]

Typed/named relational roles are protected structure.

The fixed tuple is dispensable; the role relations are not.

## Fold result

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NativeMethodRecord}
\to
\mathsf{ReifiedMethodWeb}
}
\]

is licensed.

The tuple becomes implementation/provenance encoding.

# 56. Method-role subtraction — top-level record roles reduce to existing lifecycle relations

The named tuple fields do not require a new method-specific ontology.

They factor through existing calculus roles:

## Requirement/applicability web

Contains:
- type/interface requirements;
- applicability conditions;
- extension domain/scope;
- method-local prerequisites.

## Native discharge/actuality relation

Contains:
- backend/native operator;
- invocation contract;
- resource availability required to run.

## Return resolution relation

Contains:
- decoder/classifier;
- output relation;
- malformed/partial operational statuses.

## Guarantee/coverage relation

Contains:
- native law;
- consequence guaranteed;
- scope/coverage;
- approximation/quantifier direction where applicable.

## Standing/warrant relation

Contains:
- authority;
- checker;
- support/warrant route.

## Residual/failure relation

Contains:
- semantic residual schemas;
- operational blockers;
- resumption/reopening triggers.

## Provenance/resource qualifiers

Contains:
- source provenance;
- cost/effectivity information;
- ancestry and unlock information.

These are not new method primitives. They are applications of already independent calculus boundaries:
- applicability;
- actuality;
- decoding/checking;
- semantic guarantee;
- support/warrant;
- residual;
- resource/provenance.

## Breakers

### Discharge vs guarantee

A backend can return a value without establishing the claimed law.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Discharged}
\neq
\mathsf{Guaranteed}.
}
\]

### Guarantee vs standing

A theorem/method law may describe what follows under conditions while a particular claim remains unsupported.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Guarantee}
\neq
\mathsf{Standing}.
}
\]

### Operational failure vs semantic residual

Timeout/crash does not establish semantic non-discharge.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OperationalFailure}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticResidual}.
}
\]

### Applicability vs coverage

A method may be applicable to one case while its advertised universal/bounded guarantee has narrower or differently qualified coverage.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ApplicableHere}
\neq
\mathsf{CoverageOfGuarantee}.
}
\]

Thus the lifecycle role boundaries survive subtraction.
