# 58. Hostile fixed-point regression — dependent protocols, linear use, and fairness

**Recursion source:** `Pasted text(10).txt`

The previous local fixed point is reopened only for a hostile regression attack. The target is the claim that protected reasoning behavior remains regenerable as:

\[
\boxed{
\text{typed open relation}
+
\text{answer-dependent continuation}
+
\text{binding-supplied native relation/law}
+
\text{actuality/warrant/recovery contract}.
}
\]

## 58.1 Dependent-session / value-dependent protocol attack

### Hostile behavior

A protocol receives a value \(a\), and the type/shape of the next lawful interaction depends on \(a\).

Schematic native relation:

\[
q:A,
\qquad
a\in A(q),
\qquad
q_{a}:Q(a).
\]

### Breaker attempt

If `Ask(q,kappa)` only selects among pre-existing fixed-type continuations, then a value-dependent protocol would require a stronger control primitive.

### Return through the canonical basis

The current source program already has:

\[
\kappa:\mathsf{SuppAns}(q)\to\mathsf{IProg}(A),
\]

and the continuation may construct a new relation/question from the returned distinction. The reference type grammar admits dependent sums/products:

\[
\Sigma x:A.B(x),
\qquad
\Pi x:A.B(x).
\]

Therefore branch-local future question types may depend on the supported answer, while any externally required common result carrier can be represented by an admitted dependent sum or another binding-supplied type.

### Result

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ValueDependentFutureProtocol}
\subseteq
\mathsf{AnswerDependentIProg}
+
\mathsf{DependentTyping/Binding}.
}
\]

No new inquiry-control primitive is forced.

The domain-native theorem remains binding-supplied: the calculus does not acquire session fidelity, progress, deadlock freedom, or proof-carrying protocol semantics merely because it can represent the dependency.

---

## 58.2 Linear-resource-use attack

Dependent/session-typed systems additionally pressure the structural use discipline.

Construct two continuations with the same received resource \(r\):

\[
K_1(r)
\]

uses \(r\) according to the native linear/session discipline, while

\[
K_2(r)
\]

duplicates, discards, or otherwise reuses \(r\) contrary to that discipline.

The ordinary open-role syntax alone can represent both.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AnswerAvailable}
\neq
\mathsf{AnswerLawfullyReusable}.
}
\]

### Separator question

\[
\boxed{
?K[
\mathsf{ContinuationUses}(K,r)
\land
\mathsf{AdmissibleUse}_{\mathbb B}(r,K)
].
}
\]

The protected relation is the binding-supplied usage/structural discipline.

This may be realized by:
- linear typing;
- ownership/resource accounting;
- capability discipline;
- another native admissibility theorem/checker.

### Result

Generic `IProg` is **expressively sufficient** for the protocol tree but does not, by its bare source syntax, prove native linear safety.

A lawful binding must reject or refuse to warrant continuations violating the native use discipline.

Thus the fixed-point binding clause is sharpened to include:

\[
\boxed{
\text{binding-supplied native typing/use/composition discipline}
}
\]

where consequential.

No constitutional linearity primitive is forced.

---

## 58.3 Fairness/liveness attack

Now construct two executions of the same recurrent/concurrent inquiry program with the same local transition relation:

- runtime \(E_f\) schedules an enabled protected action fairly;
- runtime \(E_u\) can indefinitely starve the same action.

Under a protected eventuality \(L\):

\[
\Con_L(E_f)\neq\Con_L(E_u).
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Program/TransitionRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{ExecutionFairness/Coverage}.
}
\]

### Separator question

\[
\boxed{
?\varphi[
\mathsf{ExecutionPolicy}(\varphi)
\land
\mathsf{LivenessPreserved}_{\mathcal H}(\varphi)
].
}
\]

Fairness is not inferred from branch existence or from the source `Ask/Return` syntax.

When liveness is protected, the method/binding web must explicitly retain:
- scheduler/execution policy or native fairness assumption;
- coverage under which the liveness theorem holds;
- resource horizon;
- actual execution provenance where liveness is empirically claimed.

### Result

The hostile case does not require a new semantic question species.

Fairness is an ordinary binding-supplied execution relation/assumption that conditions the protected guarantee.

The failure mode when no such policy is established is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExecutionCoverageGap}
}
\]

or `Unknown/ResourceBounded`, not semantic refutation.

# 60. Perceptual-aperture crawl — sufficient present as continuation quotient

**Recursion source:** `Pasted text(20260826-032754).txt`

The strongest active residual is the universal reading of the sufficient-present hypothesis.

Let \(\mathfrak H_t\) be the domain of histories available at time \(t\), and let
\(\mathcal C\) be the currently protected continuation/question family.

Define protected future equivalence:

\[
\boxed{
h\equiv_{\mathcal C}h'
\iff
\forall K\in\mathcal C,\;
\Con_K(h)=\Con_K(h').
}
\]

A candidate present representation:

\[
c:\mathfrak H_t\to S
\]

is consequence-sufficient when:

\[
\boxed{
c(h)=c(h')
\Longrightarrow
h\equiv_{\mathcal C}h'.
}
\]

It is exact coarsest when the converse also holds.

This is the history-level specialization of the canonical exact quotient law.

## 60.1 Recursive-update condition

A quotient over history is not yet a state suitable for online succession.

For each admissible next event \(e\), require a descended update:

\[
\boxed{
c(h\cdot e)
=
U(c(h),e)
}
\]

or the corresponding relational update in a nondeterministic binding.

Equivalently, protected history equivalence must be stable under admitted extension:

\[
h\equiv_{\mathcal C}h'
\Longrightarrow
h\cdot e
\equiv_{\mathcal C}
h'\cdot e
\]

at the declared future horizon.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ConsequenceSufficient}
\neq
\mathsf{RecursivelyUpdatable}.
}
\]

This distinction is native in information-state formulations for sequential control: a history statistic must not only preserve target information but support recursive evolution if it is to replace history operationally.

## 60.2 Regenerative condition

Even a recursively sufficient state may discard distinctions needed after future protection/horizon expansion.

Require separately:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Regen}_{\mathcal C}(c(h),h)
}
\]

or an explicit archive/reacquisition contract sufficient for every protected reopening obligation.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Predictive/ConsequenceSufficiency}
\neq
\mathsf{RecursiveUpdatability}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativeRecoverability}.
}
\]

## 60.3 Bounded-size breaker

The abstract quotient by \(\equiv_{\mathcal C}\) always defines a class representation at the set-theoretic level.

But a finite or bounded active representation exists only when the relevant quotient has correspondingly bounded representational complexity.

Automata provide the exact breaker.

For a language \(L\), history prefixes are equivalent when no future suffix distinguishes their acceptance consequence. Myhill–Nerode identifies finite exact-state realizability with finite index of this continuation equivalence.

Hence there are protected query families for which the exact quotient has infinitely many classes.

Computational mechanics provides a stochastic counterpart: past histories are grouped by equality of future predictive distributions into causal states; this representation is prediction-sufficient/minimal, yet established examples can have infinitely many causal states even when generated by a simple finite hidden-state process.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactSufficientPresentExistsAbstractly}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{FiniteOrBoundedPresentExists}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HistoricalLength}
\neq
\mathsf{IrreduciblePresentComplexity}.
}
\]

The second may be much smaller than the first, but it need not be bounded independently of history/horizon.

## 60.4 Derived sufficient-present contract

The strongest surviving object-free relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RegenerativeInformationState}
(c,U,\Lambda_c;\mathcal C)
}
\]

as a derived contract satisfying:

1. protected consequence sufficiency;
2. recursive successor/update descent;
3. standing applicability/coverage;
4. regenerative recovery or admitted reacquisition;
5. unlock/reopening conditions;
6. explicit resource/representation complexity if boundedness is claimed.

This is not a new primitive. It compiles from:
- exact quotient / protected equivalence;
- continuation descent;
- binding-supplied succession;
- regenerative compression licence;
- question-conditioned reopening.

## 60.5 Exact vs approximate present

If the exact quotient is too large, approximation is not automatic.

An approximate present requires a binding-supplied distortion/error contract.

Approximate information-state results in partially observed control provide a foreign realization: approximate sufficient/update properties support approximate dynamic programming with bounded performance loss under stated conditions.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactPresentTooLarge}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{LawfulBoundedApproximatePresent}.
}
\]

The latter requires an explicit approximate compression licence.

---

# 61. External ancestry does not erase the active-state lower bound

Retaining an append-only archive changes the architecture:

\[
\boxed{
\text{active present}
+
\text{external regenerative ancestry}.
}
\]

It does not make the active present semantically sufficient for every possible query.

Database view determinacy supplies a precise foreign relation.

A materialized/view representation \(V\) determines a query \(Q\) when identical view images imply identical answers to \(Q\). This is the database analogue of:

\[
c(h)=c(h')
\Longrightarrow
\Con_Q(h)=\Con_Q(h').
\]

If \(V\) does not determine \(Q\), the query cannot be answered exactly from the active view alone; base data or another retained representation must be consulted/reopened.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PresentDeterminesQuery}
\neq
\mathsf{ArchiveCanRecoverQuery}.
}
\]

And database theory further separates information-theoretic determinacy from existence of a rewriting in a particular query language:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticallyDetermined}
\neq
\mathsf{ExecutablyRewritable}.
}
\]

This matches the calculus's semantic/executable frontier distinction.

## 61.1 Context-management consequence

For conversational/research memory, a compact checkpoint may remain small while total ancestry grows only under a conditional architecture:

\[
\boxed{
\text{checkpoint answers currently protected queries directly}
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\text{new non-factoring queries trigger archive retrieval/reopening}.
}
\]

There is no universal theorem that the active reopened region remains bounded.

Some protected questions can require arbitrarily many irreducible distinctions simultaneously.

Therefore the safe storage law is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{active size tracks current irreducible protected width,
not historical length alone}.
}
\]

The archive allows inactive distinctions to leave the active surface, but it does not remove lower bounds imposed by the current question.

# 62. Network application — research-state minimization

Apply the automata/state-minimization relation recursively to the research process itself.

Two research histories \(h_1,h_2\) are foldable into the same present state exactly when no currently protected future inquiry continuation distinguishes them:

\[
\boxed{
h_1\equiv_{\mathrm{research}}h_2
\iff
\forall K\in\mathcal C_{\mathrm{research}},
\;
\Con_K(h_1)=\Con_K(h_2).
}
\]

`RefineFoldByQuestion` becomes the online state-refinement operator:

1. new question \(K\) distinguishes two folded histories;
2. reopen the implicated ancestry;
3. add the discriminator;
4. preserve recursive update and old-coordinate recovery;
5. refold.

This is a direct method-self-leverage result: automata/predictive-state minimization acts on the research-state representation itself.

No new network node is required.

# 63. Active residual after sufficient-present crawl

The sufficient-present hypothesis is repaired to:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\text{A protected-history quotient always exists abstractly;}\\
\text{a recursively usable present requires successor descent;}\\
\text{a regenerative present additionally requires recovery/reopening;}\\
\text{a bounded/economical present exists only when the protected quotient}\\
\text{has correspondingly bounded representational complexity, or when an}\\
\text{explicit approximate licence permits further compression.}
\end{gathered}
}
\]

The next highest-leverage residual is **frame discovery**.

A sufficient present is frame/binding/horizon relative. If several frames explain the same current occupants/observations but differ on protected interventions or future consequences, selecting one frame prematurely creates a destructive fold.

### NEXT QUESTION

Can candidate-frame generation and discrimination be compiled entirely as ordinary open relation completion + protected separator/stress + grounding + fold/reopen, or does frame selection require a new protected contract?

Use causal Markov-equivalent structures as the hostile domain: the same observational distribution can support multiple causal arrangements that are separated by interventions.


---

# 64. Perceptual-aperture crawl — frame discovery as higher-order open completion

The next residual asks whether frame generation/selection requires a protected contract beyond ordinary open relations.

Let the current evidence/arrangement be \(\Sigma\).

Treat a candidate frame \(F\) as a reified typed relational web containing, where consequential:
- admissible role/type relations;
- relation families;
- composition/interface structure;
- scope/applicability;
- grain/binding;
- protected horizon;
- authority/provenance.

Do not promote `Frame` to a constitutional species.

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?F[
\mathsf{WellTypedPresentation}(F)
\land
\mathsf{Expresses}(F,\Sigma)
\land
\mathsf{CompatibleWithStandingReturns}(F,\Sigma)
].
}
\]

This is higher-order `OpenRoleCompletion`.

Possible result:
- one protected frame class;
- several protected-distinct candidate frames;
- partial frame;
- representation/binding gap;
- unknown.

## 64.1 Frame equivalence is query-relative

For candidate frames \(F_1,F_2\), define current observational equivalence only relative to the admitted observation family \(\mathcal O\):

\[
F_1\equiv_{\mathcal O}F_2
\iff
\forall O\in\mathcal O,\;
\Con_O(F_1)=\Con_O(F_2).
\]

This does not imply protected equivalence under a larger intervention/continuation family.

Causal DAGs give the hostile source.

Distinct DAGs in one Markov equivalence class can encode the same observational conditional-independence structure and therefore cannot be distinguished from those observations alone. Interventions can separate members of the observational equivalence class.

The separator question is:

\[
\boxed{
?I[
F_1\equiv_{\mathcal O}F_2
\land
\Con_I(F_1)\neq\Con_I(F_2)
].
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ObservationalFrameEquivalence}
\neq
\mathsf{Interventional/ProtectedFrameEquivalence}.
}
\]

This is an executable separator, not a static warning.

## 64.2 Native method leverage: intervention design

Causal experiment-design methods do more than supply an example.

Given an observational equivalence class, they choose interventions that maximally or efficiently orient/unmask still-ambiguous causal relations under the source-domain objective.

Transported object-free method hole:

\[
\boxed{
?K[
K\in\mathsf{AdmissibleSeparators}(\mathcal F)
\land
\mathsf{SeparatesLiveFrameClasses}(K)
].
}
\]

If the binding supplies an order/cost objective, ask for a nondominated or optimal separator under that explicit objective.

No universal scalar "information gain" is imported.

This method can be applied recursively to the Inquiry Calculus method network:
- current candidate method/frame webs form the live equivalence class;
- residual questions are possible interventions;
- choose separators whose answers maximally refine the protected class subject to cost/destructiveness/authority constraints.

## 64.3 Cross-binding frame families

Candidate frames need not inhabit one binding.

If \(F_1\) and \(F_2\) use different native vocabularies, their consequences are comparable only through:
- a shared protected observation/consequence interface; or
- an explicit bridge/rebinding relation.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BothFitCurrentData}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{DirectlyComparableFrames}.
}
\]

This is a bridge obligation, not a new frame-selection primitive.

## 64.4 Derived frame-resolution macro

A recurrent implementation idiom is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResolveFrameFamily}(\Sigma,\mathcal H)
}
\]

with transparent expansion:

\[
\mathsf{ResolveOpenWeb}(W_F)
\to
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
\to
\begin{cases}
\mathsf{FoldEquivalentFrames},\\
\mathsf{Separator/StressQuestion},\\
\mathsf{Bridge/Rebind},\\
\mathsf{RepresentationGap}.
\end{cases}
\]

If a separator distinguishes a folded frame class:

\[
\mathsf{RefineFoldByQuestion}
\]

reopens only the implicated frame distinctions, subject to provenance localization.

No new semantic contract survives ablation.

## 64.5 Foreign return into the method network

Two method representations can have:
- identical current I/O observations;
- different applicability assumptions;
- different failure semantics;
- different authority;
- different counterfactual continuation behavior.

They therefore instantiate the same frame-discovery relation:

\[
\boxed{
\text{current observations fit both}
\quad\land\quad
\text{future protected question separates them}.
}
\]

The causal source relation returns to the method network without transporting DAG objects.

# 66. Method self-leverage — separator design as a network method

\land
\exists x,y\in F.\;
\Con_K(x)\neq\Con_K(y)
].

If multiple \(K\)'s are available, retain a binding-supplied nondominated frontier over consequential criteria such as:
- protected discrimination;

This composes:
- `ResidualClassify` to characterize the field;

- grounding to exclude unsupported separators;
- fold/refinement after the return.

Hence the network can use a domain-native active-learning/experiment-design method to improve the process selecting its own next question.

The historical source method name remains provenance.

# 67. Domain-selection ablation — NEXT DOMAIN is residual-to-method search plus binding provenance

The active residual asks whether domain selection requires an external research scheduler.

Represent a candidate domain-method route by:

\[
\boxed{
W_{\mathrm{route}}(D,M,\beta;\Delta)
}
\]

with relations:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{NativeTo}(M,D),\\
&\mathsf{Addresses}(M,\beta(\Delta)),\\
&\mathsf{BridgeOrBinding}(\beta,\Delta,W_M),\\
&\mathsf{Preserves}_{\mathcal H}(\beta),\\
&\mathsf{Applicable}(M,\beta(\Delta)),\\
&\mathsf{GroundedRoute}(M,\beta).
\end{aligned}
\]

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?D,M,\beta[
W_{\mathrm{route}}(D,M,\beta;\Delta)
].
}
\]

The domain name contributes provenance/native-law access; it is not itself the applicability criterion.

## Breaker against ontology-driven domain selection

Construct:
- \(D_1\): linguistically/topic-wise close to the current problem but with no lawful bridge from its native method contract;
- \(D_2\): ontologically distant but with a supported bridge whose native method directly discharges the residual.

Choosing by domain similarity selects \(D_1\), while relational applicability selects \(D_2\).

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DomainSimilarity}
\neq
\mathsf{DiscriminatoryMethodLeverage}.
}
\]

## Multiple lawful domains

Return a nondominated frontier under binding-supplied criteria such as:

\boxed{
(

\mathsf{Cost},
\mathsf{Reversibility},
\mathsf{Authority},
\mathsf{Latency},
\mathsf{FutureInquiryEffect}
).
}
\]

Thus:

\boxed{
\mathsf{NEXT\ DOMAIN}
=
\text{a projection of the residual-to-method applicability frontier}.
}
\]

No separate domain-selection semantic primitive or scheduler is required.

# 68. Global hypothesis correction — domain expansion does not monotonically imply network contraction

The research objective prefers:

\[
\text{more domain coverage}
\quad\text{with}\quad
\text{fewer independently necessary generators}.
\]

This is a compression objective, not a monotone theorem.

A new domain may exhibit a protected behavior not regenerated by the current network. If the breaker survives source/foreign return and cannot be represented by the incumbent basis, the irreducible network must expand.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DomainCoverageGrowth}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{NetworkContraction}.
}
\]

The correct invariant is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Network}_t
\text{ is inclusion/resource-minimal subject to regenerating the
currently protected corpus.}
}
\]

Network size may:
- contract when new domains reveal shared generators;
- remain stable when new domains are new bindings of old relations;
- expand when a genuinely new protected relation is forced.

This preserves epistemic integrity while retaining contraction as a search pressure rather than a guaranteed trajectory.

## Consequence for the two frontiers

The alternating loop becomes:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\text{choose residual-guided hostile domain}\\
&\to\text{derive/native-method pressure}\\
&\to\text{attempt network fold}\\
&\to\begin{cases}
\text{contract},\\
\text{stable},\\
\text{expand if forced}
\end{cases}
\end{aligned}
}
\]

No direction is precommitted.

### NEXT QUESTION

Search a domain with native lower bounds on recoverability/local decoding—e.g. coding theory / locally decodable codes / data structures—and ask whether exact protected reopening can always be localized without retaining proportionally large provenance, or whether there is an unavoidable tradeoff among:
- active compression;
- archive size;
- recovery bandwidth/work;
- localization precision;
- protected query family.

If a lower bound exists, compile it into the sufficient-present/reopening contract rather than adding a new primitive.

# 70. Recovery-locality crawl — coding/data-structure lower bounds

The active residual asks whether exact question-conditioned reopening can always be both highly localized and economical when the archive/provenance representation is compressed.

Let:
- \(H\) be protected ancestry;
- \(E(H)\) an archived encoding/storage representation;
- \(q\) a reopening question;
- \(d_q(H)\) the exact protected distinction required;
- \(P_q\) the archive cells/fragments/probes consulted by the decoder.

A local-recovery contract is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Decode}_q(E(H)|_{P_q})
=
d_q(H).
}
\]

Add resource profile:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal R_q
=
(
|E(H)|,\,
|P_q|,\,
\mathsf{decodeWork},\,
\mathsf{robustness},\,
\mathsf{localizationPrecision}
).
}
\]

## 70.1 Lower-bound breaker

Locally decodable codes provide a native source in which arbitrary message coordinates can be recovered from only a few codeword probes even under corruption.

But very low query complexity imposes strong storage redundancy: classical 2-query LDCs require exponential codeword length in the message length under the standard constant-error setting.

Static data-structure lower bounds independently exhibit space/query-time tradeoffs in the cell-probe model.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactRecoverable}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{CheaplyLocallyRecoverable}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SmallRecoveryProbeSet}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{HighlyCompressedArchive}.
}
\]

The source-domain noise/error-correction assumptions are not transported universally. What survives is the existence of binding-specific lower bounds/tradeoffs among storage, recovery work, robustness, and query locality.

## 70.2 Semantic localization vs operational recovery frontier

The semantic question may establish a smallest implicated distinction/class \(S_q\).

That does not imply the cheapest operational recovery route is the smallest stored region.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecoveryFrontier}_{\mathcal R}(q)
=
ND_{\mathcal R}
\{
r:
\mathsf{Recovers}(r,q,H)
\}.
}
\]

A larger stored block may be cheaper to fetch than a finely scattered minimal provenance set.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SmallestSemanticResidue}
\neq
\mathsf{CheapestRecoveryRoute}.
}
\]

This refines the earlier:

\[
\mathsf{SemanticLocalization}
\neq
\mathsf{RecoveryLocalization}.
\]

No new primitive is required; this is the existing recovery profile plus a binding-supplied resource order/frontier.

## 70.3 Compression-licence consequence

A claim such as "selective reopening is cheap" is now licensed only when the fold/recovery contract includes adequate evidence about:

- archive representation size;
- permitted recovery probes/work;
- robustness/fault model if relevant;
- localization granularity;
- resource tradeoff/coverage.

Otherwise exact semantic reopenability may be retained without an efficiency claim.

---

# 71. Active-aperture crawl — online paging/cache policy

The recovery tradeoff generates the next method hole:

Given bounded active capacity \(k\), which currently inactive/active distinctions should be kept in the fast reasoning surface when future questions are unknown?

Paging supplies the native source.

Mapping:

- fast cache ↔ active reasoning window;
- slow memory ↔ regenerative ancestry/archive;
- page request ↔ question requiring a folded distinction;
- cache miss ↔ reopening/retrieval;
- eviction ↔ folding an active distinction out of the fast surface.

## 71.1 Strong breaker

The maximal claim:

\[
\boxed{
\text{a current-state-only policy can always keep the optimal distinctions active}
}
\]

fails.

For paging with cache size \(k\), deterministic online algorithms have worst-case competitive ratio at least \(k\), while LRU/FIFO attain that order; the offline optimum has future knowledge unavailable to the online policy.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticallySufficientPresent}
\neq
\mathsf{FutureRecoveryCostOptimalAperture}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CurrentRelevance}
\neq
\mathsf{FutureRequestSequence}.
}
\]

## 71.2 Derived aperture-management method

Define a binding/resource-policy level method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ManageActiveAperture}(A,H,k,\mathcal R)
}
\]

with open questions:

\[
?x[
\mathsf{KeepActive}(x)
]
\]

\[
?y[
\mathsf{Fold}(y)
]
\]

subject to:
- semantic fold licences;
- active capacity;
- recovery cost;
- current/live question dependencies;
- binding-supplied future-request model or competitive objective.

This is **not** part of semantic truth/warrant.

It is a resource/control policy over already-licensed active/folded representations.

If no request distribution/future model is warranted, retain worst-case/competitive or nondominated policies rather than claiming optimality.

## 71.3 Context-management consequence

The research architecture can guarantee:

1. active items are semantically sufficient for the current active inquiry;
2. anything evicted/folded remains recoverable under its licence;
3. query-conditioned reopening is correct when recovery succeeds.

It cannot universally guarantee:

\[
\boxed{
\text{minimal total future retrieval cost}
}
\]

without assumptions about the future question sequence.

A bounded active surface can therefore coexist with unbounded history, but an adversarial/new question sequence can force repeated reopening ("context thrashing").

This is a resource phenomenon, not a failure of regenerative semantics.

## 71.4 Relation to existing network

`ManageActiveAperture` compiles from:
- fold/reopening licences;
- `FutureAwareInquiryChoice`;
- binding-supplied cost/resource frontier;
- `RefineFoldByQuestion`;
- recovery profiles.

It is retained only as a resource-management macro.

No semantic primitive is added.

---

# 72. Perceptual-aperture synthesis

The sufficient-present architecture now has four separable layers:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
1.\;&\mathsf{SemanticSufficiency}:
&&\text{does the present preserve current protected consequences?}\\
2.\;&\mathsf{StateRecursion}:
&&\text{can it update from present + new event without replaying full history?}\\
3.\;&\mathsf{RegenerativeAncestry}:
&&\text{can invalidated folds recover/reacquire required distinctions?}\\
4.\;&\mathsf{ResourceAperturePolicy}:
&&\text{which recoverable distinctions stay active to reduce future cost?}
\end{aligned}
}
\]

None may be substituted for another.

The research context manager should therefore treat:

\[
\boxed{
\Gamma_t
=
(\text{semantic present},\text{update law},\text{archive/recovery},\text{aperture policy})
}
\]

as an implementation decomposition, with all four realized through existing calculus relations and binding-supplied resource laws.

## Active residual

The next strongest breaker targets the **object/arrangement recursion** in the perceptual-aperture prompt:

Can an object always be unfolded into an internally sufficient arrangement and later refolded without changing the external relation, or do some domains exhibit emergent/contextual properties for which no protectedly compositional internal factorization exists at the selected grain?

The network should choose a domain where compositionality failure is native—candidate domains include contextual semantics, non-separable quantum systems, or biological multiscale organization—without importing their ontology.

This residual attacks H4 and the assumption that grain change can always be represented by a lawful object↔arrangement bridge.

# 73. Object↔arrangement hostile crawl — joint relational structure

The active residual attacks grain-relative object/arrangement recursion.

Let a coarse object \(o\) at grain \(g\) be unfolded at finer grain \(g'\) into an arrangement:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Unfold}_{g\to g'}(o,A).
}
\]

A naive unfolding that preserves only component occupants:

\[
A=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)
\]

is too weak.

## 73.1 Quantum joint-state breaker

Bell-state systems give a precise source-domain breaker.

All four Bell states have the same reduced state on each individual qubit (the completely mixed state), while the joint two-qubit states are distinct.

Therefore componentwise local descriptions can agree while protected joint measurements distinguish the global arrangement.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameComponentStates}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameJointArrangement}.
}
\]

The source-specific Hilbert-space/tensor-product machinery is not transported as universal ontology.

The surviving relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{an unfolded arrangement must retain every joint relation required
to regenerate protected external consequences}.
}
\]

## 73.2 Object-arrangement bridge contract

A lawful grain bridge requires:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ObjectArrangementBridge}(o,A;\mathcal H)
}
\]

such that protected external continuations factor through the arrangement:

\[
\forall K\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{ext}},
\quad
\Con_K(o)
\equiv
\Con_{\bar K}(A)
\]

for an admitted translated continuation \(\bar K\).

Refolding requires a licensed map/relation:

\[
\mathsf{Fold}_{g'\to g}(A,o')
\]

with \(o'\equiv_{\mathcal H}o\), plus ordinary recovery/reopening obligations.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ListOfParts}
\neq
\mathsf{RelationalArrangement}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Unfoldable}
\neq
\mathsf{ExactlyRefoldable}
}
\]

without a protected factorization theorem.

## 73.3 Compression result

Object↔arrangement recursion introduces no new primitive.

It compiles from:
- reification/grain change;
- open role completion for internal occupants;
- binding-supplied native composition/joint relation;
- protected path factorization;
- fold/reopen;
- `RefineFoldByQuestion`.

If an internal distinction changes an external protected consequence, the coarse object fold unlocks and refines.

---

# 74. State-as-folded-history crawl — hysteresis

The object/arrangement result generates the next question:

When the same currently visible arrangement can arise from different histories, what must a present state retain?

Hysteresis supplies the native source.

A hysteretic system can produce different outputs at the same current external input depending on prior history.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameCurrentInput/Observable}
\neq
\mathsf{SameDynamicalState}.
}
\]

The separator is a future or current response question:

\[
\boxed{
?K[
\mathsf{SameVisiblePresent}(h_1,h_2)
\land
\Con_K(h_1)\neq\Con_K(h_2)
].
}
\]

If such \(K\) exists, the current representation is not a sufficient present.

## 74.1 Important reverse breaker

Path dependence does **not** imply that the full raw history must remain active.

A state-machine representation can summarize whatever history is consequential for future behavior.

A thermostat with hysteresis, for example, can be represented by current temperature plus the on/off mode rather than the entire temperature trajectory.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PathDependence}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{FullHistoryRequired}.
}
\]

The correct question is whether there exists a recursively updateable history quotient sufficient for the protected future.

This is exactly the `RegenerativeInformationState` contract from the sufficient-present crawl.

## 74.2 Return to the sufficient-present theorem

The hysteresis crawl closes the loop:

\[
\boxed{
\text{history dependence}
\to
\text{separator on current representation}
\to
\text{state refinement}
\to
\text{recursive update test}
\to
\text{regenerative fold}.
}
\]

If the required state quotient has unbounded complexity, the earlier Myhill–Nerode/predictive-state breaker applies.

Thus no new path-dependence primitive is required.

# 76. Hostile domain crawl — asynchronous consensus and realizability

The perceptual-aperture crawl is reopened through a global breaker of the current normal form.

## 76.1 Frame

Source frame:
- distributed processes;
- local states and messages/shared-memory actions;
- asynchronous scheduler/environment;
- possible crash failure;
- consensus validity/agreement/termination;
- execution fairness/coverage.

Represent:
\[
\mathcal E=(S,\mathsf{Step},\mathsf{Sched},\mathsf{Fault},\mathsf{Fair})
\]
and a candidate protocol/method \(P\).

The protected task relation is:
\[
\mathsf{ConsensusSpec}(P,\mathcal E).
\]

## 76.2 Strong contrast

Assume the strongest overgeneralization:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentableProtocol}
+
\mathsf{LocallyAdmissibleSteps}
+
\mathsf{FairExecution}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{GuaranteedConsensus}.
}
\]

FLP breaks it: deterministic consensus cannot guarantee termination in a fully asynchronous message-passing system with even one crash failure.

The proof uses a bivalent/undecided region and constructs an admissible fair execution that remains bivalent forever.

## 76.3 Object-free relation — Fair adversarial trap

Let \(R\subseteq S\) be the unresolved region for a protected goal \(G\).

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FairAdversarialTrap}(R,\mathcal E,G)
}
\]

when:
1. \(R\) contains an admissible initial state;
2. \(R\cap G=\varnothing\);
3. the environment has a strategy that, while satisfying the declared fairness/fault constraints, repeatedly selects admissible continuations that remain in \(R\).

Schematic greatest-stability condition:

\[
\boxed{
R
\subseteq
\mathsf{Pre}^{\exists \mathrm{env}}_{\mathcal E}(R)
}
\]

with the fairness obligations carried explicitly by the binding.

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FairAdversarialTrap}(R,\mathcal E,G)
\Rightarrow
\neg\mathsf{UniformGuarantee}_{\mathcal E}(G).
}
\]

This is a protected refinement/composition of:
- `GreatestStableRegion`;
- `EnvironmentConditionedGuarantee`;
- execution fairness/coverage.

No new primitive is forced.

## 76.4 New protected distinctions

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentableMethod}
\neq
\mathsf{RealizableGlobalGuarantee}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{StaticIndistinguishability}
\neq
\mathsf{DynamicallyMaintainableIndeterminacy}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FairnessConstraintSatisfied}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{GoalEventuallyReached}.
}
\]

The last is important: fairness excludes some pathological schedules but does not by itself eliminate every fair trap.

# 77. Method self-leverage — weakest enabling environment extension

FLP returns a typed residual:

\[
\Delta_{\mathrm{cons}}
=
\mathsf{FairAdversarialTrap}
\]

under the asynchronous crash-failure environment.

Do not ask for a named algorithm first.

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?E'[
\mathsf{ExtendsOrRefines}(E,E')
\land
\mathsf{ConsensusSolvable}(E')
].
}
\]

Start with a maximally sufficient extension (for example, strong synchrony/accurate failure information) and subtract.

## 77.1 Source native method

Failure-detector theory supplies an explicit information-extension ordering.

Under the Chandra-Hadzilacos-Toueg model with a majority of correct processes, the failure detector class \(\Diamond W\) is established as sufficient for consensus and weakest under their reducibility/information comparison: any failure detector solving consensus can implement/provide at least that much relevant failure information.

This instantiates:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MinimalEnablingExtension}_{\preceq}
(E,\Delta,G).
}
\]

Open form:

\[
?e^\star[
\mathsf{Enables}(E+e^\star,G)
\land
\forall e\,
(\mathsf{Enables}(E+e,G)\Rightarrow e^\star\preceq e)
].
\]

If the preorder lacks a least element, return a nondominated minimal frontier rather than inventing uniqueness.

## 77.2 Relation to current network

This is not a new semantic generator.

It composes:
- residual classification/localization;
- representation/environment extension;
- `LeastRestrictiveProtectedContext` / frontier selection;
- bridge/implementation reducibility;
- grounding of the sufficiency and minimality theorem.

The important method-generation law is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{IMPOSSIBILITY RESIDUAL}
\to
\text{OPEN ENABLING-EXTENSION QUESTION}
\to
\text{MINIMAL SUFFICIENT EXTENSION}.
}
\]

This is the same maximal-contrast-then-subtract strategy applied to environment assumptions.

## 77.3 Strong breaker

"Add more information" is not enough.

The extension must specifically break the fair trap / indistinguishability relation relevant to the task.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MoreInformation}
\neq
\mathsf{TaskEnablingInformation}.
}
\]

# 78. Resource/method capability order — consensus hierarchy

The enabling-extension result raises a network-level question:

Can methods/resources be ordered by which protected task holes they can implement/solve?

Herlihy's wait-free consensus hierarchy supplies a native source.

For resource/object types \(X,Y\), define a protected implementation relation:

\[
\boxed{
Y\succeq_{\mathcal H,\mathcal E} X
\iff
\mathsf{Implements}_{\mathcal H,\mathcal E}(Y,X).
}
\]

If \(X\) solves task \(T\), and \(Y\) implements \(X\) under the same protected environment/horizon, then composition yields:

\[
\boxed{
Y\succeq X
\land
\mathsf{Solves}(X,T)
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{Solves}(Y,T).
}
\]

Herlihy's consensus number is one binding-specific scalar projection of this capability relation: it records the largest process count for which an object type can solve wait-free consensus.

## 78.1 Object-free network relation

For compiled methods/resources:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CapabilityPreorder}(M_i,M_j;\mathcal H,\mathcal E)
}
\]

is induced by supported implementation/bridge paths, not by method names.

This can compress the method network:
- mutually implementing methods at the protected horizon form an equivalence class;
- strict capability separation is witnessed by a task/problem solved by one but not the other;
- scalar rankings are optional binding projections.

Separator:

\[
\boxed{
?T[
\mathsf{Solves}(M_j,T)
\land
\neg\mathsf{Solves}(M_i,T)
].
}
\]

## 78.2 Breakers

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CapabilityOrder}
\neq
\mathsf{CostOrder}.
}
\]

A stronger resource may be slower/more expensive.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Implements}
\neq
\mathsf{NativeIdentity}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TaskSolvability}
\text{ is environment/horizon relative}.
}
\]

Changing failure model, process count, resource assumptions, or liveness conditions can change the order.

## 78.3 Compression result

`CapabilityPreorder` is a derived view over the existing bridge/composition/method-contract graph.

It need not become a semantic primitive.

It provides a high-leverage method-self-leverage operation:
- quotient methods by mutual implementability at the protected horizon;
- use separating tasks to preserve strict differences;
- choose minimal sufficient capability for a residual when the binding supplies an order/frontier.

# 80. Viability/avoidance compression — quantified successor closure

The active residual asks whether:
- fair adversarial traps;
- controlled invariant/viable regions;
- ordinary invariant regions

require separate relation generators.

## 80.1 Common parent

Let:
- \(K\subseteq S\) be a candidate region;
- \(x\in K\);
- \(a_1,\ldots,a_n\) be binding-supplied control/environment/nondeterministic choices;
- \(T(x,a_1,\ldots,a_n,x')\) be the successor relation;
- \(\pi=Q_1a_1\cdots Q_na_n\) be the protected quantifier/dependency prefix.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{QSuccClosed}_{\pi}(K,T)
}
\]

when:

\[
\boxed{
\forall x\in K,\;
Q_1a_1\cdots Q_na_n\;
\exists x'\,
[
T(x,a_1,\ldots,a_n,x')
\land
x'\in K
].
}
\]

Exact details of enabledness/totality remain binding-specific.

The portable relation is not "invariance" by itself. It is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{region}
+
\text{typed successor relation}
+
\text{protected quantifier/dependency orientation}.
}
\]

## 80.2 Native refinements

### Ordinary invariance

All admissible evolutions remain in \(K\).

Schematic orientation:

\[
\forall x\in K\;\forall a\;\forall x'\,
(T(x,a,x')\Rightarrow x'\in K).
\]

### Viability / controlled invariance

There exists a control choice/policy keeping the evolution in \(K\); robust controlled invariance adds universal disturbance after the control choice:

\[
\boxed{
\forall x\in K\;\exists u\;\forall d,\;
f(x,u,d)\in K.
}
\]

### Environment/adversarial trap

For a protected bad/undecided region \(R\), the adversarial orientation asks whether the environment has a response strategy that prevents forced escape.

Under a controller-then-environment model:

\[
\boxed{
\forall x\in R\;\forall u\;\exists d,\;
f(x,u,d)\in R.
}
\]

FLP is a more structured asynchronous/fairness realization: the scheduler can service the required fairness obligations while selecting a continuation that preserves bivalence.

## 80.3 Breaker

Erase the quantifier prefix.

Then the following collapse:

\[
\exists u\,\forall d
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\forall u\,\exists d.
\]

They have opposite strategic meaning.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameSuccessorRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{SameStrategicRegion}
}
\]

when quantifier/dependency orientation differs.

This recovers the earlier `QuantifierStructurePreserved` obligation.

## 80.4 Fold result

The following are protected refinements/orientations of one quantified closure family:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{InvariantRegion},
\mathsf{ViabilityRegion},
\mathsf{RobustControlledInvariant},
\mathsf{AdversarialTrap}
\subseteq
\mathsf{QSuccClosed}.
}
\]

`FairAdversarialTrap` additionally retains fairness/coverage obligations.

No new semantic primitive is required: the formula grammar, typed successor relation, and binding-supplied strategic roles suffice.

---

# 81. Fixed-point generation from quantified successor closure

Once a quantified predecessor/closure operator is formed:

\[
\mathcal P_{\pi}(K)
=
\{
x:
Q_1a_1\cdots Q_na_n\;
\exists x'\in K\;T(x,\vec a,x')
\},
\]

maximal viable/trap/safety regions are often greatest fixed points/post-fixed regions of a monotone operator, while reachability/attractor constructions often use least fixed points of a predecessor expansion.

This reopens the old pair:

\[
\mathsf{LeastClosedExtension}
\quad\text{vs}\quad
\mathsf{GreatestStableRegion}.
\]

### NEXT QUESTION

Can these two compiled patterns be folded into one binding-supplied monotone fixed-point relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Fix}_{\mu/\nu}(F)
}
\]

with least/greatest polarity retained as protected orientation, or do their regenerative/source behaviors differ in a way that cannot be recovered from one common operator contract?

A fold is valid only if:
- seed/closure behavior of least extensions regenerates;
- stability/coinductive behavior of greatest regions regenerates;
- warrant least-fixed-point semantics is not accidentally identified with object-level fixed points;
- monotonicity/complete-lattice assumptions remain explicit and binding-relative.

# 82. Fixed-point schema compression — least closure and greatest stability

The active residual asks whether:
- `LeastClosedExtension`;
- `GreatestStableRegion`

are protected orientations of one smaller relation.

## 82.1 Common parent

Let \((L,\le)\) be a binding-supplied ordered domain with enough completeness for the claimed theorem, and let:

\[
F:L\to L
\]

be a monotone operator.

Define the open relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExtremalFixedPoint}(F,\omega,x)
}
\]

where:

\[
\omega\in\{\mu,\nu\}
\]

is protected orientation:

\[
\omega=\mu
\Rightarrow
x=\operatorname{lfp}(F),
\]

\[
\omega=\nu
\Rightarrow
x=\operatorname{gfp}(F).
\]

Under Knaster-Tarski assumptions on a complete lattice, a monotone operator has least and greatest fixed points.

No order/lattice/monotonicity is imported constitutionally; all remain binding-relative.

## 82.2 Least closed extension regeneration

Given seed \(S\) and a closure/generation operator \(C\), form:

\[
F_S(X)=S\sqcup C(X)
\]

or the binding-appropriate monotone analogue.

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mu F_S
}
\]

regenerates the least closed/admissible extension containing \(S\).

Thus `LeastClosedExtension` is a \(\mu\)-orientation of `ExtremalFixedPoint`.

## 82.3 Greatest stable region regeneration

For a predecessor/stability operator \(P\):

\[
\boxed{
\nu P
}
\]

regenerates the greatest region closed/stable under the declared strategic successor condition.

This includes:
- bisimulation-style greatest relations;
- safety/trap kernels under suitable predecessor operators;
- the `GreatestStableRegion` pattern.

## 82.4 Breaker

Erase polarity.

Then inductive generation and coinductive stability collapse.

A least fixed point can be strictly different from the greatest fixed point of the same monotone operator.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameOperator}
\neq
\mathsf{SameFixedPointSemantics}
}
\]

without \(\mu/\nu\) orientation.

Further, erase monotonicity/completeness assumptions and the extremal fixed-point theorem may not apply.

## 82.5 Warrant-role breaker

Standing in the Inquiry Calculus is itself defined through a least fixed point of a support operator.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameFixedPointMathematics}
\neq
\mathsf{SameSemanticAuthorityRole}.
}
\]

An object-level least closure does not thereby acquire epistemic standing semantics.

The standing operator remains separately governed by ingress/support/authority/no-self-warrant constraints.

This preserves:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ObjectRecursion}
\neq
\mathsf{WarrantRecursion}.
}
\]

## 82.6 Fold result

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LeastClosedExtension},
\mathsf{GreatestStableRegion}
\subseteq
\mathsf{ExtremalFixedPoint}
}
\]

as protected \(\mu/\nu\) refinements under binding-supplied order/fixed-point laws.

`ExtremalFixedPoint` is a reusable mathematical relation schema/idiom, not a constitutional primitive.

---

The following prior patterns now factor more tightly:

- M027 `LeastClosedExtension` → \(\mu\)-fixed-point refinement;

- quantified viability/trap regions → predecessor operator + \(\nu\);
- standing → \(\mu\) over its independent support operator, with semantic role preserved;

This yields a stronger no-collapse law:

\mathsf{FixedPointShape}
\text{ transports mathematically;}

\mathsf{FixedPointRole}
\text{ remains binding/contract specific}.
}
\]

# 84. Intensional/effectivity crawl — proof complexity and method representation

The active residual asks whether extensionally identical methods can remain protected-different because of proof/execution resource consequences.

## 84.1 Source domain: proof complexity

Cook-Reckhow-style propositional proof systems can be sound and complete for the same tautology language while differing in proof-size behavior and simulation strength.

Let \(P_1,P_2\) prove the same protected theorem set:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Thm}(P_1)=\mathsf{Thm}(P_2).
}
\]

This extensional equality does not imply equality of minimal proof length, verification cost, or simulation relation.

For a theorem \(\phi\), define:

\[
\ell_{P}(\phi)
=
\min\{
|p|:\mathsf{Proof}_{P}(p,\phi)
\}.
\]

Then a protected resource separator may be:

\[
\boxed{
?\phi[
\mathsf{Provable}_{P_1}(\phi)
\land
\mathsf{Provable}_{P_2}(\phi)
\land
\ell_{P_1}(\phi)\not\preceq_R\ell_{P_2}(\phi)
].
}
\]

The same issue occurs with ordinary algorithms computing the same mathematical function but with different time/memory/stability profiles.

## 84.2 Object-free relation

The method web must distinguish:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExtensionalMethodRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{Realization/DerivationRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{ResourceConsequence}.
}
\]

Represent:

\[
\mathsf{Realizes}(m,\rho),
\]

\[
\mathsf{Occurrence/Derivation}(m,x,\pi,y),
\]

\[
\mathsf{ResourceProfile}(\pi,r),
\]

\[
\mathsf{ProtectedCostConsequence}(r,\mathcal H).
\]

The protected resource property belongs to the reified method occurrence/derivation path, not to input-output relation alone.

## 84.3 Breaker against extensional method folding

Suppose:

\[
\forall x,\quad
P_1(x)=P_2(x)
\]

at the protected extensional output horizon.

If the current horizon additionally protects worst-case time/proof size/memory, the two methods may remain distinguishable.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExtensionalEquivalence}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{MethodEquivalence}_{\mathcal H}.
}
\]

This independently regenerates:
- behavior-preserving rewrite vs improvement;
- endpoint vs derivation;
- representation cost vs protected consequence;
- method-record cost/provenance roles.

## 84.4 Binding relativity

Resource complexity is not semantic in isolation from a model/cost regime.

A comparison sort has an \(\Omega(n\log n)\) comparison lower bound in the comparison model, while adding stronger key-access assumptions permits different algorithms.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResourceComplexity}
=
\mathsf{ComplexityRelativeTo}(m,\mathcal E_R,\mathcal H).
}
\]

The binding must supply:
- cost model;
- input-size/grain representation;
- permitted primitive operations;
- adversarial/average/randomized quantifiers;
- resource horizon.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameProgramRepresentation}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameComplexityClaim}
}
\]

when the cost model changes.

## 84.5 Fold result

No procedural semantic primitive is forced.

Intensional/effectivity-sensitive methods are regenerated by:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ReifiedMethodForm}
+
\mathsf{Derivation/OccurrenceRelation}
+
\mathsf{BindingSuppliedResourceModel}
+
\mathsf{ProtectedConsequence/Warrant}.
}
\]

The current method-web architecture already has the necessary roles.

M_2\succeq_{\mathrm{cap}}M_1

when \(M_2\) can implement/discharge every currently protected task capability of \(M_1\) under the declared environment.

\mathcal R(M).
\]

Then:

\boxed{
\mathsf{CapabilityPreorder}
\neq

A capability-dominating method need not be resource-dominating.

No universal scalar "best method" is introduced.

# 87. Multi-axis transport — correctness bridge vs resource bridge

The active question is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CorrectnessBridge}
\stackrel{?}{=}
\mathsf{ResourceBridge}.
}
\]

## 87.1 Breaker

Comparison sorting provides a direct source breaker.

A translation may preserve the mathematical sorting relation:

\[
\mathsf{SortedPermutation}(x,y)
\]

while changing the model of permitted primitive operations.

The \(\Omega(n\log n)\) lower bound applies in the comparison model; stronger key-access assumptions permit different complexity behavior.

Therefore a semantic bridge preserving sorted output does not transport the comparison-model complexity theorem unless it also preserves the relevant primitive-operation and size/cost structure.

Proof-system simulation gives a second realization:
two proof systems may prove the same formulas, while polynomial simulation/proof-length preservation requires an additional transformation relation on proofs and their lengths.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CorrectnessTransport}
\neq
\mathsf{ResourceTransport}.
}
\]

## 87.2 Object-free relation

Let \(b_S\) translate the semantic roles/consequences.

Let \(b_R\) translate:
- derivation/execution objects;
- input-size representation;
- permitted primitive operations;
- resource measure;
- adversarial/average/randomized quantifier regime.

A resource guarantee transports only if an explicit resource diagram commutes/preserves the declared comparison:

\[
\boxed{
b_{\mathrm{cost}}\circ
\mathsf{Cost}_{D}
\preceq
\mathsf{Cost}_{E}\circ
b_{\mathrm{trace}}
}
\]

or the binding-appropriate direction.

No universal equality is required.

## 87.3 Fold result

A single bridge object may carry both semantic and resource relations, but the obligations are independent axes.

The generic contract is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AxisIndexedBridge}
(
b,\mathcal A,\mathcal H
)
}
\]

with:

\[
\forall a\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{protected}},
\quad
\mathsf{PreservesAxis}_a(b).
\]

`CorrectnessBridge` and `ResourceBridge` are protected projections of the same bridge web, not identical obligations.

This is already a refinement of `ProtectedPathFactorization` over multiple protected consequences.

No new primitive is required.

---

# 88. Cryptographic hostile return — correctness vs information leakage

The resource split selects a stronger foreign domain: zero-knowledge interactive proofs.

Two proof protocols may:
- establish the same statement/language membership;
- satisfy comparable completeness/soundness;
- differ on what the verifier can learn from the interaction.

Zero knowledge formalizes the extra information axis through simulation of the verifier's view.

## 88.1 Protected separator

Let \(P_1,P_2\) have the same protected acceptance/correctness relation.

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?V^\star,K[
\mathsf{SameCorrectness}(P_1,P_2)
\land
\Con^{\mathrm{view}}_{K,V^\star}(P_1)
\neq
\Con^{\mathrm{view}}_{K,V^\star}(P_2)
].
}
\]

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProofCorrectness}
\neq
\mathsf{InformationDisclosure}.
}
\]

The latter is a consequence of the interaction transcript/view under an adversarial verifier family, not merely the final accept/reject bit.

## 88.2 Relation to current network

This does not force an information-leakage primitive.

The current calculus already retains:
- method strategy vs realized transcript;
- derivation/occurrence provenance;
- future inquiry/information consequences;
- binding-supplied probability/computation;
- protected continuation families.

Zero knowledge is a binding-native theorem/contract over those relations.

The method web may contain a protected axis such as:

\[
\mathsf{DisclosureGuarantee}(M,z)
\]

with its simulator/checking assumptions and computational horizon.

## 88.3 Stronger bridge law

Method transport must therefore be axis-explicit.

A foreign binding may preserve:

\[
\mathsf{Correctness}
\]

but fail to preserve:

\[
\mathsf{Resource},
\mathsf{Privacy/Disclosure},
\mathsf{Authority},
\mathsf{FutureInquiryEffect}.
\]

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BridgeValid}_{\mathcal H}
\iff
\text{every axis actually protected by }\mathcal H
\text{ has a standing transport obligation}.
}
\]

Unprotected axes need not be transported.

This is exactly protected contextual equivalence applied to bridges.

---

# 89. Network compression after intensional/information transport

The new resource/privacy distinctions do not add method species.

They compress into one relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedAxisFamily}(M,\mathcal H)
}
\]

implemented simply as the family of protected continuation/consequence relations already associated with a method web.

Static method equality becomes:

\[
M_1\equiv_{\mathcal H}M_2
\iff
\forall K\in\mathcal H,\;
\Con_K(M_1)=\Con_K(M_2).
\]

Thus:
- correctness;
- proof/execution cost;
- information leakage;
- path provenance;
- authority;
- future inquiry effect

are not constitutional "dimensions"; they are possible protected consequences supplied by the binding/horizon.

If arbitrary protected axes can be added to \(\mathcal H\), then the sufficient-present/network compression depends critically on whether those consequences are **observable/checkable**.

# 90. Hyperproperty crawl — protected consequences over execution families

The active residual asks whether the current consequence machinery can represent properties that are inherently relational across multiple executions.

## 90.1 Source domain

Ordinary trace properties classify individual executions.

Hyperproperties classify sets/families of executions and therefore express relational security conditions such as noninterference that cannot be reduced to the accept/reject or local state of one run.

Let:

\[
\mathsf{Exec}(M)
\]

be the represented family of executions admitted by method/system \(M\).

A hyperproperty is represented as:

\[
\boxed{
H:
\mathsf{ExecFamily}
\rightsquigarrow
\mathsf{Judgment}
}
\]

or as an ordinary relation over a reified execution-family form.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SingleTraceConsequence}
\neq
\mathsf{ExecutionFamilyConsequence}.
}
\]

## 90.2 Breaker against single-occurrence checking

Construct two systems with individual executions that all look locally acceptable, while the relation between paired executions violates noninterference.

No single occurrence by itself supplies the violating pair relation.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticHyperproperty}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SingleOccurrenceCheckable}.
}
\]

This is not a failure of semantic representation.

It is a checker/actuality/coverage distinction.

## 90.3 Calculus lift

Recursive form closure allows:
- execution traces;
- pairs/tuples of traces;
- represented execution families;
- relations between them

to occupy typed roles.

The protected question is simply higher-order:

\[
\boxed{
?E_1,\ldots,E_k[
\mathsf{ExecutionsOf}(M,E_i)
\land
\mathsf{Violates}_H(E_1,\ldots,E_k)
].
}
\]

For genuinely set-level hyperproperties, the binding supplies an execution-family representation and corresponding relation/checker.

No constitutional hyperproperty primitive is required.

## 90.4 Self-composition method

For \(k\)-safety hyperproperties, established self-composition methods can reduce the relational property over \(k\) executions to an ordinary safety property of a \(k\)-fold product/self-composed system.

This compiles as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelfCompose}_k(M)
\to
\mathsf{SafetyCheck}.
}
\]

The method is a native composition/representation transformation plus a proof that the protected hyperproperty corresponds to the transformed safety property.

It is therefore an instance of:
- native semantic composition;
- protected path factorization;
- behavior-preserving representation change;
- grounding/checking.

## 90.5 Strong breaker against universal self-composition

Do not infer:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Hyperproperty}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{FiniteSelfCompositionSafety}.
}
\]

Only appropriate classes such as \(k\)-safety admit that reduction.

Broader hyperproperties, including examples such as generalized noninterference in the standard hyperproperty taxonomy, need not be finite-\(k\) hypersafety and therefore are not universally reducible to a finite bad-tuple safety check.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HyperpropertyRepresentable}
\neq
\mathsf{FiniteTupleWitnessable}
\neq
\mathsf{RuntimeMonitorable}.
}
\]

## 90.6 Network result

No new semantic node is forced.

The method network gains only a derived method contract:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HyperpropertyReduction}
}
\]

whose applicability explicitly requires the source hyperproperty class to admit the selected finite-product/self-composition theorem.

Failure to satisfy that applicability condition returns a representation/checking residual; it does not refute the hyperproperty.

+

}

Its domain, witness structure, checker, and coverage are part of the binding/method web.

## Active residual

The strongest remaining breaker is now **semantic property versus finite evidence**.

Question:

Can a protected claim whose falsity/validity is not finitely witnessable still enter standing without collapsing the current actuality/checker separation?

This should be pressure-tested against liveness/property verification:
- safety properties admit finite bad prefixes;
- liveness properties generally do not admit finite counterexample prefixes in the same way.

# 92. Safety/liveness crawl — semantic property vs evidence shape

The active residual asks whether liveness requires a semantic primitive beyond typed relations over infinite executions.

## 92.1 Common semantic carrier

Let:

\[
P\subseteq \mathsf{Exec}^{\omega}
\]

be a protected temporal property.

Safety and liveness are different classes of such properties, not different execution ontologies.

A safety violation has a finite bad prefix that cannot be repaired by any extension.

A liveness property has no such finite irremediable prefix: every finite prefix can still be extended to a satisfying execution.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TemporalProperty}
\text{ is common;}
\quad
\mathsf{RefutationWitnessShape}
\text{ differs}.
}
\]

## 92.2 Protected distinctions

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticProperty}
\neq
\mathsf{FiniteRefutability}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoFiniteCounterexample}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{LivenessSatisfied}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SafetyViolation}
\text{ may have finite bad-prefix evidence;}
\quad
\mathsf{LivenessViolation}
\text{ generally does not}.
}
\]

This is a checker/coverage distinction, not a new semantic relation.

## 92.3 Evidence-shape relation

Define a method-selection descriptor:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EvidenceShape}(P,w,\mathcal C)
}
\]

where \(w\) may describe:
- one finite bad prefix;
- a finite tuple/set of traces;
- an infinite/fairness-dependent run;
- a proof/certificate/ranking argument;
- another binding-supplied witness form.

For example:
- safety → finite bad prefix for refutation;
- \(k\)-safety hyperproperty → at most \(k\) traces/finite bad tuple under the source theorem;
- liveness → no universal finite bad-prefix refutation interface.

`EvidenceShape` is ordinary typed relational metadata over the method/property web.

## 92.4 Method self-leverage

The evidence shape can generate checker-method holes.

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?M[
\mathsf{AcceptsWitnessShape}(M,w)
\land
\mathsf{ApplicableTo}(M,P)
\land
\mathsf{CoverageSufficient}(M,\mathcal H)
].
}
\]

This routes:
- finite safety witnesses to reachability/invariant/counterexample methods;
- \(k\)-safety hyperproperties to eligible self-composition/product methods;
- liveness to fairness/ranking/temporal/fixed-point methods as supplied by the binding.

No hard-coded method switch is required.

## 92.5 Breaker against witness-shape identity

Two properties can share the same semantic carrier and truth value while requiring different evidence/checking methods.

Conversely, the same finite witness shape may support different semantic claims.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EvidenceShape}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticMeaning}.
}
\]

The method network must preserve both.

## 92.6 Fold result

Safety/liveness/hypersafety do not force additional semantic primitives.

They refine:
- protected consequence relation;
- witness/evidence shape;
- checker coverage;
- fairness/infinite-execution binding;
- method applicability.

is re-established relative to the enlarged domain/regression corpus.

# 94. Nonmonotonic revision crawl — append-only ancestry vs revisable standing

**Recursion source:** `Pasted text(20260826-034230).txt`

The hostile domain is belief revision / truth maintenance.

Let:
- \(H_t\) be append-only authoritative ancestry;
- \(T_t\) the current support/standing operator;
- \(S_t=\mu T_t\) the current standing set.

A new event \(e_{t+1}\) may change ingress, assumptions, applicability, inconsistency/nogood relations, or accepted support routes, producing:

\[
T_t \longrightarrow T_{t+1}.
\]

Then:

\[
S_t=\mu T_t,
\qquad
S_{t+1}=\mu T_{t+1}.
\]

Even though each \(T_t\) is monotone in its argument \(X\), it does not follow that:

\[
S_t\subseteq S_{t+1}.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AppendOnlyHistory}
\neq
\mathsf{MonotoneCurrentStanding}.
}
\]

A previously standing relation may cease to stand because all live support routes fail, applicability changes, or an explicit inconsistency policy defeats the supporting environment. Its historical warrant/provenance record remains.

This is already compatible with the canonical calculus's distinction between standing, applicability, historical ancestry, and explicit revision.

## 94.1 AGM source relation

AGM-style contraction/revision asks for successor belief states satisfying a family of rationality postulates.

Belief-base/kernel contraction supplies the more structurally relevant relation:

For target belief \(p\), a \(p\)-kernel is a minimal subset that implies \(p\).

To contract \(p\), an incision/removal set must hit every \(p\)-kernel.

This gives the object-free hole:

\[
\boxed{
?R[
\forall K\in\mathsf{MinimalSupports}(p),\;
R\cap K\neq\varnothing
\land
\mathsf{ProtectedRetention}(B\setminus R)
].
}
\]

This is a hitting-set/support-cut repair over dependency structure.

It regenerates existing:
- minimal support environments;
- support-failure cuts;
- conflict localization;
- repair/hitting;
- nondominated/minimal repair frontier.

No new contraction primitive is forced.

## 94.2 Minimal change is not unique

Partial-meet contraction selects among maximal remainder sets; kernel contraction may admit multiple incision sets.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MinimalChange}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{UniqueRevision}.
}
\]

The calculus must retain a binding-supplied preference/order or nondominated frontier where several lawful repairs survive.

No universal scalar "least change" is introduced.

## 94.3 Truth-maintenance source relation

Truth-maintenance systems retain justifications/dependencies so derived beliefs can be revised when assumptions change or contradictions arise.

The transported relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DependencyConditionedStanding}
(\lambda,E,t).
}
\]

A conclusion is active only while at least one admitted support environment remains standing and applicable.

A contradiction/nogood does not erase ancestry; it invalidates particular environments and propagates status changes through the dependency relation.

This compiles directly from:
- support environments;
- open dependency boundaries;
- applicability;
- standing recomputation;
- support-failure profile;
- residual repair.

## 94.4 Important ontology-contamination breaker: two recoveries

AGM's Recovery postulate for contraction is not the same relation as regenerative recovery in Inquiry Calculus.

AGM Recovery concerns restoration of original belief consequences after a contracted sentence is reintroduced.

Regenerative recovery concerns reconstructing/reacquiring a protected representation, competence, derivation, or distinction after a fold is reopened.

Belief-base contraction can reject AGM Recovery while still preserving provenance sufficient for regenerative reconstruction.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AGMRecovery}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativeRecovery}.
}
\]

The shared word must not induce a schema fold.

---

# 95. Iterated belief revision — current standing is not a sufficient revision state

The stronger hostile case is iterated revision.

Darwiche–Pearl show that AGM constraints on a current belief set are insufficient to regulate rational sequences of revisions; the update process may depend on an epistemic state whose structure is not captured by the presently believed propositions alone.

Formally, construct two epistemic states:

\[
E_1,E_2
\]

with identical current standing belief sets:

\[
\mathsf{Bel}(E_1)=\mathsf{Bel}(E_2)=K,
\]

but different revision dispositions:

\[
U_{E_1}\neq U_{E_2}.
\]

Then there exists an admissible next input \(p\) such that:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Bel}(U_{E_1}(p))
\neq
\mathsf{Bel}(U_{E_2}(p)).
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameCurrentStanding}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameFutureRevisionBehavior}.
}
\]

## 95.1 Sufficient-present consequence

A research/epistemic present sufficient only for current conclusions can be too coarse for future revision.

If revision behavior is protected, exact present-state equivalence must include continuation/update disposition:

\[
\boxed{
x\equiv_{\mathcal H}^{\mathrm{state}}y
\Rightarrow
\forall e,\;
U(x,e)\equiv_{\mathcal H}U(y,e)
}
\]

for the admitted future event family.

This is exactly continuation descent / recursive state sufficiency.

Thus the existing sufficient-present decomposition is strengthened:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CurrentStanding}
\neq
\mathsf{RevisionSufficientState}.
}
\]

The latter includes, actively or regeneratively:
- current standing;
- support/dependency organization relevant to revision;
- revision/update policy;
- entrenchment/selection relation when binding-supplied;
- provenance needed to reopen distinctions.

## 95.2 Belief bases as foreign source round trip

Different belief bases can have the same logical closure but respond differently to contraction/revision because their dependency/basic-belief structure differs.

This is another realization of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameExtensionalConsequenceSet}
\neq
\mathsf{SameRegenerative/RevisionPresentation}.
}
\]

It returns directly to:
- determination class vs determination presentation;
- endpoint vs derivation;
- present consequence sufficiency vs continuation sufficiency.

## 95.3 Compression result

No new nonmonotonic semantic primitive is required.

Belief revision compiles as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NewStandingIngress}
\to
\mathsf{Conflict/SupportKernelLocalization}
\to
\mathsf{CandidateSupportCuts}
\to
\mathsf{BindingSuppliedSelectionFrontier}
\to
\mathsf{SuccessorPresentation}
\to
\mu T_{t+1}.
}
\]

The authoritative ancestry remains append-only.

The active standing/presentation may revise.

This directly instantiates the project law:

\[
\boxed{
\text{HISTORY MAY BE MONOTONE WHILE CURRENT SEMANTIC COMMITMENT IS NOT.}
}
\]

Compile a reusable macro:

\boxed{
\mathsf{RevisionBySupportDelta}(\Sigma_t,e)
}

with transparent expansion:

1. preserve/ground the incoming event;

## Active residual

The next hostile question is **priority/entrenchment learning**.

Revision requires a selection/priority relation when several minimally changed successor presentations survive.

Can that priority relation itself be learned/updated from experience without making current outcomes self-warrant the revision policy that selected them?

This connects iterated belief revision to the previously established:
- predecessor-judged self-revision;
- method-policy learning;
- no-self-warrant;

# 97. Policy-learning crawl — endogenous selection and counterfactual evaluation

The active residual is whether a revision/selection policy can learn its own priority relation from outcomes generated under that same policy.

## 97.1 Source domain: contextual bandits / logged policy evaluation

At round \(t\):
- context \(x_t\) is observed;
- logging/behavior policy \(\pi_0(a\mid x_t)\) selects action \(a_t\);
- only the reward/outcome of the selected action is observed.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ObservedOutcome}(x,a)
\neq
\mathsf{CounterfactualOutcomeFamily}(x,\cdot).
}
\]

A target policy \(\pi\) cannot generally be evaluated by treating the logged sample as though actions had been chosen independently of the logging policy.

Inverse-propensity/counterfactual estimators require explicit knowledge/estimation of the logging selection probability and adequate support/overlap.

## 97.2 Protected separator

For two candidate actions/policies \(a,b\), the current policy may observe only \(a\).

Ask:

\[
\boxed{
?r_b[
\mathsf{CounterfactualOutcome}(x,b,r_b)
].
}
\]

Failure to obtain \(r_b\) does not establish that \(b\) is inferior.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelectedOutcomeEvidence}
\neq
\mathsf{ComparativePolicyEvidence}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Unselected/Unsupported}
\neq
\mathsf{Worse}.
}
\]

## 97.3 Support-overlap relation

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CoverageSupport}(\pi,\pi_0,\mathcal X)
}
\]

when every target action/context consequence whose value is claimed has adequate support under the logging policy/model assumptions.

In the simplest importance-weighting form, positivity requires:

\[
\pi(a\mid x)>0
\Rightarrow
\pi_0(a\mid x)>0
\]

on the claimed evaluation domain.

When this fails, return:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CounterfactualCoverageGap}
}
\]

rather than a target-value judgment.

## 97.4 Object-free relation

The portable relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelectionConditionedEvidence}
(
\mathsf{selector},
\mathsf{observed},
\mathsf{target},
\mathsf{coverage},
\mathsf{estimator}
).
}
\]

Any inference from selected outcomes to alternative-policy value must state:
- selection mechanism;
- overlap/coverage;
- outcome-model assumptions if used;
- estimator/checker;
- resource/variance horizon where consequential.

No special bandit primitive is transported.

## 97.5 Revision-policy consequence

A belief-revision/entrenchment policy updated only from consequences of revisions it itself selected faces the same structural defect.

If alternative revisions are never instantiated or otherwise modeled under a warranted bridge, their comparative consequence is not actual evidence.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PolicyChosenHistory}
\neq
\mathsf{EvidenceForOptimalRevisionPolicy}.
}
\]

A lawful policy-learning route requires at least one of:
- deliberate exploration/intervention;
- standing counterfactual/causal model;
- externally generated variation;
- another independently warranted comparison route.

This refines `ProspectiveEvaluationIntegrity` and `FutureAwareInquiryChoice`.

---

# 98. Doubly robust return — alternate support routes without support collapse

Doubly robust contextual-bandit evaluation combines:
- a model of outcomes/rewards;
- a model/record of the behavior policy.

Its guarantee can remain valid when one of these routes is sufficiently correct under the method's assumptions.

This provides a foreign realization of multiple alternative support routes.

However:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TwoModelRobustness}
\neq
\mathsf{TwoIndependentEvidenceSources}.
}
\]

The routes are mathematically coupled inside one estimator and have method-specific assumptions.

What transports is only:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PluralDischargeRoutes}
}
\]

with distinct failure profiles.

This is already represented by the calculus's family of minimal support environments / alternate warrant routes.

No `DoublyRobust` semantic node is retained.

## 98.1 Support-failure question

Ask:

\[
?E[
E\in\Supp(\lambda)
\land
\Closed(E,\lambda)
].
\]

A method with several admissible routes survives loss/failure of one route exactly when another closed route remains.

The estimator-specific theorem supplies which route sets are actually sufficient.

The active method compiles to:

\mathsf{LearnRevisionPolicy}

as a transparent macro:

No new semantic primitive is required.

\[

\neq

\]

\[
\boxed{

\neq

# 100. Active residual — exploration itself as a protected intervention

The policy-learning result generates the next hole.

If unchosen alternatives are not evaluable without coverage, the reasoner may need to deliberately choose an inquiry/action whose immediate expected consequence is worse than the current best estimate in order to improve future discrimination.

This is stronger than ordinary separator search because the separator may change the world and incur irreversible opportunity cost.

The current network already contains:
- `TransformativeProbe`;
- `FutureAwareInquiryChoice`;
- prospective evaluation integrity;
- binding-supplied cost/risk/reversibility;
- environment-conditioned guarantees.

### NEXT QUESTION

Can exploration/exploitation methods in online learning be fully regenerated as `FutureAwareInquiryChoice` over transformative probes plus a binding-supplied regret/information objective, or does regret minimization expose a new relation between current loss, counterfactual benchmark, and information acquisition?

Use adversarial multi-armed bandits as the hostile next domain, because their regret guarantee compares realized performance to a counterfactual comparator while feedback is deliberately partial.
