# 166. Distributed-authority crawl — authority as a supported access structure

**Recursion source:** current `REGENERATIVE PERCEPTUAL-APERTURE DOMAIN CRAWL`

The live residual was `AuthorityRecoveryGap`:
what survives when no single predecessor/external authority object remains?

Byzantine quorum systems and threshold cryptography provide the hostile source.

## 166.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorityRoot}
=
\mathsf{SingleAuthorityObject}.
}
\]

Authority can be realized through an access structure:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal A
\subseteq
\mathcal P(P)
}
\]

where each authorized set \(Q\in\mathcal A\) can jointly discharge a protected authorization relation.

Examples include:
- threshold signer sets;
- Byzantine quorums;
- replicated configuration quorums;
- multi-party acceptance committees.

## 166.2 Object-free authority basis

For authorization claim \(\lambda_A\), define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorityBasis}(\lambda_A)
=
\{E:
E\text{ is a minimal closed support environment for }\lambda_A\}.
}
\]

This is not a new support semantics.

It is the existing family of minimal support environments applied to an authority-typed claim.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TransitionAuthorityRoute}
=
\text{a standing support route for an }
\mathsf{AuthorizesTransition}
\text{ relation}.
}
\]

But retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SupportForAuthority}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthorityRole}.
}
\]

Support establishes that an authority relation stands.
The binding supplies what that authority relation authorizes.

## 166.3 Byzantine quorum coherence

A family of authorized sets is insufficient by itself.

For conflicting authoritative outcomes to be excluded under a fault model, the native quorum theorem requires an intersection/coherence condition relative to fail-prone sets.

Abstractly:

\[
\boxed{
\forall Q_1,Q_2\in\mathcal A,\;
\mathsf{StandingCorrectIntersection}(Q_1,Q_2,\mathcal F).
}
\]

The exact condition is binding-supplied.

This is a `CompositionalGuaranteeFactorization` obligation over:
- local authority shares/votes;
- quorum/access structure;
- failure model;
- global uniqueness/consistency guarantee.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorizedSubsetExists}
\neq
\mathsf{GloballyCoherentAuthorityBasis}.
}
\]

No quorum primitive is required.

---

# 167. Proactive authority refresh — semantic authority vs credential realization

Proactive secret sharing and threshold-signature refresh provide a stronger self-modification relation.

A protected service key/secret can persist while:
- shares change;
- compromised participants are refreshed;
- participants may change under dynamic variants;
- the secret need not be reconstructed during ordinary refresh.

Therefore distinguish:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthoritySemanticIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{CredentialRepresentationIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{HolderMembershipIdentity}.
}
\]

A change in share holders does not necessarily change the protected authority role.

A changed key/credential does not necessarily change the authority role if a standing transition bridge says it is the successor credential for that role.

Conversely, retaining the same key bits does not preserve authority if the governing authorization relation has changed.

## 167.1 Regenerative return

The current self-modification machine should therefore model:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorityRole}
\leftrightarrow
\mathsf{CredentialRealization}
\leftrightarrow
\mathsf{CurrentHolder/SupportBasis}
}
\]

with explicit versioned bridges rather than identity collapse.

This is `ProtectedAbstractionTower` applied to authority.

---

# 168. Threshold impossibility — below the reconstruction/support frontier

Threshold secret sharing provides an exact breaker for "reconstruct authority from whatever fragments remain."

For a \(t\)-threshold sharing, fewer than the required threshold of shares are constructed specifically to reveal no information about the protected secret under the scheme's assumptions.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ResidualAuthorityFragments}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{RecoverableCredential}.
}
\]

When no authorized reconstruction/support set survives:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CredentialRecoveryGap}.
}
\]

This is stronger than failed search:
under the declared threshold model, the available fragments are intentionally insufficient.

## 168.1 Important scope distinction

Credential recovery is not authority recovery.

Even perfect recovery of the old private key establishes only possession of the credential.

The standing authorization relation still determines whether that credential currently has authority.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CredentialRecovery}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthorityConstitution}.
}
\]

---

# 169. Dealerless distributed key generation — new credential without old authority secret

Distributed key generation (DKG) supplies the maximal contrast.

A set of participants can jointly generate a fresh threshold key without a trusted dealer and without reconstructing an old secret.

Therefore reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OldCredentialUnrecoverable}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{NoNewCredentialCanBeConstructed}.
}
\]

A new credential can be generated.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FreshCredentialConstructed}
\neq
\mathsf{FreshCredentialIsAuthoritative}.
}
\]

The DKG protocol requires a participant/configuration/fault-model context.
Even when cryptographically correct, the transcript does not by itself establish that its output key is the legitimate authority for the protected system role.

## 169.1 Split the former residual

The old `AuthorityRecoveryGap` decomposes into:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{CredentialRecoveryGap},\\
&\mathsf{AuthorityConstitutionGap}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

`CredentialRecoveryGap` asks whether the required capability/token can be reconstructed or freshly generated.

`AuthorityConstitutionGap` asks what standing relation makes one candidate credential/configuration the authorized successor.

The second cannot be solved by cryptographic key generation alone.

---

# 170. Reconfiguration crawl — membership change requires cross-version coherence

Raft membership change gives a clean non-Byzantine source:
directly switching from old to new membership can create disjoint majorities and split authority; joint consensus uses overlapping old/new majorities during transition.

Vertical Paxos supplies another binding using an auxiliary configuration master.

Asynchronous Byzantine reconfiguration supplies still another realization using forward-secure signatures and dynamic agreement structure.

The portable relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Configuration}_{t}
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{TransitionBridge}}
\mathsf{Configuration}_{t+1}
}
\]

with a protected global consistency theorem across the transition.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ValidOldConfiguration}
+
\mathsf{ValidNewConfiguration}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SafeConfigurationTransition}.
}
\]

The transition itself has semantics.

This regenerates:
- `GovernedJudgeMigration`;
- `ProtectedCoherenceFactorization`;
- `CompositionalGuaranteeFactorization`.

No new reconfiguration primitive.

---

# 171. Authority-family compression

The authority self-modification branch now compresses to four layers:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
1.\;&\mathsf{AuthorityRelation}:
&&\text{what is authorized?}\\
2.\;&\mathsf{AuthorityBasis}:
&&\text{which support/access sets can discharge it?}\\
3.\;&\mathsf{CredentialRealization}:
&&\text{what tokens/keys/votes instantiate those roles?}\\
4.\;&\mathsf{TransitionBridge}:
&&\text{how do versions of 1--3 lawfully succeed one another?}
\end{aligned}
}
\]

These layers are protectedly distinct.

The entire structure is regenerated from:
- typed authority relations in the binding;
- existing support environments/standing;
- composition/quorum law;
- bridge/coherence;
- versioned succession/reopening.

No new constitutional node is forced.

---

# 172. Repository self-modification consequences

The cohesive Rust machine should not encode "authority" as one key/root field.

Future accepted-version machinery should represent:

```text
AuthorityRelation
AuthorityBasis / SupportEnvironment family
CredentialRealization
TransitionAuthorityRoute
VersionedBridge
```

as separate referenced roles where the binding requires them.

The strongest remaining authority question is no longer credential recovery.

It is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{What question makes a candidate authority relation itself stand?}
}
\]

Attempt maximal fold:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorityConstitution}
\stackrel{?}{=}
\mathsf{OrdinaryStandingOfAnAuthorityTypedRelation}.
}
\]

If this holds, then there is no separate authority-constitution engine:
the binding defines the semantics of `Authorizes`, and the ordinary grounding/support/warrant machinery determines whether a particular `Authorizes(...)` claim stands.

Breaker to preserve:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EvidenceThatAuthorityExists}
\neq
\mathsf{Meaning/EffectOfAuthority}.
}
\]

The former may fold into ordinary standing.
The latter remains binding semantics.

This question is executed next.

# 174. Authority constitution ablation — no separate constitution engine

The maximal fold succeeds.

Let the binding provide an authority-typed relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Authorizes}(a,\tau,c)
}
\]

meaning that authority form \(a\), under transition/context \(\tau\), authorizes consequence/change \(c\).

The inquiry system does not create the semantic effect of this relation.

It asks whether a particular instance stands.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthorityConstitution}
=
\mathsf{Standing/AdmissionOfAnAuthorityTypedRelation}
}
\]

at the calculus level.

## 174.1 Exact separation retained

Do not collapse:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Standing}(\mathsf{Authorizes}(a,\tau,c))
}
\]

with:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticMeaningOfAuthorizes}.
}
\]

The binding supplies the latter.
The support/warrant machinery can establish the former.

Thus the authority problem now has no special epistemic engine.

## 174.2 Consequence for `TransitionAuthorityRoute`

`TransitionAuthorityRoute` becomes a transparent macro:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TransitionAuthorityRoute}
=
Q_{\mathrm{Ground}}
(
\mathsf{AuthorizesTransition}(a,J_t,\pi,J_{t+1})
)
}
\]

plus any native composition/quorum/bridge laws required by the binding.

The macro remains useful operationally, but it is not an irreducible relation schema.

## 174.3 Final authority residual

The hard boundary becomes informational rather than authority-specific:

\[
\boxed{
\text{If no standing evidence/authority relation distinguishes
candidate successor regimes, no internal reasoning procedure may
promote one merely because it can be generated.}
}
\]

That is ordinary `Unknown` / representation / authority-support failure.

No new primitive.

## Status

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}_{\mathrm{authority/self\mbox{-}mod}}
}
\]

under the enlarged quorum/threshold/DKG/reconfiguration corpus.

# 175. Delegation / attenuation / revocation crawl — authority standing is path-sensitive

The authority branch had folded `AuthorityConstitution` into ordinary standing of an authority-typed relation.

The next hostile question is whether delegated authority is fully determined by its current endpoint:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Authorizes}(b,c)
}
\]

or whether the derivation path remains protected.

## 175.1 Source frame

Let:
- \(a\) be a delegating authority carrier;
- \(b\) a receiving authority carrier;
- \(\phi\) the authority/scope being transferred;
- \(d:a\rightsquigarrow b\) a delegation occurrence;
- \(v\) validity conditions;
- \(r\) revocation dependencies;
- \(\delta\) redelegation permission.

The binding supplies what counts as:
- authority carrier;
- scope;
- delegation;
- attenuation;
- revocation;
- validity.

Do not assume a principal-name ontology.

## 175.2 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameCurrentAuthorizationOutcome}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SameAuthorizationState}.
}
\]

Two authorization routes may currently permit the same action while differing in:
- delegator;
- scope provenance;
- expiry;
- caveats;
- redelegation permission;
- revocation dependency;
- trusted roots;
- path-specific assumptions.

A later event can invalidate one route while leaving another standing.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SamePermissionNow}
\neq
\mathsf{SameFutureRevocationBehavior}.
}
\]

## 175.3 Attenuating delegation

Macaroon-style caveats and SPKI-style partial empowerment supply a native relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Attenuates}(\phi_{out},\phi_{in})
}
\]

meaning the delegated authority is no stronger than the authority licensed for transfer.

The exact order need not be a set-inclusion order. It is binding supplied.

A delegation edge is admissible only if its transfer relation is standing:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Delegates}(a,b,\phi_{out})
\land
\mathsf{MayDelegate}(a,\phi_{in})
\land
\mathsf{Attenuates}(\phi_{out},\phi_{in}).
}
\]

This preserves:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CanExercise}
\neq
\mathsf{CanDelegate}.
}
\]

Possessing/exercising a permission and having authority to redelegate it are separate roles unless the binding identifies them.

## 175.4 Object-capability breaker

Object-capability security supplies a maximally different realization.

Authority may be conveyed by possession of an unforgeable reference rather than by global identity lookup.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PrincipalIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthorityCarrier}.
}
\]

The calculus should therefore keep authority on typed relations/forms, not encode it around a privileged identity ontology.

## 175.5 Delegation chain composition

For:

\[
a_0
\xrightarrow{d_1}
a_1
\xrightarrow{d_2}
\cdots
\xrightarrow{d_n}
a_n,
\]

the endpoint authorization stands only when the binding-supplied composition law discharges:
- each edge's authority to delegate;
- attenuation/scope constraints;
- validity intervals/context;
- required caveat/check discharges;
- non-revocation;
- path coherence.

Define the transparent method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ScopedDelegationChain}
}
\]

as repeated:
- standing relation support;
- authority attenuation;
- bridge/composition;
- path provenance.

No delegation primitive is required.

# 176. Revocation — historical delegation occurrence vs current standing route

Delegation/revocation supplies another nonmonotonic-standing source.

A certificate/reference/delegation event may remain historical actuality while its present authorization route ceases to stand.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HistoricalDelegationOccurrence}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentDelegatedStanding}.
}
\]

Revocation/expiry changes the current support operator:

\[
T_t
\to
T_{t+1}
\]

while authoritative history remains append-only.

This is an exact binding of `RevisionBySupportDelta`.

## 176.1 Route-relative revocation

Let authorization claim \(\lambda\) have support environments:

\[
E_1,E_2,\ldots,E_k.
\]

Revoking one delegation edge may invalidate only those environments containing that edge.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OneRouteRevoked}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{AuthorizationWithdrawn}.
}
\]

Authorization leaves standing only when no admitted support route remains.

## 176.2 Provenance consequence

If future revocation is protected, the path cannot be compressed to a bare endpoint permission.

Exact fold requires enough provenance to reconstruct which current routes depend on which revocable authority edges.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PermissionEndpoint}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthorizationProvenance}.
}
\]

- lets a relation use require its own exact relation-targeted support environment to close.

This directly supports authority/delegation relations without a special authority-standing subsystem.

The proposed admission boundary should therefore be generic over:

\boxed{
\mathsf{SupportSubjectRef}
=
\mathsf{Claim}
\mid
\mathsf{Relation}.
}
\]

Do not build a claim-only `AdmissionKernel`.

Use:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AdmittedSupportRoute}
(
\mathsf{SupportSubjectRef},
\mathsf{SupportEnvironmentRef},
\mathsf{DischargeEvidence}
).

\]

Then pass the admitted route to the existing typed standing fixed point.

## 177.2 Current residual remains

The repository still states that current relation/claim standing links are relative to caller-declared closure inputs and do not yet evaluate opaque closure roles, actuality, relevance, or warrant.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TypedRelationStandingInfrastructure}
\neq
\mathsf{IndependentRelationAdmission}.

\]

The admission seam remains live, but its target type is now correctly generalized.

# 179. Provenance crawl — support environments have an algebraic compression

The path-sensitive delegation result selects database provenance as the next domain.

The current support representation already has two recurring operations:

1. several members jointly required inside one support environment;
2. several alternative support environments, any one of which suffices.

This has the same abstract shape as provenance multiplication/addition.

## 179.1 Source abstraction

For primitive evidence/support tokens \(x_i\):

- conjunction/joint derivation:

\[
x_1\otimes x_2;
\]

- alternative derivations:

\[
p_1\oplus p_2.
\]

A provenance polynomial/circuit records how an output was derived rather than only whether it was derivable.

Thus the support family:

\[
\{E_1,\ldots,E_n\}
\]

can, under a suitable finite monotone binding, be represented symbolically as:

\[
\boxed{
P_\lambda
=
\bigoplus_i
\bigotimes_{e\in E_i}
x_e.
}
\]

## 179.2 Strong breaker — flat lineage loses derivation structure

A flat union of all contributing inputs can identify two results that have different alternative/joint derivations.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{WhichInputsContributed}
\neq
\mathsf{HowTheResultWasDerived}.
}
\]

For support/warrant:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SupportTokenSet}
\neq
\mathsf{SupportRouteStructure}.
}
\]

This is exactly why future revocation/deletion may distinguish two currently equal endpoint results.

## 179.3 Object-free derived view

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SupportProvenanceCircuit}
}
\]

as a derived/folded representation of explicit support environments when a standing algebraic correspondence is available.

It may support:
- route invalidation;
- alternative-route survival;
- explanation;
- incremental recomputation;
- compact storage.

It is not canonical support semantics.

## 179.4 Breakers against universal provenance algebra

Do not assume every support/warrant binding is a commutative semiring.

Breakers include:
- ordered/path-sensitive derivation;
- noncommutative effects;
- linear/consumable evidence;
- higher authority coherence;
- nonmonotonic invalidation;
- provenance distinctions finer than the chosen annotation algebra.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemiringProvenance}
\neq
\mathsf{UniversalSupportSemantics}.
}
\]

The algebra is a binding-specific compression method.

## 179.5 Fixed-point return

Recursive rule/provenance systems require fixed-point treatment.

This matches the existing standing recurrence:

\[
\Stand=\mu T.
\]

A cyclic symbolic provenance representation is lawful only if its evaluation reproduces the same protected least-fixed-point result and does not permit rootless recurrence to manufacture support.

---

# 180. Support-provenance compression licence

A support-provenance fold is exact only if the compressed circuit descends every currently protected support operation.

Require:

\[
\boxed{
\Lambda_{\mathrm{prov}}
=
(
c,
\mathcal H,
\mathsf{Eval},
\mathsf{Recovery},
\mathsf{Invalidation},
\mathsf{Evidence}
).
}
\]

Exactness requires at least:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Eval}(c(\mathcal E))
=
\mathsf{StandingSupportResult}(\mathcal E)
}
\]

for the protected support family, and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Invalidate}_x(c(\mathcal E))
}
\]

must reproduce the effect of removing/defeating \(x\) from the explicit support routes whenever revocation/deletion behavior is protected.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameCurrentStanding}
\neq
\mathsf{SameSupportProvenance}
}
\]

unless future support changes are outside the horizon.

## 180.1 Machine use

`SupportProvenanceCircuit` can later become an optimization/view for:
- `Standing`;
- `AdmissionKernel`;
- authority-support bases;
- fold invalidation;
- incremental regenerative update.

Do not replace canonical `SupportEnvironmentArtifact` identity before round-trip fixtures prove exact regeneration.

# 181. Schema-evolution crawl — representation self-modification needs round-trip laws

The provenance result generates the next self-modification domain:
database/schema evolution.

Let:
- \(S_t,D_t\) be old schema/data;
- \(S_{t+1},D_{t+1}\) successor schema/data;
- \(f\) forward migration;
- \(g\) backward/reconstruction map;
- \(R\) retained complement/provenance.

## 181.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ForwardMigrationSucceeds}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{OldStateRecoverable}.
}
\]

Dropping or coarsening information can produce a valid new schema state while making exact old-state recovery impossible without retained complement/provenance.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ForwardCompatibility}
\neq
\mathsf{RegenerativeReversibility}.
}
\]

## 181.2 Bidirectional round-trip law

Where an exact bidirectional bridge exists, require binding-appropriate round-trip laws analogous to:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GetPut}
}
\qquad
\boxed{
\mathsf{PutGet}
}
\]

or their symmetric-lens equivalents.

Abstractly:

\[
\boxed{
g(f(x),R_x)
\equiv_{\mathcal H}
x
}
\]

and updates made through a supported old/new view must map back to a state that re-observes as the update just made.

The exact law is binding-specific.

## 181.3 Complement/provenance as retained residual

If \(f\) is many-to-one, exact inverse recovery may require:

\[
\boxed{
R_x
=
\text{information discarded by }f\text{ but protected for recovery}.
}
\]

This is the same regenerative fold relation already present in the calculus.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LossyForwardRepresentation}
+
\mathsf{RetainedComplement}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{RecoverableOldView}
}
\]

when the binding-native recovery theorem holds.

## 181.4 Important separators

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SchemaChange}
\neq
\mathsf{DataMigration}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BackwardCompatibleOldView}
\neq
\mathsf{ExactOldPhysicalStateRecovery}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentationMigration}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticRevision}.
}
\]

A migration that changes protected meaning cannot hide under a representation-only bridge.

---

# 182. VersionedRepresentationBridge — fold into bridge + recovery

Define the transparent macro:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{VersionedRepresentationBridge}
(V_t,V_{t+1})
}
\]

with:
- forward representation map;
- backward/recovery map;
- retained complement/provenance where needed;
- protected observer/update family;
- exact/approximate round-trip law;
- version identities;
- unlock conditions.

This macro expands to:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedCoherenceFactorization}
+
\mathsf{Recovery}
+
\mathsf{CompressionLicence}
+
\mathsf{VersionedSuccession}.
}
\]

No migration primitive is required.

## 182.1 Repository consequence

Canonical artifact/event history should never be rewritten in place merely because a schema evolves.

Prefer:

\[
\boxed{
\text{old immutable artifact}
\to
\text{versioned migration occurrence}
\to
\text{new immutable artifact}
}
\]

with both versions and the migration bridge retained.

If migration is lossy:
- preserve the required complement/provenance;
- or explicitly return `LossyMigrationGap`.

Do not silently claim regenerative recovery.

## 182.2 Migration fixtures

### MIG-001 — dropped protected field

Forward migration removes a field needed by an old protected continuation.

Without complement:

`LossyMigrationGap`.

With standing complement and inverse:

old protected view regenerates.

### MIG-002 — old API compatibility is not physical rollback

Old client view remains usable through translation.

Expected:
do not infer exact reconstruction of the old physical representation.

### MIG-003 — semantic migration escalates patch role

A migration changes a protected interpretation rather than only representation.

Expected:
route to semantic/binding/protection revision instead of representation-only bridge.

### MIG-004 — migration-chain coherence

\(V_0\to V_1\to V_2\) individually passes.

Expected:
composed round-trip/regeneration must also pass for the protected horizon.

### MIG-005 — migration producer vs migration validity

A generated migration candidate is accepted only after occurrence-local validation/checking of the required source-target relation.

# 183. Cross-domain compression after delegation / provenance / migration

The three passes now share one smaller structure:

\[
\boxed{
\text{current endpoint/result}
+
\text{derivation/version path}
+
\text{protected future invalidation/recovery operations}.
}
\]

The path may be folded only when every protected operation factors through the compressed representation.

This strengthens the sufficient-present law:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PATH INFORMATION IS RETAINED NOT BECAUSE HISTORY IS SACRED,
BUT BECAUSE FUTURE OPERATIONS MAY DISTINGUISH HISTORIES
THAT CURRENTLY PRODUCE THE SAME ENDPOINT.}
}
\]

Examples:
- revocation distinguishes authorization derivations;
- deletion distinguishes database provenance derivations;
- rollback/recovery distinguishes schema migration paths;
- compiler/checker replacement distinguishes self-hosting build paths;
- reopening distinguishes folded research derivations.

No new constitutional generator survives.

The strongest next question is now representation-level:

\boxed{
\text{Can support/provenance/version paths themselves be compressed
to a canonical minimal dependency circuit without losing
noncommutative, revocation, or recovery behavior?}
}
\]

The current answer is binding-relative:
- semiring/circuit compression works for suitable positive/monotone families;
- richer bindings require richer path algebra;
- exactness is licensed only by protected round-trip/invalidation tests.

This returns to `ProofCarryingFoldSearch`.

Status:

\boxed{
\mathsf{CERTIFIED\ LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}

for this delegation/provenance/schema-migration branch under current coverage.

# 184. Higher-order control crawl — continuation description vs continuation occurrence

**Recursion source:** `Pasted text(20260826-042925).txt`

The previous branch ended at a local fixed point over path-sensitive support/provenance/version behavior.

The next global breaker attacks:

\[
\boxed{
H1:
\text{typed open relations + answer-dependent IProg are sufficient orchestration}
}
\]

using effect handlers, delimited continuations, and one-shot/multi-shot control.

## 184.1 Frame

Separate:

- source continuation/program description \(K\);
- one captured runtime continuation occurrence \(\kappa_o\);
- captured dynamic environment/store snapshot \(\sigma_o\);
- resumption operation;
- use discipline \(U\);
- protected effect semantics.

A handler may:
- abort without resuming;
- resume once;
- resume multiple times under a multi-shot semantics;
- store the continuation for later use.

## 184.2 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ContinuationDescription}
=
\mathsf{CapturedContinuationOccurrence}.
}
\]

The same source continuation can be captured under different dynamic states.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameContinuationCode}
\neq
\mathsf{SameCapturedDynamicContext}.
}
\]

Also:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ContinuationAvailable}
\neq
\mathsf{ContinuationLawfullyReusable}.
}
\]

OCaml supplies a one-shot binding:
captured continuations are linearly consumed.

Multi-shot effect semantics supplies a different binding:
one captured continuation may be invoked several times.

The distinction belongs to the runtime/use discipline, not to open-question semantics.

## 184.3 Resume vs replay

A second breaker:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Resume}
\neq
\mathsf{Replay}.
}
\]

Resume continues a captured dynamic continuation according to the binding's continuation semantics.

Replay reconstructs a computation from retained history/inputs and may re-execute effects under a replay discipline.

They may coincide extensionally for a protected result under a particular workflow/runtime, but the derivations and effect obligations differ.

## 184.4 IProg sufficiency test

Source `IProg` already has:
- typed first-order program data;
- `Ask(q,\kappa)` answer-dependent continuation;
- reifiable program/code forms;
- explicit branching;
- binding-supplied native runtime discipline.

A multi-shot handler can therefore be compiled at the source/orchestration level into:
- explicit branch construction;
- repeated application/instantiation of reified continuation program data;
- or a CPS/effect-handler translation;

provided the binding/runtime supplies the required state-copy/snapshot/resource semantics.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MultiShotControl}
\subseteq
\mathsf{AnswerDependentIProg}
+
\mathsf{ReifiedProgramData}
+
\mathsf{BindingSuppliedContinuationUseLaw}.
}
\]

No new question former is forced.

## 184.5 Implementation boundary

The current Rust `ProgramIR` is structurally closer to one-shot suspension/resumption.

That is a valid implementation specialization.

Do not infer:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CurrentProgramIR}
=
\mathsf{FullCanonicalControlExpressivity}.
}
\]

If multi-shot control becomes protected, either:
- compile it away into explicit ordinary branching/CPS before `ProgramIR`;
- or introduce a runtime continuation-occurrence representation with explicit one-shot/multi-shot capability.

Do not add the runtime species before a fixture requires it.

# 185. Distributed causality crawl — journal order is not native causal order

The next hostile domain is distributed event ordering.

## 185.1 Frame

Separate:

- actual event occurrences \(e_i\);
- storage/journal serialization order \(<_{J}\);
- native causal/happened-before relation \(\prec_C\);
- binding-native concurrency relation;
- chosen replay/linearization order;
- global-state/snapshot cut.

## 185.2 Strong breaker

A one-writer append-only journal can impose:

\[
e_1 <_J e_2
\]

even when:

\[
e_1 \parallel_C e_2.
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{JournalOrder}
\neq
\mathsf{NativeCausalOrder}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TotalReplayOrder}
\neq
\mathsf{CausalDependency}.
}
\]

Lamport's happened-before relation is a partial order.
A total order may extend it for coordination, but the additional ordering between concurrent events is not thereby causal.

## 185.3 Consistent-present breaker

A distributed global state is not an arbitrary selection/prefix of local events.

A valid snapshot corresponds to a consistent cut: causal predecessors of included events must be appropriately represented, including in-flight communication state where required.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecordedPrefix}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticallyConsistentGlobalPresent}
}
\]

unless a standing bridge theorem relates the two.

## 185.4 Object-free relation

For event family \(E\):

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CausalPresentation}
=
(E,\prec_C,\mathsf{Concurrency},\mathsf{CutLaw}).
}
\]

A serialized ledger is one representation:

\[
\ell:E\to\mathbb N
\]

with:

\[
e_i\prec_C e_j
\Rightarrow
\ell(e_i)<\ell(e_j)
\]

when the serialization is guaranteed to respect causal order.

The converse is not generally valid.

## 185.5 Repository consequence

The current append-only one-writer event chain is a sound authoritative storage order.

Do not use its parent/index order as universal semantic causality.

When a binding protects concurrency/causal order, retain or derive explicit:
- causal predecessor edges;
- message/effect dependency edges;
- concurrency relation;
- or a binding-native proof that journal serialization is an admissible semantic linearization.

This is another application of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{StorageAncestry}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticSuccession}.
}
\]

No distributed-history primitive is forced.

---

# 186. Consistent-cut sufficient present

The distributed snapshot result refines the project notion of sufficient present.

For a linear research process, a prefix may be adequate.

For a concurrent binding, the corresponding general notion is a protectedly consistent cut/presentation:

\[
\boxed{
A_t
=
\mathsf{Fold}_{\mathcal H}
(\mathsf{ConsistentRegion}(H_{\le t})).
}
\]

The exact cut law is binding supplied.

Thus the domain-general statement becomes:

\[
\boxed{
\text{A sufficient present is a protectedly admissible
presentation of relevant history, not necessarily a scalar-time prefix.}
}
\]

The research process may remain implemented as one linear ledger without promoting linearity to universal semantics.

# 187. Garbage-collection crawl — active liveness is a least closure, not storage identity

The causal-history result generates the next storage question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Which retained material must remain active now?}
}
\]

Tracing garbage collection supplies a mature foreign method.

## 187.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Stored}
=
\mathsf{ActiveLive}.
}
\]

Also reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NotReferencedByCurrentQuestion}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SafeToForget}.
}
\]

A currently inactive item may still be required by:
- a protected future continuation;
- recovery;
- reopening;
- provenance/audit;
- regression;
- authority migration.

## 187.2 Conditional liveness

Weak references and ephemerons show that liveness is not always ordinary graph reachability.

Define protected active roots:

\[
R_{\mathcal H}
=
\{
\text{current question},
\text{current residual},
\text{protected continuations},
\text{recovery roots},
\text{unlock roots},
\text{authority roots required now}
\}.
\]

Let ordinary dependency propagation be \(D\).

Let conditional dependency rules be \(\mathcal C\).

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Live}_{\mathcal H}
=
\mu X.
\left(
R_{\mathcal H}
\cup
D[X]
\cup
\mathcal C[X]
\right).
}
\]

This is a derived active-view method.

## 187.3 Crucial semantic separation

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ActiveLive}
\neq
\mathsf{Standing}.
}
\]

A failed breaker may not stand as truth but may remain active because it is a protected regression witness.

A standing conclusion may leave active memory if it is folded and regenerable for the current horizon.

Also:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FoldedButStored}
\neq
\mathsf{ActiveLive}.
}
\]

The append-only ledger can retain ancestry while the active surface is aggressively collected.

## 187.4 Ephemeron-style cache relation

A derived cache/result \(v\) may be kept active only while its authoritative keys/dependencies \(k_1,\ldots,k_n\) remain live.

Transparent macro:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ConditionalRetention}
(k_1,\ldots,k_n;v).
}
\]

This is useful for:
- decoder caches;
- factorization caches;
- support-provenance indexes;
- compiled active views;
- method capability indexes.

It must never become the only copy of authoritative evidence.

# 188. Least-closure generator ablation

\mathsf{LeastClosureEvaluator}
(S_0,F)

under an admitted contract that establishes:
- a domain/order in which closure is defined;
- monotonicity of \(F\);
- a finite termination condition or other binding-native least-fixed-point theorem;
- explicit `Unknown/Blocked` behavior when those obligations are absent.

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mu F
}
\]

can be computed by the binding-appropriate method.

Bindings:

- active liveness:

- ephemeron liveness:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameFixedPointAlgorithm}
\neq
\mathsf{SameRelationMeaning}.

\]

So `LeastClosureEvaluator` is a reusable method/backend, not a new semantic ontology.

This folds multiple implementation methods without erasing their typed relation roles.

# 191. Nonmonotonic reasoning crawl — one least standing set is not a universal native semantics

**Recursion source:** `Pasted text(20260826-042925).txt`

The previous branch parameterized a reusable `LeastClosureEvaluator` for monotone closure problems.

The strongest next breaker is a native reasoning domain in which adding assumptions/default consequences can defeat earlier inferences and the theory may admit zero, one, or several fixed-point extensions.

Sources:
- Reiter default logic;
- answer-set/stable-model semantics;
- assumption-based truth maintenance.

## 191.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NativeConsequenceSemantics}
=
\mathsf{SingleMonotoneLeastFixedPoint}.
}
\]

A default theory can have:
- no extension;
- one extension;
- multiple mutually incompatible extensions.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OneTheory}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{OneNativeAcceptedExtension}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LeastClosureEvaluator}
\neq
\mathsf{UniversalNativeReasoningEngine}.
}
\]

This does not break the calculus's positive standing kernel.

It constrains how nonmonotonic native semantics must interface with it.

---

# 192. Reify the extension family instead of making core standing nonmonotone

Let a binding-native defeasible theory be \(T\).

Let:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Extension}_{\mathbb B}(T,E)
}
\]

be the binding-supplied relation meaning that \(E\) is an admissible native extension/model/answer set of \(T\).

The native backend may generate candidate \(E\).

A native checker may verify:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{IsExtension}_{\mathbb B}(T,E).
}
\]

Then the core calculus may admit the **positive meta-claim**:

\[
\boxed{
\text{“}E\text{ is a valid native extension of }T\text{.”}
}
\]

through ordinary actuality/checking/support/standing.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NativeNonmonotonicity}
\neq
\mathsf{CoreStandingNonmonotonicity}.
}
\]

The native semantic complexity can remain inside the binding while the core standing relation stays positive and grounded.

## 192.1 Extension-relative vs global standing

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExtensionRelativeConsequence}
\neq
\mathsf{GloballyStandingConsequence}.
}
\]

A proposition may hold in one extension and fail in another.

Therefore an unqualified globally standing consequence requires an explicit aggregation/quantification policy supplied by the inquiry question/horizon.

---

# 193. Credulous and skeptical consequence compile to existential / counterextension questions

For proposition \(p\):

## Credulous/native-possible consequence

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Credulous}_{\mathbb B}(T,p)
\iff
\exists E\;
[
\mathsf{Extension}_{\mathbb B}(T,E)
\land
p\in E
].
}
\]

This is an ordinary open role-completion question.

One checked extension containing \(p\) is a positive witness.

## Skeptical/native-forced consequence

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Skeptical}_{\mathbb B}(T,p)
\iff
\forall E\;
[
\mathsf{Extension}_{\mathbb B}(T,E)
\Rightarrow
p\in E
].
}
\]

Equivalent breaker form:

\[
\boxed{
?E[
\mathsf{Extension}_{\mathbb B}(T,E)
\land
p\notin E
].
}
\]

A found checked counterextension refutes skeptical entailment.

Failure to find one does not establish skeptical entailment unless search coverage is exhaustive or an independent certificate/theorem proves no such counterextension exists.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoCounterextensionFound}
\neq
\mathsf{NoCounterextensionExists}.
}
\]

This is exactly the existing search-failure/coverage law.

## 193.1 Answer-set-programming return

ASP systems explicitly distinguish:
- stable models;
- brave/credulous consequences;
- cautious/skeptical consequences;
- interrupted/unknown search conditions.

This domain therefore strengthens, rather than changes, the current typed status discipline.

---

# 194. ATMS return — assumption-indexed standing views

Assumption-based truth maintenance systems maintain:
- explicit justifications;
- environments of assumptions;
- minimal labels for contexts in which a node holds;
- minimal inconsistent environments (`nogoods`).

This strongly matches the existing:
- support-environment family;
- minimal support basis;
- invalidation/nogood structure;
- provenance-sensitive standing.

Define the transparent view:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AssumptionIndexedStandingView}(x)
=
\{E:
x\text{ stands under admitted assumption environment }E\}.
}
\]

This is not one global belief set.

It is a family of conditional standing results.

## 194.1 Important separation

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ConditionalStandingUnder}(E)
\neq
\mathsf{UnconditionalStanding}.
}
\]

A context-local derivation is not automatically globally promoted.

## 194.2 Machine consequence

The current repository support-environment machinery is already structurally close to an ATMS-style foundation.

Future optimization may index:
- minimal closing environments;
- minimal nogoods;
- context-conditioned standing.

But the repository must retain the canonical support identities and grounding rules rather than importing ATMS as semantic authority.

# 195. CounterextensionSearch — a smaller generator for breaker-driven inquiry

\boxed{
\mathsf{CounterextensionSearch}(P,\mathcal E)
=
?x[
x\in\mathcal E
\land
\neg P(x)

The search has typed exits:

\boxed{

This is not a new question former.

# 196. CEGAR crawl — abstract breaker is not concrete breaker

`CounterextensionSearch` is now attacked by running it over an abstraction rather than the concrete domain.

Let concrete behaviors be \(C\).

Let sound over-approximation be \(U\) with:

\[
\boxed{
C\subseteq U.
}
\]

Search:

\[
?u[
u\in U
\land
\neg P(u)
].
\]

If no abstract breaker exists and the over-approximation theorem stands:

\[
\boxed{
\neg\exists u\in U:\neg P(u)
\Rightarrow
\neg\exists c\in C:\neg P(c).
}
\]

But if an abstract breaker \(u\) exists:

\[
\boxed{
u\in U
\not\Rightarrow
u\in C.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AbstractCounterexample}
\neq
\mathsf{ConcreteBreaker}.
}
\]

A candidate abstract trace must be concretized/feasibility-checked.

## 196.1 CEGAR repair law

If \(u\) is concretely feasible:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ConcreteBreaker}.
}
\]

If \(u\) is spurious:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentationGap/PrecisionResidual}.
}
\]

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SpuriousBreaker}
\to
\mathsf{ExtractSeparator}
\to
\mathsf{RefineAbstraction}
\to
\mathsf{Repeat}.
}
\]

This is exactly a mature foreign realization of:
- `RefineFoldByQuestion`;
- frame/probe co-design;
- residual-driven representation extension;
- breaker ladder possible/constructible/realizable/actual.

No CEGAR primitive is needed.

# 197. Dual under-approximation — failure there is not proof

Now let:

\[
\boxed{
L\subseteq C
}
\]

be a sound under-approximation of concrete behaviors.

Search:

\[
?\ell[
\ell\in L
\land
\neg P(\ell)
].
\]

If a breaker is found:

\[
\boxed{
\ell\in L
\Rightarrow
\ell\in C,
}
\]

so a checked \(\ell\) is a concrete breaker.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\neg\exists \ell\in L:\neg P(\ell)
\not\Rightarrow
\neg\exists c\in C:\neg P(c).
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{UnderApproximateSearchFailure}
=
\mathsf{UnknownOutsideCoverage}.
}
\]

Proof-guided under-approximation widening provides a native repair method:
use local proofs/results to expand the under-approximation rather than turning failed search into global absence.

L_t
\subseteq
C

u\in U_t,\;\neg P(u)

must be checked for concreteness.

refine \(U_t\).

## Lower-side ambiguous exit

No breaker in \(L_t\):

widen \(L_t\).

Define the transparent method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BidirectionalApproximationInquiry}.
}
\]

Recurrence:

\[
\boxed{
(L_t,U_t)
\to
\begin{cases}

\mathsf{WidenLower},\\

\end{cases}

\]

This is a stronger reusable search strategy than either pure testing or pure over-approximate proof.

# 199. Exact fold vs oriented approximation

The approximation crawl forces a new protected distinction in compression contracts:

\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactFold}
\neq
\mathsf{SoundOverApproximation}
\neq

An exact fold supports round-trip protected equivalence.

An over-approximation safely supports some universal/proof directions while possibly introducing spurious alternatives.

An under-approximation safely supports some existential/witness directions while omitting possible alternatives.

Therefore approximate compression must carry **orientation**:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ApproximationContract}
=
(
\mathsf{Direction},
\mathsf{ProtectedQueryFamily},
\mathsf{SoundnessLaw},
\mathsf{Coverage},
\mathsf{Residual}
).

# 204. Quantum-instrument crawl — a probe is both return-producing and state-transforming

The previous branch reduced inquiry to typed candidate generation, checking,
directional coverage, standing, and refinement.

The next hostile question is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{What if asking/checking itself changes the object being queried?}
}
\]

Quantum measurement supplies a binding in which this is not an implementation
accident but part of the native semantics.

## 204.1 Frame

Let:

- \(x\) be the pre-probe state;
- \(u\) an actualizable probe/measurement operation;
- \(r\) the returned outcome;
- \(x'\) the post-probe state;
- \(C\) the measurement/context arrangement.

The relevant native relation has the shape:

\[
\boxed{
x
\xrightarrow{u/r}
x'.
}
\]

Equivalently:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Instrument}_u:
X
\rightsquigarrow
R_u\times X.
}
\]

The exact mathematical structure is binding-supplied.

## 204.2 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Probe}
=
\mathsf{PassiveRead}.
}
\]

A probe can simultaneously:
- return information;
- change the source state;
- alter which later probes are possible or consequential.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProbeOutcome}
\neq
\mathsf{ProbeStateEffect}.
}
\]

Two probe implementations may induce the same immediate outcome statistics while
having different post-probe state transitions.

Hence:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameImmediateAnswerProfile}
\neq
\mathsf{SameProbeSemantics}
}
\]

whenever later continuations can inspect the changed state.

This is an especially strong breaker against answer-only compression.

## 204.3 Repository actuality return

The current Rust `ActualEvent` already structurally contains:

- `state_before`;
- `operator`;
- `raw_return`;
- `state_after`;
- context/boundary/binding/provenance references.

Thus the repository already has the correct event *shape* for a
state-transforming probe.

No new event primitive is forced.

What remains binding-specific is checking the semantic transition relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProbeEffect}_{\mathbb B}
(u,x,r,x').
}
\]

A stored before/after pair proves identity linkage only until a standing
binding/backend/checker route establishes that \(x'\) is the lawful post-probe
state of this actual occurrence.

## 204.4 New implementation boundary

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EventContainsStateAfter}
\neq
\mathsf{StateTransitionSemanticsValidated}.
}
\]

Future actuality admission may therefore include a binding-native support/check
route for:

\[
\mathsf{ProbeEffect}_{\mathbb B}
(
\mathsf{operator},
\mathsf{stateBefore},
\mathsf{rawReturn},
\mathsf{stateAfter}
).
\]

This remains an ordinary relation subject.

# 205. Quantum contextuality — local standing need not globalize

Kochen–Specker/contextuality attacks a stronger frame assumption:

\[
\boxed{
\text{locally admissible answers across contexts}
\Rightarrow
\text{one context-independent global assignment}.
}
\]

This implication fails in contextual measurement scenarios.

## 205.1 Context family

Let:

\[
\mathcal C=\{C_i\}
\]

be compatible/co-measurable contexts.

A local assignment is:

\[
s_i\in\mathsf{Assignments}(C_i).
\]

Local compatibility on overlaps does not universally imply that there exists:

\[
g\in\mathsf{Assignments}\Big(\bigcup_iC_i\Big)
\]

such that:

\[
g|_{C_i}=s_i
\]

for all \(i\), once the empirical/native admissibility constraints are imposed.

Define the open globalization question:

\[
\boxed{
?g[
\forall i\;
\mathsf{RestrictsTo}(g,C_i,s_i)
\land
\mathsf{GloballyAdmissible}_{\mathbb B}(g)
].
}
\]

## 205.2 Residual

If the local family is standing but no lawful global filling exists:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GlobalizationObstruction}.
}
\]

Do not return:
- contradiction among the local sections;
- one arbitrarily selected global frame;
- a fabricated joint assignment.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LocallyStandingContextFamily}
\neq
\mathsf{GloballyJointlyRepresentableAssignment}.
}
\]

This strengthens the retained:

\[
\mathsf{LocalCompatibility}
\neq
\mathsf{GlobalCompletion}.
\]

## 205.3 Compression result

No contextuality primitive is required.

The structure is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FrameFamily}
+
\mathsf{OverlapRestriction}
+
\mathsf{ConstraintCompletion}
+
\mathsf{GlobalizationBreaker}.
}
\]

`GlobalizationObstruction` is a typed residual of an ordinary open completion
question.

# 206. No-cloning / incompatibility — represented branching is not forkable actuality

The strongest experimental branch breaker comes from noncopyable and
measurement-disturbed states.

## 206.1 False identification

An inquiry program may represent:

\[
\boxed{
u_1
\quad\text{or}\quad
u_2
}
\]

as alternative future probes.

This does not imply one actual source occurrence can be copied so that both probes
are physically realized from exactly the same source state.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentedAlternativeBranching}
\neq
\mathsf{ForkableActuality}.
}
\]

Quantum no-cloning supplies an exact source binding in which arbitrary unknown
state copying is unavailable.

More generally, resource-sensitive or destructive-test domains can impose the
same separation without quantum semantics.

## 206.2 Counterfactual experimental comparison

To lawfully compare two probes from a "same" source condition, require a
binding-supplied relation such as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ReprepareEquivalent}_{\mathcal H}(x,x_1,x_2)
}
\]

or another native replication/reset theorem.

Without such a route:

\[
\boxed{
\text{counterfactual branch in the model}
\not\Rightarrow
\text{jointly actualizable experiment}.
}
\]

## 206.3 Probe order

For state-transforming/incompatible probes:

\[
u_2\circ u_1
\]

and:

\[
u_1\circ u_2
\]

need not have the same protected consequence.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameProbeSet}
\neq
\mathsf{SameProbeSequence}.
}
\]

This is ordinary composition/order sensitivity.

## 206.4 Network return

The previously retained use/resource discipline now extends uniformly over:

- returned values;
- captured continuations;
- physical/query source states;
- experimental opportunities.

General surviving relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RepresentedAvailability}
\neq
\mathsf{LawfulActualUseMultiplicity}.
}
\]

Multiplicity/reuse is binding supplied.

No linearity/no-cloning primitive is promoted.

# 207. Partial-observation crawl — unobservable does not mean irrelevant

The quantum branch creates the next question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{What state should an inquiry policy retain when the true source state is
not directly observable?}
}
\]

Imperfect-information games and POMDPs supply mature bindings.

## 207.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CurrentObservation}
=
\mathsf{DecisionSufficientState}.
}
\]

Two histories can yield the same current observation while supporting different
beliefs/information sets and therefore different future consequences.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameCurrentObservation}
\neq
\mathsf{SameInformationState}.
}
\]

Also:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{UnobservableDistinction}
\neq
\mathsf{IrrelevantDistinction}.
}
\]

A hidden distinction may be unavailable as an action discriminator while still
changing outcome probabilities or adversarial possibilities.

## 207.2 Strategy uniformity

In an imperfect-information game, a lawful observation-based strategy must act
uniformly on histories that the agent cannot distinguish.

Let:

\[
h_1\approx_{\mathrm{obs}} h_2.
\]

Then an admitted strategy may require:

\[
\boxed{
\sigma(h_1)=\sigma(h_2).
}
\]

This is a binding-native strategy applicability condition.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HiddenStateDifference}
\neq
\mathsf{LawfullyActionableDifference}.
}
\]

## 207.3 POMDP return

Under the POMDP assumptions, a belief state:

\[
b_t
=
P(S_t\mid\text{observation/action history})
\]

is a sufficient statistic for the history relevant to future optimal control.

This yields a concrete source for regenerative compression:

\[
\boxed{
\text{full history}
\to
\text{belief/information state}
\to
\text{future policy}.
}
\]

But do not universalize probability or the POMDP theorem.

Other bindings may use:
- knowledge sets;
- observation histories;
- finite-memory controller states;
- symbolic constraints;
- other sufficient representations.

# 208. Protected discriminator signature — exact factorization criterion

The partial-observation pass answers one of the long-standing high-leverage
questions.

Let \(H\) be the represented history/arrangement space and
\(\mathcal K\) the currently protected continuation/policy family.

A candidate signature:

\[
\chi:H\to Z
\]

is protectedly sufficient iff every protected continuation factors through it:

\[
\boxed{
\forall K\in\mathcal K\;
\exists \bar K
\quad
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(K(h))
=
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(\bar K(\chi(h)))
}
\]

for all covered \(h\).

Therefore complete answer-profile equality is not required independently.

The exact required discriminator is:

\[
\boxed{
h_1\equiv_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H} h_2
\iff
\forall K\in\mathcal K,\;
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(K(h_1))
=
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(K(h_2)).
}
\]

The coarsest exact signature is the quotient map:

\[
\boxed{
q_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}:
H
\to
H/{\equiv_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}}.
}
\]

This is the current contextual quotient specialized to the protected continuation
family.

## 208.1 Minimality

A signature \(\chi\) is minimal/coarsest exact when:

\[
\boxed{
\chi(h_1)=\chi(h_2)
\iff
h_1\equiv_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}h_2
}
\]

up to representation renaming/isomorphism.

If only one implication holds, \(\chi\) may be a refinement or an unsafe
coarsening.

## 208.2 Answer to high-leverage question A

The smallest protected discriminator signature does **not** require complete
equality of every conceivable answer profile.

It requires equality only under the declared protected continuation family.

If that family later expands, the quotient may reopen.

This is exactly:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{QuestionConditionedReopening}.
}
\]

# 209. Automata crawl — exact condition for a finite sufficient present

The factorization criterion is now rebound to automata theory.

For a language \(L\), Myhill–Nerode identifies histories/prefixes \(x,y\) when:

\[
\boxed{
x\equiv_L y
\iff
\forall z,\;
xz\in L
\Leftrightarrow
yz\in L.
}
\]

These are precisely histories indistinguishable by every future continuation
suffix.

The theorem states:

\[
\boxed{
L\text{ has a finite DFA}
\iff
\equiv_L\text{ has finite index}.
}
\]

When finite, the equivalence classes form the unique minimal DFA state space.

This is an exact foreign realization of the project sufficient-present quotient.

## 209.1 Generalized sufficient-present criterion

For current protected continuation family \(\mathcal K\), define:

\[
N_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}
=
\left|
H/{\equiv_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}}
\right|.
\]

If:

\[
\boxed{
N_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}<\infty
}
\]

and successor operations are congruent/well-defined on the quotient, then an exact
finite-state protected sufficient present exists:

\[
\boxed{
S_{\min}
=
H/{\equiv_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}}.
}
\]

Any exact finite-state presentation must have at least that many protectedly
distinct states/classes.

## 209.2 Breaker

If the continuation equivalence has infinite index:

\[
\boxed{
N_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}=\infty,
}
\]

then there is no exact finite-*state* quotient preserving all those continuations.

Therefore reject the universal hope:

\[
\boxed{
\text{every growing inquiry admits a bounded finite-state sufficient present}.
}
\]

Possible lawful responses are:

1. retain growing distinctions;
2. use a richer symbolic state that can denote infinitely many quotient classes;
3. change grain/horizon/protected continuation family;
4. use an explicit approximation contract.

## 209.3 Important non-identification

Infinite index does not imply no compact symbolic implementation.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FiniteIndex}
\neq
\mathsf{FiniteDescriptionComplexity}.
}
\]

For example, an unbounded integer-valued symbolic state may represent infinitely
many exact equivalence classes with a finite program/schema.

So the theorem controls finite-state compression, not every form of compact
representation.

# 210. Continuation-quotient minimization method

The automata result gives a mature method for exact regenerative compression.

Define the transparent method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ContinuationQuotientMinimize}.
}
\]

Inputs:
- candidate presentation/history states;
- protected continuation/test family;
- native successor/update relations;
- comparison checker;
- coverage.

Program:

1. begin with a coarse candidate partition;
2. search for a continuation that separates two currently identified states;
3. split the class on the separator;
4. repeat until stable under protected continuations and successor operations;
5. certify quotient factorization;
6. retain separator provenance and reopening condition.

This is a generalization of DFA partition refinement and an implementation of:

\[
\mathsf{RefineFoldByQuestion}.
\]

It is not a new semantic primitive.

## 210.1 Relationship to earlier methods

The following now become one coherent family:

- separator-basis extension;
- protected discriminator signature;
- contextual quotient;
- DFA minimization;
- CEGAR representation refinement;
- sufficient-present folding.

They share:

\[
\boxed{
\text{identify}
\to
\text{find distinguishing continuation}
\to
\text{split}
\to
\text{repeat}
\to
\text{factor}.
}
\]

CEGAR additionally permits oriented approximate presentations.
Myhill–Nerode provides an exact continuation quotient in its native binding.

# 213. Blackwell comparison crawl — protected continuation sufficiency is a representation order

The previous pass identified the coarsest exact quotient relative to a protected
continuation family \(\mathcal K\).

The next hostile question is whether this relation is merely an automata-specific
construction or a domain-general information order.

Statistical decision theory supplies the foreign domain.

## 213.1 Frame

Let:

- \(X\) be a richer experiment/representation;
- \(Y\) be a candidate compressed experiment/representation;
- \(\Theta\) the binding-native source/world parameter;
- \(\mathcal D\) the protected family of downstream decision problems;
- \(K\) a downstream decision/continuation;
- \(\mathsf{Con}\) the protected consequence/risk/payoff comparison.

The exact quantitative order is binding supplied.

## 213.2 Protected simulation order

Define the derived representation order:

\[
\boxed{
X \succeq_{\mathcal D} Y
}
\]

iff every protected downstream use available from \(Y\) can be matched from \(X\)
without worsening the declared protected consequence.

In exact observational form:

\[
\boxed{
\forall K_Y\in\mathcal D\;
\exists K_X
\quad
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(K_X(X))
=
\mathsf{Obs}_{\mathcal H}(K_Y(Y)).
}
\]

In a statistical-decision binding the comparison may instead be native risk/value
dominance.

The calculus does not impose the scalar comparison.

## 213.3 Blackwell return

For the classical finite statistical-experiment binding, Blackwell's comparison
theorem characterizes one experiment as at least as informative as another when
the poorer experiment is obtainable from the richer one by a stochastic
post-processing/garbling; equivalently, the richer experiment is no worse for every
decision problem in the theorem's scope.

Therefore Blackwell informativeness is a native realization of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedSimulationOrder}.
}
\]

Exact information equivalence corresponds to mutual dominance/simulability under
the declared decision family.

## 213.4 Strong breaker

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EqualPerformanceOnCurrentTasks}
\neq
\mathsf{UniversalDecisionEquivalence}.
}
\]

Two representations can agree on every currently tested decision while some
unprotected decision problem distinguishes them.

Thus current-task success does not establish all-decision sufficiency.

# 214. Le Cam deficiency return — approximate sufficiency is decision-family-relative

The Blackwell order is exact/qualitative.

Comparison-of-experiments theory also supplies an approximate relation:
deficiency measures how well one experiment can simulate another in decision
terms, and the comparison can be restricted to a specified class of decision
problems.

This gives a mature binding for the project's directional approximation contract.

## 214.1 Protected approximate simulation

Define a binding-native distortion:

\[
\boxed{
\delta_{\mathcal D}(X,Y)
}
\]

meaning the worst declared decision loss when \(X\) is used to simulate/replace
\(Y\), according to the native theorem.

Do not universalize a metric.

The general calculus retains only:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ApproxSim}_{\mathcal D,\varepsilon}(X,Y)
}
\]

with:
- direction;
- protected decision/continuation family;
- binding-native soundness theorem;
- declared distortion bound;
- coverage;
- residual.

## 214.2 Repository return

The current Rust `CompressionLicense` already distinguishes:

\[
\mathsf{Exact}
\quad\text{vs}\quad
\mathsf{Approximate}(\mathsf{DistortionContractRef}),
\]

and retains:
- protected continuations;
- horizon;
- evidence;
- residual;
- recovery contract;
- unlock conditions.

Therefore the Blackwell/Le-Cam crawl does not force a compression artifact schema
change.

It does force a checker-level obligation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DistortionContractRef}
\text{ must state the approximation orientation and protected query/decision family.}
}
\]

A generic "approximate" label without inferential direction remains insufficient.

# 215. Predictive-state crawl — stochastic continuation quotient

The deterministic Myhill--Nerode result is now rebound to controlled stochastic
systems.

Predictive State Representations (PSRs) represent state through predictions of
future observable tests conditioned on action/history rather than requiring a
privileged latent-state ontology.

## 215.1 Future-test signature

Let \(\mathcal T\) be a protected family of action-conditional future tests.

Define the predictive signature:

\[
\boxed{
\chi_{\mathcal T}(h)
=
\left(
P(\tau\mid h)
\right)_{\tau\in\mathcal T}.
}
\]

The exact representation form is binding supplied.

Histories may be folded when:

\[
\boxed{
h_1\equiv_{\mathcal T}h_2
\iff
\forall \tau\in\mathcal T,\;
P(\tau\mid h_1)=P(\tau\mid h_2).
}
\]

This is a stochastic continuation quotient.

## 215.2 Strong breaker

Reject:

\boxed{
\mathsf{LatentStateIdentity}
=
\mathsf{PredictiveStateSufficiency}.

A useful/sufficient state representation can be grounded in future observable
predictions without asserting one privileged hidden ontology.

Conversely two latent descriptions may differ while remaining predictively
equivalent at the protected horizon.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Ontic/LatentModelIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{ProtectedPredictiveEquivalence}.
}
\]

## 215.3 Network compression

The following now belong to one family:

- Myhill--Nerode future-suffix equivalence;
- POMDP belief-state sufficiency;
- PSR predictive state;
- protected discriminator signature;
- contextual quotient.

The common relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{history representations are equal exactly to the extent
the protected future-test family cannot distinguish them.}
}
\]

Probability remains binding supplied.

# 216. Open-ended continual learning — current sufficiency is not future-task sufficiency

The strongest live residual is now:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal K_t
\to
\mathcal K_{t+1}.
}
\]

A fold \(c_t\) may be exact for every currently protected continuation:

\[
\forall K\in\mathcal K_t,\quad
K=\bar K\circ c_t,
\]

while a newly protected continuation \(K^\star\) fails to factor:

\[
\boxed{
K^\star\not=\bar K^\star\circ c_t.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CurrentTaskSufficiency}
\neq
\mathsf{FutureTaskSufficiency}.
}
\]

## 216.1 Exact reopening condition

If \(K^\star\) separates:

\[
c_t(h_1)=c_t(h_2)
\]

but:

\[
\mathsf{Obs}(K^\star(h_1))
\neq
\mathsf{Obs}(K^\star(h_2)),
\]

the old fold must reopen.

Successful exact repair requires at least one of:

1. retained ancestry containing a supported separator/recovery path;
2. lawful reacquisition/re-experimentation;
3. a previously declared upper envelope
   \(\overline{\mathcal K}\supseteq\mathcal K_{t+1}\)
   against which the original fold was already certified.

Without these:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FutureTaskRecoveryGap}.
}
\]

## 216.2 Strong breaker against universal bounded memory

In one formal continual-learning framework, optimal continual learning is shown to
generally require perfect memory and solve an NP-hard problem.

Use this only as a source-domain impossibility witness, not as a universal theorem
about every learning architecture.

It is sufficient to reject the unconditional claim:

\[
\boxed{
\text{arbitrary open-ended exact learning always admits a bounded-memory
optimal implementation}.
}
\]

## 216.3 Governing law

A bounded active present is compatible with open-ended learning only when the
information needed for future refinements is either:

\[
\boxed{
\text{already protected in the active quotient}
}
\]

or:

\[
\boxed{
\text{regeneratively reachable from retained ancestry/reacquisition}.
}
\]

This sharpens H5/H6 rather than breaking them.

# 217. Complementary learning systems — active semantic fold plus episodic recovery store

The continual-learning residual selects a cognition/neuroscience domain with a
mature architecture for the stability/plasticity tension.

Complementary Learning Systems (CLS) is a scientific model/theory, not a
mathematical necessity theorem.

Its useful structural distinction is:

- fast, sparse/pattern-separated storage of specific episodes;
- slow, distributed/overlapping integration of regularities;
- replay/interleaving from episodic storage into the integrative system.

## 217.1 Object-free return

The portable architecture is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EpisodeStore}
+
\mathsf{SemanticFold}
+
\mathsf{Replay/InterleavingBridge}.
}
\]

This maps directly to:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AppendOnlyRegenerativeAncestry}
+
\mathsf{EconomicalActivePresent}
+
\mathsf{QuestionConditionedReplay/Reopening}.
}
\]

## 217.2 Protected distinctions

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticSummary}
\neq
\mathsf{EpisodeRecoverability}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Replay}
\neq
\mathsf{OriginalActuality}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{IntegratedGeneralization}
\neq
\mathsf{LiteralEpisodeRetention}.
}
\]

A replayed/reconstructed trace is a new occurrence derived from ancestry; it does
not become the original event.

## 217.3 Machine consequence

The research architecture should not force one store to optimize simultaneously
for:
- exact episodic recovery;
- maximal semantic compression;
- rapid incorporation of new information;
- minimal interference.

The current ledger + derived active network already forms the right two-level
architecture.

No neuroscience primitive is imported.

If future implementation uses generated/pseudo-replay rather than exact retained
episodes, that is an approximate recovery/rehearsal method unless a standing
theorem/check establishes exact protected equivalence.

Do not label generated rehearsal as exact ancestry recovery by default.

# 218. Developmental representational redescription — new accessibility is not new evidence

The open-ended continuation-family problem is now attacked from developmental
cognition.

Representational Redescription (RR) is a developmental theory/model according to
which knowledge initially embedded in relatively implicit/procedural
representations may later be re-represented in more accessible and manipulable
formats.

Use this as a source of a relational distinction, not as a universal cognitive law.

## 218.1 Strong breaker

A system may already perform task \(T\) successfully using representation \(R_I\),
while another operation \(M\) cannot inspect/manipulate the internal components
needed for explicit reasoning about that performance.

After a representation transform:

\[
R_I
\xrightarrow{\rho}
R_E,
\]

the original performance may remain while new operations become possible.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BehavioralCompetence}
\neq
\mathsf{RepresentationalAccessibility}.
}
\]

And:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NewAccessibleRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{NewExternalEvidence}.
}
\]

The representation transformation can expose structure already latent in prior
actuality.

## 218.2 Inquiry-system return

Reification and recursive form closure already permit:

\[
\boxed{
\text{relation/process}
\to
\text{represented form}
\to
\text{new relation over that form}.
}
\]

So representational redescription compiles to:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Reify}
+
\mathsf{Rebind/Reexpress}
+
\mathsf{NewContinuationAccessibility}.
}
\]

The resulting claims still require ordinary support/warrant.

A new representation can create a new **questionable relation** without creating
new evidence for its answer.

## 218.3 New reopening condition

A fold that preserved only external task performance may become too coarse when a
new protected continuation asks about:
- internal decomposition;
- explanation;
- deliberate modification;
- cross-domain transfer;
- metacognitive/reportable structure.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PerformanceEquivalent}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{MetarepresentationallyEquivalent}.
}
\]

This is another exact instance of protected continuation expansion.

---

# 219. Representation-order ablation — Blackwell, Myhill–Nerode, PSR, and compression share one parent

The preceding domains support a network contraction.

Define the derived preorder:

\[
\boxed{
A\succeq_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}B
}
\]

when every protected continuation/use available from \(B\) can be simulated from
\(A\) with the same protected consequence (or the binding-native no-worse
criterion).

Then:

\[
\boxed{
A\simeq_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}B
\iff
A\succeq_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}B
\land
B\succeq_{\mathcal K,\mathcal H}A.
}
\]

This is not a new constitutional relation.
It is a derived order over presentations generated by existing typed
factorization/equivalence machinery.

## 219.1 Native realizations

- Blackwell order:
  stochastic decision-theoretic simulability.

- Myhill--Nerode:
  exact deterministic equivalence under all suffix continuations.

- POMDP/PSR:
  action/observation predictive sufficiency.

- exact compression:
  mutual protected simulability between source and folded presentation.

- directional approximation:
  one-way simulability under a distortion/soundness contract.

- bridge preservation:
  a cross-binding path is admitted when the declared protected uses commute.

## 219.2 Strong non-identification

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SamePresentationOrder}
\neq
\mathsf{SameNativeSemanticLaw}.
}
\]

Blackwell garbling, DFA quotient, belief-state update, and schema migration are not
the same mathematics internally.

They instantiate the same higher-level protected-simulation question.

This is method/network compression, not domain erasure.

---

# 220. Open-ended sufficient-present theorem schema

The current sufficient-present aspiration can now be stated with an exact
conditional form.

Let:

- \(H_t\) be history/ancestry;
- \(\mathcal K_t\) current protected continuations;
- \(c_t:H_t\to Z_t\) current active fold.

A currently exact present requires:

\[
\boxed{
\forall K\in\mathcal K_t,\quad
K=\bar K\circ c_t
}
\]

at the declared horizon.

For an open-ended future continuation family, exact regenerative sufficiency
requires:

\[
\boxed{
\forall K^\star\text{ later protected},
\quad
K^\star
\text{ either factors through }c_t
\text{ or can trigger supported recovery/refinement.}
}
\]

Therefore a bounded active representation does **not** require that all future
distinctions already be active.

It requires that every later protected distinction be either:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AlreadyRepresented}
\quad\text{or}\quad
\mathsf{RegenerativelyRecoverable}.
}
\]

If neither holds:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SufficientPresentFailure}.
}
\]

This is the strongest current formulation of H5/H6.

---

# 221. Repository implementation consequences

The current repository's `CompressionLicense` already names:
- horizon;
- protected continuations;
- exact vs directional approximate kind;
- distortion contract;
- evidence;
- residual;
- recovery;
- unlock conditions.

This is structurally aligned with the new representation-order result.

No core artifact schema change is forced by this crawl.

## 221.1 Required semantic checks

Future compression/admission evaluation should establish:

```text
ProtectedSimulation(source, folded, continuations, horizon)
RecoverySufficiency(folded, recovery, unlock_conditions)
ApproximationOrientation(distortion_contract)
```

as ordinary checked relations.

## 221.2 Fixtures

### COMP-DEC-001 — current-task equality is not universal sufficiency

Two presentations produce equal results for every current protected continuation.

Add a new decision/continuation that separates them.

Expected:
the prior fold reopens.

### COMP-DEC-002 — one-way informativeness is not equivalence

Presentation \(A\) can simulate every protected use of \(B\), but not conversely.

Expected:
record directional relation;
do not admit exact equivalence.

### COMP-DEC-003 — approximate loss is query-family-relative

An approximation stays within its bound for protected family \(\mathcal D_1\) but
not \(\mathcal D_2\).

Expected:
the distortion contract cannot be reused outside its declared family.

### PSR-001 — stochastic continuation equivalence

Two histories induce identical distributions for every declared future test.

Expected:
they may fold for that predictive horizon.

Add a distinguishing test.

Expected:
reopen and split.

### LEARN-001 — future continuation unlock

A fold licensed under \(\mathcal K_t\) is followed by new protected
\(K^\star\).

Expected:
factor if possible; otherwise recover ancestry and refine.

### LEARN-002 — unrecoverable future distinction

The new continuation distinguishes forms that were destructively merged and no
ancestry/reacquisition route survives.

Expected:

`FutureTaskRecoveryGap` / `SufficientPresentFailure`.

### MEM-001 — semantic summary vs episodic ancestry

Delete active detailed episodes while preserving authoritative ancestry and exact
recovery.

Expected:
active semantic fold survives.

Delete the only recovery ancestry.

Expected:
the same compression is no longer licensed for horizons requiring episodic
reopening.

### MEM-002 — generated replay is not original actuality

A generative replay backend emits a plausible old episode.

Expected:
new replay occurrence is preserved as generated/derived;
it cannot stand in for the historical actual event without an independent exact
bridge.

### DEV-001 — performance equivalence vs accessibility

Two representations solve the same external task.

Only one permits a newly protected internal/explanatory continuation.

Expected:
the representations are not folded under the expanded horizon.
