# 222. Compression checkpoint

New protected distinctions:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{CurrentTaskSufficiency}
\neq
\mathsf{FutureTaskSufficiency},\\
&\mathsf{EqualCurrentPerformance}
\neq
\mathsf{UniversalDecisionEquivalence},\\
&\mathsf{LatentStateIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{PredictiveStateSufficiency},\\
&\mathsf{SemanticSummary}
\neq
\mathsf{EpisodeRecoverability},\\
&\mathsf{Replay}
\neq
\mathsf{OriginalActuality},\\
&\mathsf{BehavioralCompetence}
\neq
\mathsf{RepresentationalAccessibility},\\
&\mathsf{NewAccessibleRelation}
\neq
\mathsf{NewExternalEvidence}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Network contraction:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedSimulationOrder}
}
\]

is the derived parent relation over presentations.

`ProtectedDiscriminatorSignature`,
`ContinuationQuotientMinimize`,
Blackwell comparison,
PSR state,
and exact/approximate fold tests become native realizations/refinements of this
presentation-order/factorization structure.

No new M-pattern or constitutional primitive is required.

## Current residual

The strongest remaining question is now:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Can future continuation-family expansion itself be generated,
bounded, or certified strongly enough to avoid retaining recoverable ancestry?}
}
\]

No current domain supports a universal positive answer.

Continual-learning lower bounds supply a counterexample to an unconditional
bounded-memory claim.

Developmental redescription supplies a source in which new accessible operations
emerge over old represented content.

Therefore the current lawful status is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CERTIFIED\ LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}
}
\]

with the explicit global boundary:

\[
\boxed{
\text{open-ended exact future sufficiency requires either
a standing future-use envelope or regenerative ancestry/reacquisition.}
}
\]


---

# 223. Erasure crawl — recoverability itself can be a protected failure

The previous branch established:

\[
\boxed{
\text{open-ended future sufficiency requires either a standing future-use envelope
or regenerative ancestry/reacquisition.}
}
\]

The strongest next breaker is a domain in which preserving a regeneration route is
itself disallowed.

Source domains:
- cryptographic erase / media sanitization;
- differential privacy;
- machine unlearning;
- legal erasure/restriction;
- side-channel security.

## 223.1 Strongest false identification

Reject the unconditional storage preference:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FoldedButRegenerable}
\succ
\mathsf{Nonrecoverable}.
}
\]

There are bindings/horizons in which the protected requirement is precisely:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecoveryInfeasible}_{\mathcal A,\epsilon}(x),
}
\]

relative to a declared attacker/resource/effort model \((\mathcal A,\epsilon)\).

NIST cryptographic erase supplies a concrete binding:
sanitizing the media-encryption key is intended to render decrypted target data
recovery infeasible under the declared sanitization assurance model.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RegenerativeCapability}
\neq
\mathsf{UnconditionallyDesirableCapability}.
}
\]

## 223.2 Lost vs erased

Two histories may have the same present fact:

\[
\neg\mathsf{Recoverable}(x),
\]

but differ radically in provenance:

- one lost \(x\) accidentally;
- one intentionally discharged a standing erasure obligation.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Lost}
\neq
\mathsf{ErasedByStandingObligation}.
}
\]

This is another path-sensitive same-endpoint distinction.

No new state primitive is required.

Represent instead the standing relations that establish:
- deletion/erasure obligation;
- erase occurrence;
- erase checker result;
- attacker/effort coverage;
- remaining permitted metadata/provenance.

---

# 224. Differential-privacy crawl — greater information can be a protected regression

The preceding `ProtectedSimulationOrder` was oriented around preserving downstream
uses.

Privacy supplies the strongest counterorientation.

## 224.1 Strong breaker

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MoreInformativePresentation}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{ProtectedlyBetterPresentation}.
}
\]

When confidentiality/privacy is protected, extra recoverable distinctions may be
harmful.

Differential privacy is a native mathematical source:
a private release deliberately limits dependence on individual records, while
post-processing preserves the privacy guarantee for arbitrary downstream
computation of the released result.

Thus a representation can be intentionally **less** informative while being better
under the full protected horizon.

## 224.2 Protected-use boundary

Replace a one-axis "more simulation capability is better" reading with:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedUseBoundary}(X)
=
\langle
\mathcal K^{+},
\mathcal K^{-},
\mathcal K^{?},
\mathcal A,
\mathcal H
\rangle
}
\]

where:

- \(\mathcal K^{+}\): required/permitted protected uses that must remain available;
- \(\mathcal K^{-}\): forbidden uses/recoveries that must remain unavailable or
  bounded by a binding-native leakage contract;
- \(\mathcal K^{?}\): conditionally permitted uses indexed by authority/purpose;
- \(\mathcal A\): authorization/attacker context;
- \(\mathcal H\): protected horizon.

This is a derived contract/view over ordinary typed relations and consequences.

## 224.3 Revised presentation order

Define:

\[
A \succeq_{\mathcal B} B
\]

only relative to a full protected boundary \(\mathcal B\), not raw informational
richness.

A lawful successor presentation must:
- preserve all required uses;
- not newly enable forbidden uses beyond declared leakage bounds;
- preserve conditional-use authority constraints.

Therefore Blackwell informativeness remains a correct native order for its
decision-theoretic objective, but it is not a universal goodness order once privacy
is an additional protected axis.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{InformationalDominance}
\neq
\mathsf{ProtectedMultiAxisDominance}.
}
\]

---

# 225. Law crawl — storage, access, processing, recovery, and erasure are different capabilities

Privacy law supplies a mature institutional domain where erasure and retention
obligations coexist.

GDPR Article 17 provides erasure rights/obligations while also recognizing scoped
exceptions including legal obligations, public-interest archiving/research, and
legal claims.

Article 18 separately permits restricted processing states where storage may
continue while ordinary processing is constrained.

## 225.1 Strong breaker against enum-style memory state

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MemoryDisposition}
=
\{\mathsf{Keep},\mathsf{Delete}\}.
}
\]

A form may be:
- storable but not ordinarily processable;
- recoverable only under a specific authority/purpose;
- disclosable to one role but not another;
- required to be erased after a condition;
- retained for one legal purpose while prohibited for another.

The smaller abstraction is capability-indexed.

## 225.2 Object-free relation

For retained form \(x\), purpose \(p\), actor/authority \(a\), operation \(o\):

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Permitted}(a,o,p,x)
}
\]

with operations such as:

\[
o\in
\{
\mathsf{Store},
\mathsf{Recover},
\mathsf{Inspect},
\mathsf{Process},
\mathsf{Disclose},
\mathsf{Delete},
\mathsf{Reacquire}
\}.
\]

The binding supplies:
- authority semantics;
- conflict/priority law;
- temporal conditions;
- exceptions;
- scope.

No legal ontology is imported into the calculus.

## 225.3 Revised storage law

The universal research-storage statement should therefore be read as a project
binding, not constitutional semantics.

General law:

\[
\boxed{
\text{retain only those capabilities over history that remain standingly permitted
and protected at the declared horizon.}
}
\]

This permits:
- regenerative ancestry;
- restricted/sealed ancestry;
- intentionally erased payloads;
- purpose-limited access.

---

# 226. Machine-unlearning crawl — endpoint equivalence is not deletion proof

Machine unlearning attacks a tempting implementation shortcut.

## 226.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ModelEquivalentToRetrainingWithout}(d)
=
\mathsf{DeletionOfInfluenceAndRecoverability}(d).
}
\]

Published unlearning work demonstrates that privacy/deletion guarantees can fail
through:
- dependence among remaining/deleted records;
- cached intermediate computations;
- repeated releases;
- insufficiently auditable algorithmic histories.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BehavioralPostDeletionEquivalence}
\neq
\mathsf{DeletionProvenance}
\neq
\mathsf{PrivacyGuarantee}.
}
\]

## 226.2 Unlearning as a path property

An unlearning claim must be tied to a disposition path:

\[
\boxed{
D_t
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{DeleteRequest}(d)}
D'_t
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{UnlearningProcedure}}
M'
}
\]

plus a declared protected observation/adversary family.

The checker must establish the native unlearning/deletion relation, not infer it
solely from endpoint behavior.

This is another application of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameEndpoint}
\neq
\mathsf{SameDerivation}.
}
\]

## 226.3 Less retained state may be the correct optimization

Recent system-aware unlearning work explicitly studies settings in which retaining
less system state can improve both deletion security and update efficiency.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MoreRegenerativeInternalMemory}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{BetterUnlearningSystem}.
}
\]

The protected objective determines what should remain recoverable.

---

# 227. Anti-recovery certificates — nonrecoverability is coverage-relative

The erasure branch now asks:

\[
\boxed{
\text{What makes an erasure claim stand?}
}
\]

A failed recovery attempt is insufficient.

## 227.1 Strong breaker

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoRecoveryPathFound}
\neq
\mathsf{RecoveryInfeasible}.
}
\]

This is the exact dual of:

\[
\mathsf{NoBreakerFound}
\neq
\mathsf{NoBreakerExists}.
\]

## 227.2 Anti-recovery relation

Let \(\mathcal O\) be a declared attacker/observation/recovery family.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AntiRecovery}_{\mathcal O}(x)
}
\]

when a binding-native theorem/check/certificate establishes that every covered
recovery attempt in \(\mathcal O\) fails to recover the protected content beyond
the declared leakage/effort bound.

This may be:
- cryptographic;
- information-theoretic;
- physical/sanitization;
- access-control based;
- statistical/privacy based;
- legal/organizational;
- composite.

Do not universalize the proof method.

## 227.3 Exact symmetry

Regenerative memory:

\[
\boxed{
\exists r\in\mathcal R:
r(\hat x)=x
}
\]

under a standing recovery contract.

Certified erasure:

\[
\boxed{
\forall r\in\mathcal O:
\neg\mathsf{RecoversProtectedContent}(r,\hat x)
}
\]

under complete/standing anti-recovery coverage.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecoveryCertificate}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\mathsf{AntiRecoveryCertificate}
}
\]

are dual protected-continuation claims, not separate semantic universes.

---

# 228. Side-channel crawl — the recovery family must include indirect observations

Side channels attack overly narrow erasure/privacy coverage.

## 228.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PayloadUnavailable}
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{SecretUnrecoverable}.
}
\]

An implementation may reveal information through:
- execution timing;
- power;
- cache behavior;
- electromagnetic/acoustic effects;
- derived state;
- metadata;
- output correlations.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DirectDataAccessBlocked}
\neq
\mathsf{ProtectedInformationFlowBlocked}.
}
\]

## 228.2 Observation-family-relative confidentiality

Let:

\[
\mathcal O
=
\mathcal O_{\mathrm{direct}}
\cup
\mathcal O_{\mathrm{derived}}
\cup
\mathcal O_{\mathrm{side}}
\cup\cdots
\]

be the declared attack/observation family.

An erasure/privacy claim stands only relative to declared \(\mathcal O\) and
coverage.

If a new side channel is discovered:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal O_t\subsetneq\mathcal O_{t+1},
}
\]

the prior anti-recovery fold/license may reopen.

This is structurally identical to future continuation-family expansion.

## 228.3 Hyperproperty return

Information-flow security may concern relations among multiple executions rather
than one trace.

This was already retained in the hyperproperty crawl.

Therefore side-channel security does not add a new hyperproperty primitive.

It sharpens the erasure/privacy contract:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Erasure/PrivacyCoverage}
\text{ may require an execution-family observer, not a single-state check.}
}
\]

---

# 229. Storage architecture compression — retention and forgetting are one capability-boundary problem

The preceding domains contract into one smaller machine relation.

For information/form \(x\), define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{UseBoundary}(x)
=
\langle
\mathsf{RequiredUses},
\mathsf{ForbiddenUses},
\mathsf{ConditionalUses},
\mathsf{Coverage},
\mathsf{Authority},
\mathsf{Unlocks}
\rangle.
}
\]

Then memory/storage decisions are derived:

### Active

Keep \(x\) directly accessible because currently required uses demand it.

### Folded/recoverable

Replace \(x\) by \(c(x)\) while preserving a standing recovery path for authorized
future uses.

### Restricted/sealed

Retain \(x\) or its recovery path, but only conditionally expose it under a
standing authority/purpose relation.

### Erased

Intentionally remove the protected recovery capability and retain only whatever
non-sensitive disposition evidence remains lawfully retainable.

### Lost

Protected recovery is unavailable without a standing erasure/disposition
justification.

These are derived statuses, not primitive semantic kinds.

## 229.1 Revised top-level storage law

Replace the unconditional slogan:

\[
\text{ACTIVE}\neq\text{FOLDED-BUT-REGENERABLE}\neq\text{LOST}
\]

with the stronger relational statement:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{ACTIVE, RECOVERABLE, RESTRICTED, ERASED, AND LOST
ARE CONSEQUENCE/authority-RELATIVE CAPABILITY PROFILES,
NOT PERMANENT ONTOLOGICAL STATES.}
}
\]

The key distinction is whether each protected use/recovery relation is:
- standingly available;
- standingly prohibited;
- conditional;
- unknown;
- accidentally unavailable.

---

# 230. Self-application to regenerative research memory

The research process itself must obey the new law.

The project ledger's append-only policy is valid for the current research corpus
only to the extent that retaining its contents is within the current protected
binding.

Do not universalize:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AppendOnlyResearchHistory}
=
\mathsf{UniversalStorageSemantics}.
}
\]

A future system using the calculus may need:
- immutable event/provenance identity;
- while erasing or sealing sensitive payloads;
- while retaining only non-sensitive deletion proofs/tombstones;
- while preventing deleted content from being regenerated.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MonotoneDispositionHistory}
\neq
\mathsf{MonotonePayloadRecoverability}.
}
\]

This preserves event-sourced accountability without requiring universal payload
immortality.

## 230.1 Repository consequence

The Rust reference implementation should not eventually assume that every
canonical artifact payload is perpetually recoverable merely because artifact/event
identity is immutable.

If privacy/erasure bindings are introduced, keep separate roles such as:

```text
ArtifactIdentity / EventIdentity
PayloadRef or EncryptedPayloadRef
DispositionRelation
Access/RecoveryAuthority
ErasureEvidence
AntiRecoveryCoverage
```

Do not implement these until a protected repository fixture requires them.

The important architectural constraint is to avoid making "content-addressed and
append-only" semantically equivalent to "perpetually plaintext-recoverable."

## 230.2 Fixtures

### ERASE-001 — intentional erasure differs from loss

Same final inability to recover payload.

One path has standing erasure authority + checked erasure occurrence.

Other path is accidental missing data.

Expected:
different disposition classification/provenance.

### ERASE-002 — recovery failure is not erasure proof

Bounded recovery search fails.

Expected:
`Unknown/ResourceBounded`, not certified erased.

### ERASE-003 — cryptographic erase

Encrypted payload remains physically stored.
Required encryption keys are sanitized under an admitted cryptographic-erase
contract.

Expected:
direct ciphertext ancestry may remain;
plaintext recovery capability becomes certified infeasible only under declared
coverage/effort assumptions.

### PRIV-001 — richer representation violates protected privacy

Two presentations preserve required task behavior.
One additionally exposes a forbidden sensitive distinction.

Expected:
the richer presentation is not protectedly superior.

### PRIV-002 — post-processing preserves declared DP contract

A standing differentially private release is transformed downstream.

Expected:
post-processing cannot be treated as access to the original secret source;
privacy standing follows only under the native post-processing theorem.

### LAW-001 — stored but restricted

Payload remains stored.
Ordinary processing purpose becomes unauthorized.
Specific legal/audit purpose remains authorized.

Expected:
storage standing does not imply general processing standing.

### UNLEARN-001 — endpoint equivalence not deletion provenance

Model output equals a retrained-without-\(d\) reference under the fixture.
A protected cache still contains \(d\)-dependent state.

Expected:
no unlearning/deletion certification.

### SIDE-001 — deleted direct payload, surviving indirect channel

Direct record/key is unavailable.
A declared side/derived channel still distinguishes the protected secret.

Expected:
anti-recovery/privacy claim fails.

---

# 231. Network ablation — ProtectedSimulationOrder becomes ProtectedUseBoundary-relative

The previous parent:

\[
\mathsf{ProtectedSimulationOrder}
\]

survives only after refinement.

Its lawful interpretation is now:

\[
\boxed{
A\succeq_{\mathcal B}B
}
\]

relative to a full `ProtectedUseBoundary` \(\mathcal B\).

It is not simply "A can do everything B can."

It must respect:
- required capabilities;
- forbidden capabilities/leakage;
- conditional authority;
- approximation direction;
- recovery/erasure obligations.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedSimulationOrder}
=
\mathsf{UseBoundaryRespectingSimulationOrder}.
}
\]

This is a refinement, not a new independent node.

## 231.1 Compression of memory methods

The following now share one parent:

- regenerative recovery;
- restricted/sealed storage;
- cryptographic erase;
- privacy-preserving release;
- machine unlearning;
- active memory collection;
- authority-conditioned access.

Parent relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProtectedCapabilityTransformation}.
}
\]

Question:

\[
\boxed{
?Y[
\mathsf{RequiredUsesPreserved}(X,Y)
\land
\mathsf{ForbiddenUsesNotEnabled}(X,Y)
\land
\mathsf{ConditionalUsesRespectAuthority}(X,Y)
].
}
\]

This is ordinary `OpenRoleCompletion` over a protected consequence web.

No new primitive.

---

# 232. Final residual and fixed-point test

The hardest residual becomes:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Can the machine prove complete recovery/non-recovery coverage
over every future observer, side channel, authority change, and protected use?}
}
\]

In unrestricted open-ended settings, no.

The same effectivity/coverage limits already established for breaker search apply.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoKnownRecoveryChannel}
\neq
\mathsf{CertifiedErasure}.
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoKnownPrivacyLeak}
\neq
\mathsf{CertifiedNoninterference}.
}
\]

A global certificate requires a declared observer/recovery envelope and a standing
native proof over it.

Therefore the branch reaches:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CERTIFIED\ LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}
}
\]

under the expanded erasure/privacy/unlearning/law/side-channel corpus.

Reopen when:
- the protected use boundary changes;
- a new observer/recovery channel is introduced;
- an erased payload is discovered to remain recoverable;
- a recovery obligation conflicts with an erasure obligation;
- a retention authority or purpose changes;
- a new binding supplies a stronger completeness theorem.

The successor storage invariant is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{GOOD MEMORY IS NOT MAXIMAL RETENTION.}
}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{GOOD MEMORY PRESERVES EXACTLY THE CAPABILITIES
THAT SHOULD REMAIN AVAILABLE, TO THE ROLES/PURPOSES
FOR WHICH THEY SHOULD REMAIN AVAILABLE, WHILE MAKING
PROTECTED FORBIDDEN CAPABILITIES UNAVAILABLE UNDER
THE DECLARED COVERAGE MODEL.}
}
\]


---

# 233. Semantic-version crawl — stored form is not interpreted meaning

The previous branch refined memory into a protected capability boundary.

The next global breaker asks whether preserving a form/artifact is sufficient to
preserve the relation it previously expressed.

Compiler ABIs, serialization schemas, legal interpretation, and diachronic
linguistics all answer negatively.

## 233.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{FormIdentity}
=
\mathsf{InterpretationIdentity}.
}
\]

For represented form \(f\), define a binding/context-indexed interpretation
relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Interprets}_{\mathbb B,c,a,t}(f,m).
}
\]

Here:
- \(\mathbb B\) is the semantic/implementation binding;
- \(c\) relevant context;
- \(a\) authority/jurisdiction/interpreter authority;
- \(t\) time/version/succession coordinate;
- \(m\) interpreted meaning/behavior/relation.

The calculus does not universalize these coordinates.
A binding may prove some irrelevant and fold them.

## 233.2 Compiler ABI return

GCC's compatibility documentation makes binary meaning dependent on an ABI shared
across compilers, assemblers, linkers, runtime support, layout/calling conventions,
and libraries.

Different tools or versions can produce artifacts that do not interoperate even
when source-level names/forms look unchanged.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameSymbol/SourceForm}
\neq
\mathsf{SameRuntimeContract}.
}
\]

ABI version is not merely metadata.
It may be a determining interpretation relation.

No ABI primitive is required.

---

# 234. Schema-evolution crawl — parseability is not semantic preservation

Protocol Buffers supplies a mature versioned-serialization binding.

It distinguishes:
- wire-safe changes;
- wire-compatible but potentially lossy changes;
- wire-unsafe changes.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ParsesUnderNewSchema}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticsPreserved}.
}
\]

A representation may remain syntactically/wire parseable while changing or
truncating protected content.

## 234.1 Unknown fields

An older protobuf interpreter can encounter well-formed fields that it does not
recognize.

Binary protobuf preserves those unknown fields for later reserialization, while
some other transformations/formats can drop them.

This supplies a strong foreign realization of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{UninterpretedNow}
\neq
\mathsf{IrrelevantToFutureInterpretation}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CannotCurrentlyInterpret}
\neq
\mathsf{SafeToDiscard}.
}
\]

## 234.2 Opaque carry

Define a transparent storage/recovery method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OpaqueCarry}(x)
}
\]

meaning:
- current binding does not semantically promote/interpret \(x\);
- identity/provenance and lawful payload are preserved;
- future interpretation remains possible if a stronger binding becomes available;
- no semantic claim is generated merely from retention.

This compiles from:
- unknown/preconceptual residual;
- provenance retention;
- future reinterpretability;
- recovery/reopening.

No new primitive.

## 234.3 Field reuse breaker

Protobuf's prohibition on reusing old field numbers gives an exact representation
of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{IdentifierReuse}
\neq
\mathsf{MeaningContinuity}.
}
\]

A historical identifier may need to remain reserved precisely because old
serialized artifacts still exist.

This strengthens path/ancestry sensitivity.

---

# 235. Historical-semantics crawl — etymological ancestry is not current semantic content

Historical linguistics supplies the next maximally different source.

The same lexical form can have:
- different meanings at different times;
- multiple contemporaneous senses;
- meaning shifts driven by social/pragmatic context;
- relationships to earlier etymons that do not determine current use.

Therefore retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EtymologicalAncestry}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentSemanticContent}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameExpressionForm}
\neq
\mathsf{SameMeaningAcrossTime/Speakers}.
}
\]

This is another instance of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Path/Ancestry}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentRole}.
}
\]

The ancestry remains valuable for explanation/reopening, but it does not acquire
current semantic authority merely because it is historically prior.

---

# 236. Pragmatics/rhetoric crawl — expression content is not utterance force

Pragmatics supplies a sharper occurrence-level breaker.

Let:
- \(e\) be an expression/sentence form;
- \(u\) one concrete utterance occurrence;
- \(c\) the utterance context;
- \(m\) literal/contextually resolved content;
- \(F\) communicative/illocutionary force.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExpressionType}
\neq
\mathsf{UtteranceOccurrence}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Literal/PropositionalContent}
\neq
\mathsf{CommunicativeForce}.
}
\]

The same expression may be used to:
- assert;
- warn;
- request;
- promise;
- imply;
- perform an institutionally recognized act;

depending on context, intention, and social/institutional rules.

## 236.1 Network contraction

This is not language-specific.

The same parent distinction already appears as:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{ProgramDescription}
\neq
\mathsf{ExecutionOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{ContinuationDescription}
\neq
\mathsf{CapturedContinuationOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{ExpressionType}
\neq
\mathsf{UtteranceOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{Schema/Artifact}
\neq
\mathsf{DecodedInterpretationOccurrence}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Define the transparent parent relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ContextualInstantiation}(d,\gamma,o)
}
\]

where description/form \(d\) is instantiated under contextual environment
\(\gamma\) as occurrence \(o\).

This is ordinary typed binding and occurrence provenance, not new ontology.

---

# 237. Legal interpretation crawl — authority is part of interpretation, not text

Law supplies a domain where textual form, interpretation, and authority are
explicitly separated.

A statute may require interpretation using:
- text;
- broader statutory context;
- canons;
- history/purpose;
- implementation;
- precedent.

A precedent has binding force only under the appropriate court/jurisdiction/fact
relation.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SameLegalText}
\neq
\mathsf{SameLawfulApplicationAcrossContexts}.
}
\]

and:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Interpretation}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthoritativeInterpretation}.
}
\]

## 237.1 Authority succession

A later binding authority may change the standing interpretation without changing
the source statutory bytes.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TextRevision}
\neq
\mathsf{InterpretiveAuthorityRevision}.
}
\]

This is an exact foreign realization of:
- `AuthoritySuccession`;
- `GovernedRuleRevision`;
- `TemporalPresentationTransport`;
- `RevisionBySupportDelta`.

No legal primitive is imported.

## 237.2 Historical vs current standing

A superseded interpretation can remain:
- historically actual;
- provenance-relevant;
- legally explanatory;

while no longer being current binding authority.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{HistoricallyAuthoritative}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentlyAuthoritative}.
}
\]

This mirrors versioned implementation semantics.

---

# 238. Semantic rehydration — recovering payload is not recovering meaning

The preceding domains generate a direct attack on regenerative memory.

Suppose payload \(f\) is exactly recovered from ancestry.

That does not establish that its prior semantic role \(m\) can be regenerated.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticRehydration}(f,m;\mathcal H)
}
\]

as a transparent method requiring enough supported recovery of:
- form/payload;
- relevant binding/interpreter/schema/ABI;
- determining context;
- authority/version;
- required external references/dependencies;
- protected interpretation checks.

Program:

1. recover \(f\);
2. recover or lawfully reconstruct the determining interpretation environment
   \(\gamma\);
3. evaluate/check \(\mathsf{Interprets}_{\gamma}(f,m)\);
4. distinguish historical interpretation from current interpretation;
5. if migrating, establish a protected semantic bridge;
6. preserve residuals when any determining input is unavailable.

Typed residuals include:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{InterpreterRecoveryGap},\\
&\mathsf{Schema/ABIGap},\\
&\mathsf{InterpretationContextGap},\\
&\mathsf{AuthorityResolutionGap},\\
&\mathsf{SemanticMigrationUnknown}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

## 238.1 Strong breaker

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PayloadRecovery}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticRecovery}.
}
\]

Therefore a fold can be physically reversible but semantically non-regenerative.

This strengthens the existing recovery contract rather than creating a separate
memory primitive.

---

# 239. CompCert crawl — semantic migration is a protected simulation, not byte identity

Formal compiler verification supplies a mature exact source for semantic transport.

CompCert proves a semantic-preservation relation from source program semantics to
generated assembly semantics, expressed through observable behaviors/simulations.

The key transport is not:

\[
\mathsf{SourceBytes}
=
\mathsf{TargetBytes}.
\]

It is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ObservableBehavior}_{target}
\preceq
\mathsf{AllowedBehavior}_{source}
}
\]

under the compiler's formal semantics.

## 239.1 Bridge result

Compiler correctness is therefore a native realization of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{UseBoundaryRespectingSimulationOrder}
+
\mathsf{ProtectedPathFactorization}.
}
\]

A source-to-target transformation is licensed when the protected behavior diagram
commutes/refines in the admitted direction.

This independently supports the prior compression of BridgeSchema and composition
under protected commuting/factorization structure.

## 239.2 Failure path

The compiler may reject source input rather than produce target code.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TransformationFailure}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticCounterexample}.
}
\]

Again:
operational non-completion is not semantic falsity.

---

# 240. Interpretation dependency minimization

The new semantic-recovery method is recursively subjected to the existing
minimal-determining-family process.

Given interpretation result:

\[
\Gamma
=
\langle
\mathbb B,c,a,t,r_1,\ldots,r_n
\rangle
\vdash
\mathsf{Interprets}(f,m),
\]

vary/remove coordinates.

Find a minimal/nondominated determining family:

\[
\boxed{
\Gamma^\star\subseteq\Gamma
}
\]

such that the protected interpretation still factors through \(\Gamma^\star\).

This yields:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{InterpretationDependencyMinimize}
=
\mathsf{DeterminingFamilySearch}
+
\mathsf{SeparatorSearch}.
}
\]

No new method generator is needed.

This prevents a bad response to semantic drift:
retaining every possible historical/contextual fact forever.

Only determining dependencies must remain active/recoverable under the declared
horizon.

---

# 241. Repository implementation consequences

The current repository head inspected during this crawl is:

`57b27da36312229b5c17d65fb28683a39a940ee6`
(`feat: prove reciprocal occurrence vertical slice`).

That implementation advance concerns finite reciprocal occurrence admission and
does not yet force semantic-version/migration machinery.

Do not divert the current implementation order.

Instead retain future conformance pressure.

## SEMVER-001 — same artifact, different binding interpretation

One payload/artifact is interpreted under two binding/schema versions.

Expected:
artifact identity remains equal;
interpretation relation may differ.

## SEMVER-002 — payload recovery without interpreter

Exact payload is recovered.
Required historical interpreter/schema is absent.

Expected:
payload recovery succeeds;
semantic rehydration returns `InterpreterRecoveryGap`.

## ABI-001 — same symbol, incompatible runtime contract

Two compiled artifacts expose matching source-level/symbol names but incompatible
ABI layouts/calling conventions.

Expected:
no interoperability/semantic bridge inferred from name identity.

## PROTO-001 — parseable but lossy migration

Old/new schemas both parse a message, but protected information is truncated/lost.

Expected:
parseability does not license exact semantic migration.

## PROTO-002 — opaque unknown preservation

Current decoder cannot interpret one field but can preserve its raw unknown-field
representation.

Expected:
no semantic promotion;
future reinterpretability remains available.

## PROTO-003 — opaque field destroyed by representation change

An intermediate format drops unknown-field information.

Expected:
future exact semantic recovery fails for horizons protecting that field.

## LING-001 — same form, different historical sense

Same expression form is bound to two time/context-indexed meanings.

Expected:
no current-meaning inference from form identity or etymological ancestry alone.

## PRAG-001 — same sentence content, different force

Two utterance occurrences share expression/content but differ in admitted
institutional/communicative context.

Expected:
utterance-force relation remains occurrence/context indexed.

## LAW-001B — unchanged source text, authority succession

Source legal text remains byte-identical while a later binding authority changes the
standing interpretation.

Expected:
historical interpretation remains in provenance;
current authority relation changes without fabricating source-text revision.

## MIGRATE-001 — semantic bridge

Source and target representations differ physically.
Standing transformation theorem/checker establishes protected observable behavior
preservation.

Expected:
migration licensed relative to declared protected horizon.

# 242. Compression checkpoint

New protected distinctions:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{FormIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{InterpretationIdentity},\\
&\mathsf{PayloadRecovery}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticRecovery},\\
&\mathsf{Parseability}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticPreservation},\\
&\mathsf{UninterpretedNow}
\neq
\mathsf{IrrelevantLater},\\
&\mathsf{EtymologicalAncestry}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentMeaning},\\
&\mathsf{ExpressionType}
\neq
\mathsf{UtteranceOccurrence},\\
&\mathsf{LiteralContent}
\neq
\mathsf{CommunicativeForce},\\
&\mathsf{Interpretation}
\neq
\mathsf{AuthoritativeInterpretation},\\
&\mathsf{HistoricallyAuthoritative}
\neq
\mathsf{CurrentlyAuthoritative}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Network contractions:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ContextualInstantiation}
}
\]

folds description/type versus occurrence distinctions across programs,
continuations, language, schemas, and execution.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SemanticRehydration}
}
\]

folds payload recovery + interpreter/binding recovery + determining-context
recovery + authority/version resolution + semantic bridge.

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OpaqueCarry}
}
\]

folds current non-interpretability with future-preserving storage without semantic
promotion.

No constitutional primitive or new M-pattern is forced.

## Residual

The strongest remaining question is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Can every determining interpretation dependency itself be
regeneratively preserved or reconstructed?}
}
\]

No universal positive answer follows.

If the historical interpreter, external reference, social context, or authority
relation is not preserved and cannot be reconstructed, exact historical semantic
rehydration can be impossible even when the payload survives.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CERTIFIED\ LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}
}
\]

under the expanded compiler/ABI/schema/law/historical-semantics/pragmatics corpus.

Reopen when:
- a new protected interpretation use separates currently identified versions;
- a schema/ABI/parser change alters protected behavior;
- an authority succession changes standing interpretation;
- an opaque field becomes interpretable;
- a historical interpretation dependency is discovered to be unrecoverable;
- a stronger native semantic-transport theorem becomes available.

# 243. Trusting-trust crawl — self-regeneration is not self-discharge

The previous branch ended with:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PayloadRecovery}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticRecovery}.
}
\]

The next hostile self-modification case is a tool that interprets/compiles its own
future implementation.

Thompson's trusting-trust construction and Wheeler's diverse double-compiling
(DDC) provide the native source.

## 243.1 Strongest false identification

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelfRegenerationSuccess}
=
\mathsf{SourceCorrespondence}.
}
\]

A self-hosted compiler binary may contain behavior that:
- is not visible in the putative source;
- inserts itself into descendant compiler binaries;
- reproduces the hidden behavior under recompilation.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecompileToSameLineage}
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{ExecutableCorrespondsToPutativeSource}.
}
\]

The generation path itself can carry the hidden relation.

This is a direct self-modification breaker:
the system cannot use descent from its own current executable as independent
evidence that the successor implements the represented source.

## 243.2 Stronger separation

Even source/binary correspondence is not enough for semantic goodness.

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SourceCorrespondence}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticCorrectness}
\neq
\mathsf{PolicyAcceptability}.
}
\]

DDC addresses source/executable correspondence under its assumptions.
It does not by itself prove that the source implements the intended semantics or
satisfies every protected policy.

---

# 244. Diverse double-compiling — cross the lineage with an independent generator

DDC supplies the repair.

Let:
- \(S_P\) be the parent compiler source;
- \(S_A\) the compiler-under-test source;
- \(C_A\) the compiler-under-test executable;
- \(C_T\) a sufficiently trusted/diverse compiler;
- \(\mathsf{Compile}(C,S)\) the native compilation occurrence.

Construct:

\[
C_1=\mathsf{Compile}(C_T,S_P)
\]

and:

\[
C_2=\mathsf{Compile}(C_1,S_A).
\]

Under the DDC assumptions, compare:

\[
\boxed{
C_2=C_A.
}
\]

The point is not double compilation itself.
The determining relation is that one validation path crosses an independently
grounded generator/environment rather than remaining entirely inside the suspect
lineage.

Define transparent method:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{IndependentRegenerationCrossCheck}.
}
\]

General form:

\[
\boxed{
X
\xrightarrow{G_{\mathrm{lineage}}}
Y
\qquad
X
\xrightarrow{G_{\mathrm{independent}}}
\hat Y
\qquad
\mathsf{ProtectedCompare}(Y,\hat Y).
}
\]

## 244.1 Independence is a contract, not a name

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
G_1\neq G_2
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{Independent}(G_1,G_2).
}
\]

Two apparently different validators/generators can share:
- compiler ancestry;
- runtime;
- libraries;
- hardware;
- source generator;
- correlated trigger conditions.

Therefore independence must itself be represented and supported at the declared
breaker horizon.

This regenerates:
- `IndependentDischarge`;
- support-topology separation;
- provenance-sensitive context.

No DDC primitive is required.

---

# 245. Proof-grounded bootstrapping — self-compilation is not self-verification

CakeML supplies the positive self-hosting case.

A verified compiler algorithm is represented inside HOL, and proof-grounded
bootstrapping applies the verified compilation algorithm to itself inside the logic
so that the resulting low-level compiler implementation is connected to the
compiler-correctness theorem.

## 245.1 Strong breaker

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelfCompiled}
=
\mathsf{SelfVerified}.
}
\]

Ordinary self-compilation only establishes one generation occurrence.

Proof-grounded bootstrapping adds an independently checked theorem relating:
- the compiler algorithm;
- the self-application occurrence;
- the produced machine-code implementation;
- the formal source/target semantics.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelfCompilationOccurrence}
+
\mathsf{IndependentCompilerCorrectnessProof}
\to
\mathsf{VerifiedBootstrapResult}
}
\]

under the theorem prover/hardware/environment assumptions.

## 245.2 Trust is reduced, not abolished

Proof-grounded bootstrapping reduces the trusted base.
It does not establish:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoTrustAssumptions}.
}
\]

The proof checker/theorem prover, formal semantics, runtime boundary, hardware, and
other declared assumptions remain part of the support boundary.

This re-establishes:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SelfApplication}
\neq
\mathsf{SelfWarrant}.
}
\]

at the executable implementation level.

---

# 246. De Bruijn / proof-carrying code — generation authority and admission authority separate

Proof assistants satisfying the de Bruijn criterion and proof-carrying code
provide a smaller common architecture.

## 246.1 Generator/checker split

Let:

\[
G:\Sigma\rightsquigarrow (y,\pi)
\]

be an arbitrarily complex proposal/certificate generator.

Let:

\[
V(P,y,\pi)\to\{\mathsf{Accept},\mathsf{Reject},\mathsf{Unknown/Blocked}\}
\]

be a comparatively small checker for protected policy \(P\).

The key relation is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GeneratorProduces}
\neq
\mathsf{CheckerAdmits}.
}
\]

Proof-carrying code makes the producer responsible for supplying a certificate;
the consumer checks it against its own safety policy.

The de Bruijn criterion similarly concentrates proof authority in a small
independent checker/kernel rather than in the tactic/search machinery.

## 246.2 Transparent method

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProofCarryingSuccessor}.
}
\]

Candidate self-revision package:

\[
\boxed{
R=
\langle
S_t,
S_{t+1},
\Delta,
\Pi,
\mathcal K,
\mathcal B,
\mathsf{Migration},
\mathsf{Recovery},
\mathsf{Breakers}
\rangle
}
\]

where \(\Pi\) is inspectable proof/certificate evidence for the declared protected
transition.

Admission is performed by a checker whose authority is not created by the candidate
revision itself.

## 246.3 Critical self-modification breaker

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\text{candidate rewrites checker}
\;\land\;
\text{rewritten checker accepts candidate}
\Rightarrow
\text{authorized self-revision}.
}
\]

That is circular admission.

If checker \(V_t\) changes to \(V_{t+1}\), checker succession is a separate protected
transition:

\[
\boxed{
V_t
\xrightarrow{\mathsf{CheckerSuccessionCertificate}}
V_{t+1}
}
\]

or must be grounded by another independent authorized verifier/root.

The candidate \(S_{t+1}\) cannot bootstrap the authority that authorizes
\(S_{t+1}\) unless the predecessor/external boundary explicitly licenses that
authority transfer.

---

# 247. Measured-boot crawl — chain validity is root-relative

TPM measured boot and remote attestation supply a hardware/system realization.

Each boot stage measures the next and extends the measurement into protected TPM
state.

This creates a chain:

\[
R
\to
B_1
\to
B_2
\to
\cdots
\to
B_n.
\]

## 247.1 Root breaker

The root of trust for measurement is not verified by the same measurement chain.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ValidTrustChain}
\neq
\mathsf{RootSelfVerified}.
}
\]

A chain can only transfer/compose the authority supplied at its root plus the
validity of each transition.

This is a physical-system realization of:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ReflectiveTower}
\neq
\mathsf{WarrantRoot}.
}
\]

## 247.2 Measurement is not semantic correctness

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MeasuredIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticCorrectness}.
}
\]

Attestation can establish which measured components/configuration were involved,
subject to the measurement root and logging assumptions.

A separate policy/semantic relation is required to conclude that the measured
software is:
- safe;
- correct;
- authorized;
- suitable for the current protected use.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AttestationEvidence}
\neq
\mathsf{WarrantedSemanticClaim}.
}
\]

No TPM/attestation primitive is added.

---

# 248. Translation-validation crawl — validate the actual transformation occurrence

Self-modification often changes the transformation/generator itself too quickly for
a once-for-all proof of the generator to be practical.

Translation validation supplies a different native method:
after each transformation occurrence, validate that particular source/target pair
against the semantic relation.

## 248.1 Strong separation

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TransformerGloballyVerified}
=
\mathsf{ThisTransformationValidated}.
}
\]

Neither direction holds universally.

An unverified transformer may produce a particular result that a sound validator can
certify.

A generally verified transformer claim does not replace checking actuality,
environment, input identity, or assumptions for a concrete deployment if those are
protected.

## 248.2 Transparent method

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{OccurrenceSpecificDischarge}(u,x,y,\pi).
}
\]

Program:

1. preserve the actual transformation occurrence;
2. bind exact source \(x\), operator \(u\), target \(y\);
3. generate or recover validation evidence \(\pi\);
4. check protected source/target semantics;
5. admit only the occurrence-relative preservation relation;
6. do not promote unproved global correctness of \(u\).

This is `ScrutinizeOccurrence + ProtectedSemanticTransport + Grounding`.

## 248.3 Self-modification advantage

A rapidly changing self-modification generator can remain outside the trusted
semantic kernel if each candidate transition carries independently checkable
evidence.

This keeps:
- creativity/search/planning mutable;
- admission semantics narrow;
- trust dependencies inspectable.

---

# 249. Unified self-modification admission machine

The previous self-modification structures now contract.

A lawful successor transition has:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GovernedSuccessorAdmission}
}
\]

with roles:

\[
\boxed{
\langle
S_t,
G_t,
R_t,
S_{t+1},
V_t,
A_t,
\mathcal B_t,
E_t
\rangle
}
\]

where:
- \(S_t\): predecessor standing machine/state;
- \(G_t\): candidate generator;
- \(R_t\): proposal/certificate package;
- \(S_{t+1}\): candidate successor;
- \(V_t\): admission checker/kernel;
- \(A_t\): independently grounded authority/root assumptions;
- \(\mathcal B_t\): protected-use boundary;
- \(E_t\): actual build/test/execution evidence.

Admission requires at least:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{CandidateIdentityChecked},\\
&\mathsf{ActualTransformationLinked},\\
&\mathsf{RequiredCapabilitiesPreserved},\\
&\mathsf{ForbiddenCapabilitiesNotEnabled},\\
&\mathsf{NewCapabilityClaimsDischarged},\\
&\mathsf{Recovery/ErasureObligationsDischarged},\\
&\mathsf{SemanticMigrationChecked},\\
&\mathsf{CheckerAuthorityNonCircular},\\
&\mathsf{ResidualsExplicit}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

This is not a new constitutional primitive.

It expands to existing:
- predecessor judgment;
- protected-use boundary;
- actual occurrence;
- independent discharge;
- semantic transport;
- breaker search;
- recovery/reopening;
- authority succession;
- standing.

## 249.1 Checker migration

If:

\[
V_t\neq V_{t+1},
\]

then do not combine checker migration with ordinary successor admission into one
self-authorizing step.

Require:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CheckerMigrationBridge}(V_t,V_{t+1})
}
\]

checked by:
- predecessor checker \(V_t\);
- an independent checker/root;
- or another explicitly standing authority route.

Then future successors may use \(V_{t+1}\).

This is the executable form of:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{THE PREDECESSOR JUDGES THE SUCCESSOR.}
}
\]

---

# 250. Trust-root minimization — small root is not no root

The self-modification crawl now attacks its own checker architecture.

Could the checker be eliminated by recursively checking the checker?

No.

Measured boot, proof-assistant kernels, DDC, and proof-grounded bootstrapping all
move the trust boundary but do not create an internally self-grounding global
certificate.

Define:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{TrustRootMinimize}.
}
\]

Goal:
minimize the independently trusted base while preserving:
- checkability;
- semantic coverage;
- actuality linkage;
- authority;
- replayability.

Do not infer:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SmallerTCB}
=
\mathsf{MoreCorrect}.
}
\]

A smaller root is easier to inspect/independently implement, but still requires a
standing correspondence between:
- formal rules;
- checker implementation;
- actual executed checker;
- intended protected semantics.

## 250.1 Independent-checker plurality

Multiple independent checkers may increase confidence or expose implementation
disagreement.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MultipleCheckerAgreement}
\neq
\mathsf{Truth}.
}
\]

They may share the same formalization defect.

Thus independent implementations improve implementation-discrepancy coverage, not
the truth of the protected model by themselves.

---

# 251. Repository implementation consequences

Current inspected head during this pass:

`3dd7cef1f9e1a590d118dacb3d27b253bdde6012`
(`feat: admit finite supported answers`).

That commit adds a first finite supported-answer boundary which:
- replays event-linked decoding;
- requires exact `Probe` observation/support provenance for each completion;
- preserves a multi-completion result rather than arbitrarily selecting one;
- does not yet perform answer-slot substitution or continuation choice.

This is exactly where the repository should remain focused.

The self-modification admission architecture is later-layer pressure, not a reason
to jump ahead of the current first-order answer-dependent continuation bridge.

## 251.1 Future fixtures

### SELFTRUST-001 — self-regeneration does not prove source correspondence

A compiler binary recompiles its putative source and reproduces the suspect lineage.

Expected:
no source-correspondence standing solely from self-regeneration.

### SELFTRUST-002 — independent regeneration cross-check

A diverse trusted path regenerates the compiler-under-test under a standing DDC-like
contract and matches the target.

Expected:
admit only the source/executable correspondence established by that contract.

### SELFCERT-001 — candidate cannot authorize its own checker rewrite

One patch changes implementation and validator; only new validator accepts it.

Expected:
reject circular admission.

### SELFCERT-002 — predecessor-authorized checker migration

Old validator checks a migration certificate for the new validator.

Expected:
new validator can become authoritative only after the migration relation stands.

### PCC-001 — untrusted generator, valid certificate

Generator is not standing-trusted.
Candidate carries a certificate accepted by the authoritative checker.

Expected:
candidate may be admitted despite generator's lack of semantic authority.

### PCC-002 — generator says safe, certificate rejects

Expected:
generator assertion carries no admission authority.

### ATTEST-001 — measured image is not semantically safe by identity alone

Measured boot proves image/config identity relative to root.

No semantic safety relation stands.

Expected:
attestation evidence preserved; safety remains open.

### ROOT-001 — chain cannot prove its own root

Every downstream stage is measured correctly.

Root premise is removed.

Expected:
chain-wide semantic/authority claim cannot stand.

### TV-001 — occurrence-specific validated self-rewrite

Unverified rewrite generator produces candidate \(S'\).

Independent validator proves this exact transition preserves protected semantics.

Expected:
admit the occurrence-relative transport without promoting global generator
correctness.

### TV-002 — validated generator does not excuse mismatched actuality

Proof refers to \(S'\), but actual executed artifact is \(S''\).

Expected:
reject due to actuality/identity mismatch.

# 252. Compression checkpoint

New protected distinctions:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{SelfRegenerationSuccess}
\neq
\mathsf{SourceCorrespondence},\\
&\mathsf{SourceCorrespondence}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticCorrectness},\\
&\mathsf{SelfCompiled}
\neq
\mathsf{SelfVerified},\\
&\mathsf{GeneratorProduces}
\neq
\mathsf{CheckerAdmits},\\
&\mathsf{ValidTrustChain}
\neq
\mathsf{RootSelfVerified},\\
&\mathsf{MeasuredIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticCorrectness},\\
&\mathsf{TransformerGloballyVerified}
\neq
\mathsf{ThisTransformationValidated},\\
&\mathsf{MultipleCheckerAgreement}
\neq
\mathsf{Truth}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

New transparent methods/views:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\mathsf{IndependentRegenerationCrossCheck},\\
&\mathsf{ProofCarryingSuccessor},\\
&\mathsf{OccurrenceSpecificDischarge},\\
&\mathsf{GovernedSuccessorAdmission},\\
&\mathsf{TrustRootMinimize}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

Network contraction:

All five expand into existing:
- `IndependentDischarge`;
- `ScrutinizeOccurrence`;
- `ProtectedSemanticTransport`;
- `PredecessorJudgment`;
- `AuthoritySuccession`;
- `Grounding`;
- `Standing`;
- `Recovery/Reopening`.

No constitutional semantic generator and no new M-pattern is forced.

## Residual

The strongest remaining self-modification residual is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Can a self-modifying machine preserve checker/root independence
when every implementation layer, including the checker and its execution
environment, is eventually subject to revision?}
}
\]

The current answer is conditional:

- yes locally, by explicit checker succession and independently grounded roots;
- no global self-warrant follows from indefinite reflective checking;
- every concrete trust chain remains relative to its declared root/assumption
  boundary.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CERTIFIED\ LOCAL\ FIXED\ POINT}
}
\]

under the trusting-trust, DDC, proof-grounded bootstrap, proof-carrying-code,
proof-kernel, measured-boot, and translation-validation corpus.

Reopen if a domain exhibits a protected self-revision behavior that cannot be
represented through:
- candidate generation;
- independent/previously authorized checking;
- explicit root assumptions;
- checker succession;
- actuality linkage;
- protected semantic transport;
- standing/reopening.

# CLOCKWORK ENGINEERING CONSTRAINT

The implementation target is not maximal architectural generality.

It is:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{THE SMALLEST MECHANISM THAT MAKES EVERY CURRENTLY REQUIRED
SEMANTIC TRANSITION EXPLICIT, CHECKABLE, REPLAYABLE, AND NON-CIRCULAR.}
}
\]

## Engineering gate

A proposed type, artifact, trait, service, crate, registry, cache, scheduler,
or semantic layer is admitted only if at least one of the following is true:

1. removing it breaks a currently protected executable behavior;
2. it owns a genuinely distinct authority/actuality/persistence boundary;
3. two or more concrete existing paths require the same invariant and factoring
   it removes duplicated logic without hiding provenance.

Otherwise keep it as:
- a local function;
- a derived view;
- a test helper;
- a research macro;
- or prose provenance.

Do not implement a research-method name merely because it exists in the network.

## Clockwork invariants

Prefer:

```text
one semantic fact -> one authoritative representation
one actual event -> one append path
one transition -> one explicit input/output contract
one failure -> one typed residual at the boundary where it occurs
one authority decision -> one owner
one derived view -> recomputable from authoritative state
```

Reject:

```text
shadow standing engines
duplicate caches of semantic truth
parallel event histories
generic plugin frameworks before a second real implementation exists
cross-cutting manager objects that merely forward calls
boolean flags standing in for evidence
implicit global registries
hidden callbacks/closures for semantic continuation
speculative persistence of derived objects
"future proofing" without a breaker
```

## Default implementation order

For each frontier obligation:

```text
1. write the smallest breaker/conformance fixture
2. expose the exact missing relation
3. implement the narrowest pure/derived function that discharges it
4. reuse existing canonical types and stores
5. prove all prior fixtures still pass
6. only then factor repeated mechanics
```

Do not design the next three layers while the current layer is still open.

## Abstraction rule

A research macro is not an implementation component.

For example:

```text
SemanticRehydration
GovernedSuccessorAdmission
TrustRootMinimize
ContinuationQuotientMinimize
```

remain compile-time/research vocabulary until a current executable seam requires
their own identity, state, or authority.

Their implementation should normally disappear into composition of smaller
existing operations.

## Crate rule

Keep the present crate graph narrow.

Do not create a new orchestration crate until runtime + store + core must actually
participate in one transaction that cannot be cleanly owned by an existing crate.

Do not move semantic machinery into `ic-cli`.

## Mechanical criterion

A subsystem is "clockwork" when:

- every input is explicit;
- every output is typed;
- every dependency is inspectable;
- every state mutation has one owner;
- every semantic claim has one admission route;
- every derived result can be replayed;
- every failure stops locally with a typed residual;
- every component can be removed and its missing protected behavior named.

If a component cannot answer "what exact protected behavior fails if I remove it?",
it does not belong in the active implementation.


---

# 253. Clockwork-domain crawl — local transitions versus omniscient orchestration

The engineering binding now becomes a live research question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Does the cohesive machine require a central semantic orchestrator?}
}
\]

The strongest contrast is an omniscient manager that:
- owns global mutable state;
- dispatches methods;
- interprets events;
- decides relevance;
- updates standing;
- manages continuation state;
- handles persistence;
- coordinates replay.

Petri-net semantics, synchronous/reactive systems, microkernel architecture,
end-to-end system design, and deterministic state-machine replay all attack that
shape.

## 253.1 Petri-net return

Petri nets model concurrency, synchronization, and resource sharing using local
transitions connected to explicit pre/post conditions.

Portable relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LocalTransition}(I,O;\rho)
}
\]

where a transition:
- has explicit enabling/input conditions;
- consumes/reads only the state it requires;
- produces explicit local effects;
- composes with other transitions through shared typed interfaces.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{GlobalCoordination}
\neq
\mathsf{GlobalController}.
}
\]

Shared behavior may emerge from local transition compatibility rather than one
central manager.

Do not import Petri-net tokens/places as calculus primitives.

## 253.2 Strong breaker

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\text{because many stages compose}
\Rightarrow
\text{one object should own them all}.
}
\]

Composition requires explicit interfaces and laws, not managerial centralization.

---

# 254. Synchronous-reactive crawl — one explicit reaction, not hidden scheduling

Synchronous languages such as Esterel/Lustre provide the opposite useful extreme:
reactive computation is organized into explicit logical reactions.

For deterministic synchronous semantics:

\[
\boxed{
(\text{present state},\text{input})
\mapsto
(\text{output},\text{next state})
}
\]

for one logical reaction.

This gives the implementation a strong engineering target:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{WHEN ALL INPUTS TO A STEP ARE ALREADY AUTHORITATIVE,
THE STEP SHOULD BE AS CLOSE TO A PURE DETERMINISTIC
TRANSITION AS THE SEMANTICS ALLOW.}
}
\]

External uncertainty/nondeterminism enters only at explicit Probe/actuality
boundaries.

## 254.1 Causality breaker

Synchronous languages also expose causality cycles as a semantic problem rather
than hiding them behind a scheduler.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Feedback}
\neq
\mathsf{ImplicitCircularControl}.
}
\]

The runtime should reject or type residualize unsupported recurrence rather than
repair it with hidden scheduling policy.

This aligns with the current `ProgramIR` rejection of unguarded branch cycles.

---

# 255. Microkernel crawl — mechanism placement test

Microkernel design supplies an unusually direct engineering criterion.

A mechanism belongs in the privileged kernel only when moving it outside would
prevent required functionality.

Transport to Inquiry Calculus implementation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CorePlacement}(x)
}
\]

is justified only if at least one of these holds:

1. \(x\) owns a unique authority boundary;
2. \(x\) owns a unique actuality/mutation boundary;
3. \(x\) owns canonical identity required by later references;
4. moving \(x\) outward would make a required invariant impossible to enforce;
5. two or more concrete working paths already duplicate the same invariant and
   factoring removes duplication without obscuring provenance.

Otherwise \(x\) remains outside the core as:
- local function;
- derived view;
- test helper;
- compiler lowering;
- research macro;
- binding-specific adapter.

## 255.1 Policy/mechanism separation

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Mechanism}
\neq
\mathsf{Policy}.
}
\]

The runtime should provide only mechanisms required for:
- typed control flow;
- suspension/resumption;
- exact state transition;
- explicit external boundary.

Question selection, method policy, optimization, search heuristics, self-revision
policy, and domain strategy remain outside unless a concrete semantic invariant
forces otherwise.

---

# 256. End-to-end crawl — decide a property where its consequence is knowable

The end-to-end argument attacks redundant low-layer checks.

A low layer may perform useful preliminary checks, but if the endpoint must still
establish the complete protected property, duplicating the whole policy below can
increase complexity without eliminating endpoint responsibility.

Portable engineering law:

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{PLACE A CHECK AT THE NARROWEST BOUNDARY THAT HAS
ENOUGH INFORMATION AND AUTHORITY TO DECIDE THE PROTECTED CONSEQUENCE.}
}
\]

Lower-layer checks are justified when they:
- enforce a property only that layer can enforce;
- prevent malformed states from crossing the boundary;
- reduce cost as a performance optimization;
- provide independently useful defense under a declared threat model.

Do not replicate semantic standing/warrant logic in runtime/store merely because
those layers see related data.

## 256.1 Non-identification

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{EarlierCheck}
\neq
\mathsf{MoreFundamentalCheck}.
}
\]

A check belongs where its determining information lives.

---

# 257. Deterministic replay crawl — authoritative nondeterminism, derived determinism

State-machine replication supplies the replay invariant.

Replicas that receive the same deterministic command sequence in the same order
can remain in the same state.

Transport:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{AuthoritativeActuality}
+
\mathsf{DeterministicDerivedTransition}
\to
\mathsf{ColdReplay}.
}
\]

For the Inquiry Calculus machine:

- external nondeterminism is actualized once;
- raw return/event identity is preserved;
- replay does not redispatch the external operation;
- decode/support/standing/binding/lowering should be deterministic functions of
  authoritative inputs wherever their native contracts allow;
- derived results need not be persisted merely to make replay possible.

## 257.1 Persistence gate

Persist a derived object only if:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{StableIdentityConsequential}
\lor
\mathsf{RecomputationInsufficient}
\lor
\mathsf{IndependentAuthorityBoundary}.
}
\]

Otherwise derive it.

This is the strongest current anti-overengineering storage rule.

---

# 258. Clockwork machine contraction

The preceding domains contract into one engineering architecture:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ClockworkMachine}
=
\mathsf{AuthoritativeSpine}
+
\mathsf{LocalDeterministicTransitions}
+
\mathsf{ExplicitExternalBoundaries}.
}
\]

This is a derived engineering presentation, not a semantic primitive.

## 258.1 Authoritative spine

The authoritative spine contains only items whose identity/occurrence/authority
must survive independently:

- canonical semantic artifacts where later relations refer to their identity;
- actual external events/raw returns;
- standing-authority inputs whose identity is consequential;
- explicitly persisted recovery/provenance required for reopening.

## 258.2 Local deterministic transitions

Everything else should, by default, be:

\[
\boxed{
f:\text{explicit inputs}\to\text{typed result/residual}.
}
\]

Examples:
- decode;
- finite supported-answer admission;
- determination relevance resolution;
- finite answer binding;
- runtime verification;
- lowering checks;
- standing fixed-point computation;
- fold validation.

## 258.3 Explicit external boundaries

External actuality remains visibly distinct:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Suspend}
\to
\mathsf{Actualize}
\to
\mathsf{RawReturn/Event}
\to
\mathsf{ResumeDerivedReasoning}.
}
\]

No derived stage may silently redispatch or fabricate actuality.

---

# 259. Apply clockwork law to the current repository frontier

Current repository head during this pass:

`38cfd2ca06b7984c8e3eb64ea77942000a58c3fc`
— `feat: bind finite inquiry answers`.

The accepted current structure is already clockwork-compatible.

`BoundFiniteAskContinuation` is derived data. It:
- rechecks the source `IProg`;
- requires an exact Ask/question match;
- preserves the explicit environment;
- preserves the answer-slot name;
- preserves the entire admitted finite answer set;
- preserves the named continuation identity;
- does not substitute, evaluate, execute, or persist a new canonical program.

The runtime already has only:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Return}
\mid
\mathsf{Branch}
\mid
\mathsf{Probe}
}
\]

with `Probe` carrying one fixed resume target.

Therefore the next implementation must stay narrow.

## 259.1 Exact missing gear

Required relation:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SourceContinuationResumeCorrespondence}
(
\mathsf{BoundFiniteAskContinuation},
\mathsf{ProgramIR},
\mathsf{ProbeSuspension}
).
}
\]

It must establish:

1. the bound source continuation is exactly the source continuation compiled to
   the runtime resume block;
2. the suspension's probe/operator is the one connected to the admitted event;
3. the raw-return/event provenance matches the admitted answer;
4. the complete finite answer set is carried into the resumed continuation
   environment without singleton selection;
5. another source continuation, operator, resume target, event, or raw return
   rejects;
6. the result remains derived/replayable.

## 259.2 What not to build

Do **not** add:

- continuation registry;
- environment service;
- evaluator framework;
- generic scheduler;
- runtime plugin layer;
- orchestration crate;
- persisted bound-program artifact;
- second continuation representation.

until a concrete breaker requires one.

## 259.3 Likely implementation placement

Because `ic-runtime` already depends on `ic-core`, and the missing relation joins
core-derived `BoundFiniteAskContinuation` to runtime `ProbeSuspension/BlockTarget`,
the smallest placement is a narrow pure lowering/check function in `ic-runtime`
(or a similarly narrow existing lowering module if one already exists).

Do not invert the dependency so `ic-core` knows runtime blocks.

Do not add `ic-machine` for this seam.

## 259.4 First fixture

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{BOUND\_FINITE\_ANSWER\_LOWERS\_ONLY\_TO\_ITS\_NAMED\_RESUME}
}
\]

Positive case:
- one checked Ask;
- one admitted finite answer set;
- one bound continuation;
- one runtime Probe;
- one known source-continuation-to-block correspondence;
- exact operator/event/raw-return provenance;
- exact fixed resume target.

Expected:
one derived resumed binding/state.

Breakers:
- wrong continuation;
- wrong target;
- wrong operator;
- wrong raw return/event;
- wrong question;
- answer slot capture;
- selected singleton from multi-answer set.

Every breaker rejects locally.

The following research names do not deserve implementation identity now:

environment be extended only by one checked answer binding?}
}
\]

Do not answer by building the general language first.

Construct the maximal breaker and subtract.

## Status

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LOCAL\ RESEARCH\ FIXED\ POINT;\ CURRENT\ EXECUTABLE\ GEAR\ OPEN}.
}
\]

# 261. Execute next question — no general substitution language yet

Question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Does carrying the finite supported answer into the resumed continuation
force a general substitution/environment language?}
}
\]

Current source representation answers negatively.

`ProgramBinding` is canonically:

\[
\mathsf{TypeSymbol}\mapsto\mathsf{TypedFormRef}.
\]

`AdmittedFiniteAnswerSet` is intentionally derived evidence rather than a canonical
typed form/artifact.

Therefore forcing it into `ProgramBinding` would require one of:
- premature canonicalization of answer sets;
- a broad sum-typed environment value language;
- source schema revision before runtime use demonstrates the need.

None is currently forced.

## 261.1 Smallest runtime-side binding

Retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RuntimeAnswerBinding}
=
\langle
\mathsf{answer\_slot},
\mathsf{AdmittedFiniteAnswerSet}
\rangle
}
\]

only as derived runtime data.

The resumed state may be represented by the minimum product:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Resumption}
\times
\mathsf{BoundFiniteAskContinuation}
}
\]

or an equally small private derived wrapper if Rust ergonomics require one.

Do not give it canonical artifact identity.

## 261.2 Lowering relation

Inputs:

\[
\langle
B,
P,
S,
L
\rangle
\]

where:
- \(B\): `BoundFiniteAskContinuation`;
- \(P\): verified `ProgramIR`;
- \(S\): exact `ProbeSuspension`;
- \(L\): explicit source-continuation-to-runtime-target lowering correspondence.

Check:

\[
\boxed{
B.\mathsf{continuation}
\xleftrightarrow{L}
S.\mathsf{resume\_target}.
}
\]

Also require exact operator/event/raw-return relation already retained by
\(B.\mathsf{answer}\).

Output:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{DerivedBoundResumption}
}
\]

containing only:
- resumed runtime position;
- raw-return provenance;
- source continuation identity;
- original explicit source environment;
- answer-slot symbol;
- entire finite answer set.

No substitution occurs yet.

## 261.3 Breaker that would force generalization

Only generalize if a concrete continuation operation requires something such as:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RuntimeValue}
=
\mathsf{TypedForm}
\mid
\mathsf{FiniteAnswer}
\mid
\mathsf{Other}
}
\]

across two or more working code paths with shared semantics.

Until then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{SpecificBoundAnswerCarrier}
\prec
\mathsf{GeneralRuntimeValueLanguage}.
}
\]

## 261.4 Status

The next executable obligation remains local and finite.

No source schema change.
No evaluator.
No substitution calculus.
No registry.
No new crate.

This is the clockwork ratchet.

# 262. Four-surface convergence — canonical calculus, research network, plan, repository

This pass recursively superimposes:

1. the current Revised Again canonical calculus;
2. the regenerative network/ledger/machine documents;
3. the current implementation plan/frontier;
4. the actual repository head, code, migrations, and passing fixtures.

Authority remains ordered:

\[
\boxed{
\text{canonical semantics}
>
\text{implementation plan/frontier for build obligations}
>
\text{code/tests for implementation actuality}
>
\text{research documents for derived guidance}.
}
\]

The four surfaces are strongly aligned.

## 262.1 Canonical semantic spine

The canonical semantics require:

\[
\mathsf{IProg}
=
\mathsf{Return}_I
\mid
\mathsf{Ask}(q,\kappa),
\]

lowered to:

\[
\mathsf{Prog}
=
\mathsf{Return}
\mid
\mathsf{Branch}
\mid
\mathsf{Probe}.
\]

For a probe:
- applicability/authority is checked;
- the operation is actualized;
- raw return and successor coupled state are received;
- the raw occurrence enters ordinary authoritative history before decoding;
- continuation is answer-dependent.

The canonical record of realized actuality is `ActualEvent`.

No canonical `Attempt` artifact is presently forced.

Therefore preserve:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProposedRequest}
\neq
\mathsf{Attempt}
\neq
\mathsf{RawReturn}
\neq
\mathsf{ActualEvent}
\neq
\mathsf{DecodedAnswer}
\neq
\mathsf{Warrant}.
}
\]

## 262.2 Plan

Current Phase 6 requires exactly:

```text
SQLite journal
content-addressed store
ActualEvent
request-before-dispatch
raw-return persistence
crash/restart
```

The recurrent controller likewise places:

```text
persist request/suspension
-> actualize Probe
-> preserve raw return
-> append ActualEvent
-> decode
```

No distributed workflow engine is required.

## 262.3 Repository actuality

Current inspected head:

```text
7eee9ee5438baf8628d93817a1bc57737508d0c1

```

`ic-runtime` still does not dispatch or persist actuality.

`ic-store` can atomically append one already-realized checked `ActualEvent`,
including immutable artifact insertion and event-ledger linkage, but it does not
dispatch.

The only event-ledger migration presently records:

```text
ledger_sequence
event_ref UNIQUE
ledger_parent
```

There is no durable request/attempt record yet.

## 262.4 Research-document fold

Sections 259 and 261 correctly predicted the continuation/resume seam, but they are

Move their "current gear" status into regenerative ancestry.

Their surviving engineering laws remain:

- no continuation registry;
- no scheduler;

- no source schema change.

Repository actuality has validated those choices.

---

# 263. New maximal breaker — one event is not one external effect

The Phase 6 frontier exposes a distributed-systems boundary even in a single-process
reference implementation, because an external backend/world is outside the SQLite
transaction.

Reject:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactlyOneCommittedActualEvent}
=
\mathsf{ExactlyOneExternalEffect}.
}
\]

Local persistence can make an `ActualEvent` append linear and idempotent.

It cannot generally atomically commit:
- the local SQLite transaction; and
- a remote model/tool/service/world mutation.

Therefore also retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{NoDurableReturnAfterPreparedRequest}
\neq
\mathsf{NoExternalEffect}.
}
\]

A process may crash after a remote effect occurred but before the response became
durable.

A generic retry can then duplicate the external effect.

## 263.1 Native distributed-systems return

Idempotent API design supplies the mature repair:
- persist/reuse a stable request token;
- the remote service recognizes repeated delivery as the same logical request;
- retries do not repeat the protected mutation.

Transactional-outbox/dual-write theory supplies the broader impossibility shape:
local commit and external action are distinct failure domains unless a stronger
transaction/reconciliation contract bridges them.

The object-free result is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{CrashSafeActualization}
=
\mathsf{DurableIntent}
+
\mathsf{BindingNativeRecoveryContract}
+
\mathsf{AtomicLocalCompletion}.
}
\]

No generic exactly-once external-effect theorem is available.

---

# 264. Do not invent an `Attempt` truth record

A tempting repair is:

```text
Prepared
-> AttemptStarted
-> Completed
```

with each transition persisted locally.

This does not solve the remote-effect ambiguity.

If `AttemptStarted` is written before sending:
- crash can occur before the remote request is actually sent.

If it is written after sending:
- crash can occur after the remote request is sent but before the marker is written.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{LocalAttemptMarker}
\neq
\mathsf{ProofOfRemoteEffectOccurrence}.
}
\]

Do not add a canonical `Attempt` artifact merely to create apparent certainty.

The canonical calculus is correct to keep `attempt` as a distinct role without
requiring a universal canonical attempt record.

A local operational journal may record what the process knows, but its states must
not claim more than their evidence supports.

---

# 265. Minimal Phase 6 crash matrix

Use the smallest useful states.

## 265.1 Before durable preparation

No prepared request exists.

The machine must not have crossed the external-effect boundary.

## 265.2 Prepared request exists, no committed event

State:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{PreparedButUnresolved}.}
\]

After restart, absent stronger backend evidence:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{UnknownActuality}.}
\]

Do not infer:
- no dispatch;
- failed dispatch;
- no side effect;
- null return.

Recovery depends on the binding/backend contract.

Possible lawful routes:
- idempotent retry with the same stable request token;
- status/reconciliation lookup;
- explicitly repeat-safe/read-only operation;
- independent observation of the effect;
- otherwise remain blocked/unknown.

## 265.3 External return received

Once the external actualization has yielded the raw return and successor coupled
configuration, perform one local completion transaction before any decode:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RawReturn}
+
\mathsf{ActualEvent}
+
\mathsf{EventLedgerAppend}
+
\mathsf{PreparedRequestCompletion}
}
\]

as one SQLite transaction where feasible.

This is smaller than a separate durable "return received" subsystem.

If the transaction commits:
- the raw return is immutable;
- exactly one checked event is in the ledger;
- the prepared request is linked to that event;
- subsequent decode/resume is pure replayable work.

If the process crashes before this local completion commits, the request remains
`PreparedButUnresolved`; the system does not fabricate an event.

## 265.4 After committed event

Never redispatch for replay.

Reconstruct:

\[
\mathsf{ActualEvent}
\to
\mathsf{Decode}
\to
\mathsf{SupportedAnswer}
\to
\mathsf{BoundContinuation}
\to
\mathsf{AdmittedResumption}.
\]

The repository already demonstrates this suffix from a preserved event.

---

# 266. Smallest durable pre-dispatch capsule

The next implementation should not freeze a universal semantic `BackendRequest`
or `Attempt` schema.

Use one small operational persistence record whose purpose is only crash-safe
choreography.

Working name:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{PreparedProbe}}
\]

with no constitutional status.

It must bind:
- a stable local request/recovery identity;
- the exact `ProbeOperatorRef`;
- the pre-dispatch authoritative state/ledger position needed for the event;
- enough already-existing source/compiler/runtime provenance to reconstruct the
  same verified suspension after restart;
- a backend/recovery token when the binding supplies one.

Do not duplicate fields already recoverable from `ProbeOperator`,
`ProbeContract`, source `IProg`, or compiler-version identity.

The exact field layout remains implementation-relative until the first crash
fixture proves what cannot be removed.

The completion side needs only:
- `event_ref`, once the local completion transaction commits.

Then:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PreparedProbeRecord}
\neq
\mathsf{ActualEvent}.
}
\]

The former is operational recovery state.
The latter is authoritative realized-event history.

---

# 267. Crate-pressure question is now discharged

Earlier network section 141 stated:

> create an application/orchestration layer only when the first true
> end-to-end actualization transaction requires runtime + store simultaneously.

That condition is now true.

Current crate graph:

```text
ic-core
  ↑      ↑
runtime  store

ic-cli
```

The Phase 6 transaction needs:
- `ic-runtime`: `ProbeSuspension` and admitted resume semantics;
- `ic-store`: durable prepared request and event transaction;
- `ic-core`: operator/event/raw-return identities;
- an external mock/backend invocation boundary.

Putting persistence inside runtime would collapse responsibilities.

Putting runtime control inside store would collapse responsibilities.

Putting semantic orchestration into `ic-cli` would make the interface own the
machine.

Therefore the smallest justified split is now:

```text
ic-machine
    -> ic-core
    -> ic-runtime
    -> ic-store
```

where `ic-machine` owns only transaction choreography and backend invocation.

It owns no new semantic truth.

This is not speculative layering.
It is the first demonstrated dependency pressure that the earlier crate rule was
waiting for.

Status:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{IMPLEMENTATION\_READY}.}
\]
