# 268. First Phase 6 clockwork fixture sequence

Implement in this order.

## P6-ACT-001 — prepared intent precedes external dispatch

Persist one `PreparedProbe` before the mock executor is invoked.

Breaker:
executor invocation with no durable prepared row.

Expected:
reject / impossible through the machine API.

## P6-ACT-002 — prepared intent is not actuality

Crash/reopen with prepared request but no committed event.

Expected:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{UnknownActuality}}
\]

unless a binding-native reconciliation route supplies stronger evidence.

No `ActualEvent` may be fabricated.

## P6-ACT-003 — ambiguous crash does not generic-retry a mutating effect

Mock a backend whose effect can occur before the response is persisted and which
supplies no idempotency/reconciliation contract.

Restart.

Expected:
remain `UnknownActuality` / blocked.
Do not redispatch automatically.

## P6-ACT-004 — idempotent recovery reuses the same request token

Use a mock idempotent backend.

Crash after prepared request.

Restart/retry with the same token.

Expected:
one protected external mutation, one eventual committed event.

The idempotency guarantee is binding/backend supplied, not inferred by the
calculus.

## P6-ACT-005 — raw return and event commit before decode

Successful mock actualization.

Commit raw return + checked `ActualEvent` + ledger link + prepared-request completion
in one local transaction.

Inject crash immediately after commit and before decode.

Expected:
restart reconstructs the raw return/event exactly and performs no redispatch.

## P6-ACT-006 — committed event cold-replays to the same admitted resumption

Reuse the already passing event-to-resumption path.

Expected:
fresh-process replay yields the same:
- event;
- raw return;
- finite decoded set;
- support routes;
- bound continuation;
- fixed runtime resume target.

## P6-ACT-007 — one prepared request cannot finalize to two event identities

After a request is linked to a committed `event_ref`, reject a different event
completion for the same prepared request.

This is local history integrity.
It is not a claim of globally exactly-once external effect.

# 269. Plan compression after convergence

The current plan should not be expanded.

Its Phase 6 line:

```text
request-before-dispatch
raw-return persistence
crash/restart
```

already regenerates the newly localized machinery.

The research result merely sharpens its implementation meaning:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf{request\text{-}before\text{-}dispatch}
&\Rightarrow
\mathsf{durable\ intent},\\
\mathsf{crash/restart}
&\Rightarrow
\mathsf{unknown\ actuality\ when\ remote\ effect\ cannot\ be\ reconciled},\\
\mathsf{raw\ return\ persistence}
&\Rightarrow
\mathsf{atomic\ local\ completion\ before\ decode}.
\end{aligned}
}
\]

No new canonical semantic object is needed.

No distributed transaction framework is needed.

No workflow engine is needed.

No retry framework is needed.

One thin composition crate and one small durable operational seam are now justified.

After the first Phase 6 fixture, ask:

the exact suspended inquiry can be reconstructed after restart without
duplicating canonical `ProbeOperator` / source-program information?}
}
\]

Solve by ablation over the crash fixture.

Do not design the final request schema in advance.

## Current status

\quad

# 270. Execute the residual — minimum `PreparedProbe` hypothesis

Question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{What must a durable pre-dispatch capsule retain that cannot already be
regenerated from canonical references?}
}
\]

The current repository gives a strong subtraction.

`ProbeOperatorRef` already determines/references:
- query;
- boundary;
- active view;
- backend;
- executable code;
- return type;
- decoder contract;
- probe contract;
- compiler version.

Do not copy them into the operational request record.

`ActualEvent` later requires:
- ledger parent;
- state before;
- question/boundary;
- operator;
- raw return;
- state after;
- grain;
- route;
- binding;
- backend version;
- provenance.

Question/boundary and much static contract context are reachable through the
operator/probe contract.

State-after, actual route, backend-version occurrence, provenance, and raw return
are properties of the realized actualization and need not be fabricated before
dispatch.

The event itself does not encode the source `IProg` / answer-dependent continuation
identity.

Therefore the smallest current candidate capsule is:

```text
PreparedProbe {
    recovery_token,
    operator_ref,
    source_iprog_ref,
    pre_event_position,
    suspension_reconstruction_hint,
}
```

where:
- `recovery_token` is operational identity/idempotency/reconciliation input, never
  semantic object identity;
- `operator_ref` fixes the exact compiled external operation;
- `source_iprog_ref` restores the exact `Ask` and named continuation;
- `pre_event_position` minimally denotes the state/event-history position from which
  the eventual `ActualEvent` must be checked;
- `suspension_reconstruction_hint` is retained only if deterministic recompilation
  from source + compiler version cannot yet regenerate the exact runtime resume
  target.

## 270.1 Strong subtraction rule

Prefer:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{suspension\_hint}
=
\varnothing
}
\]

if the current compiler/runtime can deterministically regenerate the verified
`ProbeSuspension` and `ContinuationLowering` from:
- source `IProg`;
- canonical operator/compiler version;
- reconstructed pre-state.

Only persist a runtime-local resume target if a crash fixture proves that the target
cannot otherwise be regenerated exactly.

Likewise attempt to derive `pre_event_position` from the current serial event head
and reconstructed state. Persist an explicit state/parent reference only if omission
creates a replay ambiguity or stale-parent defect.

## 270.2 Field ablation fixture

For each candidate field \(f\):

1. remove \(f\);
2. crash immediately after durable preparation;
3. reopen fresh process;
4. attempt to reconstruct the exact verified suspension and eventual event context;
5. compare against the pre-crash request under the protected replay horizon.

Keep \(f\) only if:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{Remove}(f)
\Rightarrow
\mathsf{ProtectedReplayDifference}
}
\]

or reconstruction becomes `Unknown`.

This makes the durable request schema an output of the calculus rather than a
preconceived architecture.

## 270.3 Current expected irreducible core

The strongest current hypothesis is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PreparedProbe}
\simeq
\mathsf{RecoveryToken}
+
\mathsf{ProbeOperatorRef}
+
\mathsf{SourceIProgRef}
+
\mathsf{MinimalPreEventAnchor}.
}
\]

The runtime resume target is tentatively derived, not persisted.

This is still a working implementation hypothesis until the crash fixtures execute.

Previous section 267 inferred:

Phase 6 runtime + store pressure
=> create ic-machine

The crash-safe invariant was localized entirely at the persistence boundary:

```text
prepare_external_effect(...)
complete_external_effect(...)
```

without making `ic-store` depend on `ic-runtime`.

Current workspace still contains only:

```text
ic-core
ic-runtime
ic-store
ic-cli
```

Therefore the stronger engineering law is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{cross-layer workflow}
\not\Rightarrow
\text{new component}.
}
\]

Create a component only when the protected invariant itself cannot be owned by an
existing boundary without responsibility collapse or duplicated authority.

`ic-machine` returns to:

\[
\boxed{\mathsf{DEFERRED\ CANDIDATE}.}
\]

Reopen only when a concrete reusable production path cannot be expressed cleanly as
composition of the existing crate APIs.

This is a successful self-correction of the research network.

# 273. New live hole — request/operator correspondence

Current `prepare_external_effect` checks:
- the request reference names an existing artifact;
- the operator is a valid `ProbeOperator`;
- the ledger parent is current;
- the dispatch token is consistent;
- one unresolved single-writer effect rule.

It does not establish:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RequestFor}(r,o).
}
\]

A stored unrelated artifact can occupy `request_ref` beside a valid operator.

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{StoredArtifact}(r)
+
\mathsf{ValidOperator}(o)
\not\Rightarrow
\mathsf{RequestFor}(r,o).
}
\]

This is the exact typed dispatch gap currently named by the repository frontier.

Ablation against the current `ProbeOperator` shows that most fields duplicate

# 275. First mock binding — BackendRequest can remain a role, not a new nominal artifact

The current canonical `ProbeOperator` already names an
`executable_code_ref`, whose role may be a prompt, tool call, method invocation,
or other executable representation.

For the first decisive mock backend, choose the identity transport binding:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{MockBackendRequest}(o)
=
\mathsf{ExecutableCode}(o).
}
\]

Operationally:

```text
request_ref == operator.executable_code_ref
```

after re-resolving and rechecking the operator.

Then the existing content-addressed executable artifact occupies the
`BackendRequest` role for this binding.

This satisfies the project ontology:

\[
\boxed{
\text{role distinction}
\neq
\text{requirement for a new nominal type}.
}
\]

A separate canonical `BackendRequest` artifact whose only content is
`operator_ref` plus the same executable-code reference would be a one-to-one
wrapper with no demonstrated protected gain.

Therefore do not add it yet.

Likewise do not add `SurfacePlan` merely because the implementation plan names the
future compiler phase.

Reopen a distinct request artifact only when a provider-specific envelope contains
consequential material not already represented by the operator/executable code.

Examples of legitimate future breakers:
- provider parameters change operational behavior;
- exact transport envelope must be replayed/audited separately from semantic code;
- one executable code artifact lawfully lowers to multiple protected-distinct backend requests;
- transport adapter/version changes independently of operator compiler identity.

# 276. Stronger dispatch breaker — pending recovery state is not dispatch authority

The current operational state is intentionally public/recoverable:

```text
ExternalEffectState::Pending { ... }
```

That is correct for recovery inspection.

But:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{PendingState}
\neq
\mathsf{FreshDispatchAuthority}.
}
\]

Crash breaker:

```text
prepare
-> crash before durable completion
-> reopen
-> exact prepare call again
```

The journal can return the same pending state idempotently.

If a future provider API treats any `Pending` value as authority to dispatch, an
ambiguous external effect could be repeated.

Therefore the first executable provider boundary should use a linear ephemeral
capability:

```rust
DispatchPermit
```

with:
- private construction;
- no `Clone`/`Copy`;
- returned only when a new preparation is durably inserted in the current call;
- consumed by the provider dispatch;
- never recreated automatically from an already-pending recovery row.

A suitable preparation result is conceptually:

```text
PrepareOutcome =
    Fresh(DispatchPermit)
  | Existing(ExternalEffectState)
```

where `Existing(Pending)` carries no dispatch permit.

This compiles the distributed-systems recovery law directly into the Rust type
boundary instead of relying on comments.

It remains operational mechanism, not semantic authority.

---

# 277. Native-domain return — CAS actions, linear capabilities, and serialization identity

Three mature structures jointly constrain the current gear.

## 277.1 Content-addressed execution

Remote-execution systems identify a reproducible action through a compact digest
whose referenced closure determines the operation.

Transported lesson:

\[
\boxed{
\text{request identity should point into existing content-addressed execution
ancestry rather than duplicate that ancestry}.
}
\]

This supports using `ProbeOperatorRef` as the compact provenance root.

## 277.2 Linear capability / typestate

A one-use capability can represent permission to cross an effect boundary without
turning recovery state into authority.

Transported lesson:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{RecoveryDescription}
\neq
\mathsf{EffectPermission}.
}
\]

Rust ownership can enforce the first mock occurrence without a capability
framework.

## 277.3 Wire identity is not semantic canonicality

Provider/protocol serializers may offer deterministic output without stable
cross-version canonical serialization.

Therefore retain:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ExactWireIdentity}
\neq
\mathsf{SemanticRequestEquivalence}.
}
\]

When a real provider later forces a separate backend-envelope artifact:
- define project-owned canonical artifact encoding for provenance identity;
- retain exact outbound bytes when protected;
- never infer semantic request equality solely from third-party serialized-byte
  equality or inequality.

No new semantic generator is introduced by any of these methods.

# 278. Smallest typed-dispatch fixture sequence

Implement before any provider framework.

## P10-REQ-001 — foreign stored artifact is not a request for an operator

Store unrelated artifact \(a\) and valid operator \(o\).

Attempt:

```text
prepare(token, request=a, operator=o, ...)
```

under the identity mock binding.

Expected:
reject because:

```text
a != o.executable_code_ref
```

This is the current decisive breaker.

## P10-REQ-002 — exact executable-code role prepares

Use:

```text
request_ref = o.executable_code_ref
```

Expected:
durable preparation succeeds.

No new request artifact is required.

## P10-REQ-003 — operator ancestry is transitive request provenance

Change any protected operator input:
- question;
- boundary;
- backend;
- executable code;
- compiler version.

Expected:
operator identity changes and the prior request/operator pair no longer validates.

Do not duplicate those fields in the journal.

## P10-REQ-004 — fresh preparation returns one linear dispatch permit

Expected:
new row -> one nonclone dispatch permit.

## P10-REQ-005 — recovered pending state returns no dispatch permit

Crash/reopen with unresolved preparation.

Expected:
recovered pending/unknown state but no automatic permission to dispatch.

## P10-REQ-006 — permit is consumed once by the mock provider

Provider receives the exact executable-code artifact identified by the permit and
returns opaque bytes.

The permit cannot be reused through ordinary Rust ownership.

## P10-REQ-007 — provider return remains raw

The mock provider's bytes become `RawReturn`.

No decode, answer selection, support, or warrant occurs at dispatch.

# 280. Execute the next question — what must the first mock provider return?

Question:

\[
\boxed{
\text{After consuming a fresh dispatch permit, what is the smallest provider
return that lets the existing Phase 6 machinery complete actuality without
smuggling interpretation into the provider?}
}
\]

Maximal candidate:

```text
ProviderResult {
    decoded_answer
    status
    claim
    state_after
    route
    backend_version
    provenance
    raw_bytes
}
```

Subtract everything already owned downstream.

The provider must not return:
- decoded answers;
- claims;
- support;
- standing;
- semantic status.

Those belong after raw actuality.

The minimum provider-level return is:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf{ProviderRawReturn}
=
\mathsf{ExactRawBytes}
+
\mathsf{OccurrenceMetadataRequiredToConstructActualEvent}.
}
\]

For the first deterministic mock, occurrence metadata may be fixed by the fixture /
binding and therefore need not become a general result type.

Thus the narrowest first implementation may simply be:

```rust
fn dispatch(permit: DispatchPermit, code: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>
```

or the equivalent fallible opaque-byte return.

The caller then constructs:
- `RawReturn`;
- `ActualEvent` from already bound event context plus the returned bytes;
- calls `complete_external_effect`.

Only if a breaker shows that backend version, route, post-state, or provenance
cannot be regenerated from the binding/fixture should those values be added to the
provider return.
